# Apotheosis feature matrix

This matrix merges the per-terminal and per-topic prior-art surveys (kitty, WezTerm, Ghostty, foot, Alacritty, Rio, iTerm2, xterm, Windows Terminal, VTE/Konsole, tmux/zellij, Warp/Wave, plus topic dossiers) into one row per feature across 20 categories. Each row names the mechanism Apotheosis adopts, its control surface, an adopt level (must, should, could, no), the surveyed sources it was seen in, and a rationale. Claims about third-party software are graded in Appendix A and published or documented performance figures are collected in Appendix B; items marked unverified were not confirmed against a primary source. Section content below is copied verbatim from the per-category files.

## vt-core

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VTCORE-01 | Separable terminal core crate | Grid, parser driver, selection, search, damage and PTY loop live in a crate with no windowing, Wayland or wgpu types; GUI, mux, control socket and tests consume it. | internal | must | alacritty | Headless tests, benchmarks, a sandboxed or remote core, and reuse by the control socket all depend on it. |
| VTCORE-02 | Packed 8-byte cell with style and extras side tables | Cell is a u64: codepoint (21 bits), wide state, flag bits, content tag, style id, extras id. Zero-width marks, grapheme tails, hyperlink and graphics refs live in an extras table; default cell needs no lookup and rows stay memcpy-able. Ghostty's 64-bit layout (24-bit content, style id, protected, hyperlink, semantic bits) and Rio's are near-identical; take whichever fits grapheme clusters. VTE's 20-byte and tmux's spill-record cells are budget references only. | internal | must | ghostty, rio, alacritty, vte-konsole, tmux-zellij | Three mature designs converge on 8 bytes; per-cell heap (alacritty Arc extra) or 20+ byte cells cost too much at 100k-line scrollback across many panes. Grapheme clusters force an extras concept, so alacritty's char-per-cell is not copied. |
| VTCORE-03 | Background-only cell tags | A content tag marks cells that are only a palette or RGB background; the colour sits in the content bits, so they never touch the style table. | internal | should | ghostty, rio | Large blank coloured regions (TUIs, selection, cleared lines) skip style interning; cheap once VTCORE-02 has a content tag. |
| VTCORE-04 | Ref-counted style interning | Per-grid (per-page) table of packed styles (fg, bg, underline colour, SGR flags); id 0 is default; open-addressed table over a flat item array so ids are item indices and refcount changes skip hashing; dead items reclaimed lazily; a full table grows capacity. | internal | must | ghostty, rio | O(1) style access and small cells; ghostty's Robin Hood table at 0.8125 load is the proven concrete choice. Per-page tables keep GC local to scrolled-off pages. |
| VTCORE-05 | Row hint flags and occupancy high-water mark | Each row carries one-sided flags (wrap, continuation, grapheme, styled, hyperlink, semantic prompt) that may false-positive, never false-negative, plus an upper bound of modified columns since reset. Erase, insert, delete, reset and is-empty take fast paths on unflagged rows. | internal | must | ghostty, alacritty, vte-konsole | Source comment cites about 4x on unstyled rows; flags double as reflow and hyperlink-GC inputs. One-sided rule keeps correctness trivial. |
| VTCORE-06 | Ring-buffer row storage | Rows sit in a ring with a rotating origin; full-buffer scroll is a modular add; recycled rows reset only the occupied span; a full-screen scroll records damage once; history shrink adjusts length without freeing. | KDL scrollback.lines | must | alacritty, rio | O(1) scroll, proven at 100k lines. Ghostty uses linked pages instead; ring is simpler and we still page style tables per chunk if needed (see Open questions). |
| VTCORE-07 | Soft-wrap flag and paragraph reflow | Per-row hard/soft wrap flag; a paragraph is soft-wrapped rows plus the final hard row. Resize re-flows paragraphs while tracking cursor and selection, and carries per-row metadata (semantic marks, block ids) onto every chunk. Alternate screen is not reflowed. | KDL scrollback.reflow | must | ghostty, alacritty, vte-konsole, rio | Universal; paragraph model from VTE's rewrap design gives documented edge cases. Flag lives on the row (ghostty/VTE), not on the last cell (alacritty), so metadata carries cleanly and search across wraps needs no line table. |
| VTCORE-08 | Wide-char spacers and seam repair | Wide chars use a spacer cell; a leading-spacer state marks a wide char split by soft wrap; erase and insert name the seams they cut and repair them in O(1), preserving the wrap flag. | internal | should | alacritty, rio, ghostty | Prevents half-glyph corruption and false hard breaks on reflow. |
| VTCORE-09 | Reflow options: truncate and no-history-pull | Reflow can be disabled per profile (truncate); growing rows may be forbidden from pulling scrollback into the active area. | KDL scrollback.reflow, per-pane override | could | ghostty, vte-konsole | Needed for children owning their screen (ConPTY-like, nested remote); config toggle only. |
| VTCORE-10 | Model/diff separation (change log) | Terminal state is cells plus change records; deltas between snapshots feed the renderer and mux sync. Informs damage design only; no public crate. | internal | could | wezterm | Useful for pane mirroring and remote views; damage tracking (VTCORE-06) covers the renderer. |
| VTCORE-11 | Table-driven Williams parser with bounded buffers | Const-generated [state][byte] transition table (ground, escape, CSI, DCS, OSC, APC, PM, SOS) with explicit UTF-8 handling; fixed parameter array, per-sequence payload cap, over-cap sequences dropped and parser returns to ground; unknown sequences swallowed correctly. | internal | must | ghostty, xterm, topic-escape-registry, vte-konsole, foot | Foot's table-free state machine and tables reach the same result; table plus bounded buffers meets the no-allocation hot-path rule and blocks memory-exhaustion attacks. |
| VTCORE-12 | ASCII run fast path | Scan printable ASCII runs (memchr/SIMD) and write them in bulk when no charset, insert mode or OSC 8 is active; otherwise use the generic printer. | internal | should | foot | Foot's fast-printer swap is its measured vtebench lead; run scanning should exceed it. Optimisation, so not must. |
| VTCORE-13 | UTF-8 only, ignore raw C1 | Bytes 0x80-0x9F are never controls; only 7-bit ESC Fe forms parse. S7C1T/S8C1T and DECSCL 8-bit modes are accepted but ignored; replies are always 7-bit. | internal | must | topic-escape-registry, xterm | Prevents C1 injection and continuation-byte mis-decoding. Deliberate divergence from xterm's C1 reply modes. |
| VTCORE-14 | Full C0 and bare-ESC set | SO/SI, ENQ (empty), DECSC/DECRC, RIS, IND/NEL/RI, HTS, DECALN. RIS also resets extension state (keyboard stacks, graphics, hyperlink ids). | RIS/DECSTR | must | topic-escape-registry, xterm | Baseline for vttest and curses. |
| VTCORE-15 | Cursor, erase and scroll CSI set | CUx, ED/EL (ED 3 clears saved lines), ECH, REP, ICH/DCH/IL/DL, DECSTBM, DECSCUSR; scrollback receives only full-width top-anchored scrolls. | internal | must | topic-escape-registry, xterm | Every full-screen TUI depends on them. |
| VTCORE-16 | SGR with colon and semicolon forms | SGR 0-9, 21-29, 53/55, 30-107; 38/48/58 as ;5;n, ;2;r;g;b, :5:n, :2::r:g:b (colour-space id ignored); 4:x underline styles; per-parameter colon bitmask; unknown sub-parameter forms consumed and ignored, never reinterpreted. Advertise COLORTERM=truecolor. | KDL env | must | xterm, ghostty, topic-escape-registry | Both separators are in the wild; bitmask avoids the stray-colour bug. |
| VTCORE-17 | Synchronized output as presentation hold | Bytes always parse into the grid; mode 2026 only makes the renderer keep drawing the last frame while glass UI keeps animating. Ends on end marker, a timeout (about 1 s, configurable) from begin, full reset or real resize; legacy DCS = 1 s / 2 s also honoured; redraw wakeups suppressed meanwhile. | DECRQM 2026; KDL sync-timeout | must | foot, alacritty, rio, ghostty | Rio's hold needs no byte buffering or size cap (alacritty buffers); foot's refresh-skip matches. Timeout stops a hung frozen screen. |
| VTCORE-18 | Visibility and in-band theme reports | xdg_toplevel suspended drives CSI ? 2048-style and CSI ? 999 style reports; modes 2031, 2033, 996, 998 answered truthfully via DECRQM; theme report from our palette. | DECRQM; KDL | should | foot, topic-escape-registry | Lets apps pause when hidden and follow theme; cheap with an own Wayland client. Exact mode numbers and report syntax need confirming. |
| VTCORE-19 | Complete DECRQM/DECRPM | CSI [?] Ps $ p answers CSI [?] Ps ; Pm $ y with 0-4 for every ANSI and private mode, including read-only ones; backed by one mode bitset. | CSI ? Ps $ p | must | xterm, topic-escape-registry, vte-konsole | Primary capability discovery path for 2026, 2027, 2031, 2048; truthful answers required. |
| VTCORE-20 | Generated mode table | A single source file lists every mode, default, writability and owner; code, DECRQM answers and docs are generated from it; test checks DECRQM against it. | docs generated | should | vte-konsole | Keeps behaviour, queries and docs consistent. |
| VTCORE-21 | XTSAVE / XTRESTORE | CSI ? Pm s and CSI ? Pm r save and restore listed private modes independently in a one-level cache (stack per mode if cheap). | CSI ? Pm s / r | should | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Small; apps restore state they found. |
| VTCORE-22 | Palette, SGR and title stacks | XTPUSHCOLORS/XTPOPCOLORS/XTREPORTCOLORS (CSI # P/Q/R), XTPUSHSGR/XTPOPSGR (CSI # { and CSI # }), title push/pop via XTWINOPS 22/23 with index 1-10. | CSI # P/Q/R, # {, # }, CSI 22/23 t | should | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Safe appearance change and restore; supports our theme switching without losing app colours. |
| VTCORE-23 | Identity replies | DA1 (4 sixel, 22 colour, 28 rectangular edit as implemented), DA2, DA3, XTVERSION (DCS > \| Apotheosis version ST), DECRQSS (SGR, DECSTBM, DECSCUSR). | CSI c, CSI > c, CSI > q, DCS $ q | must | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Toolkit autodetection at startup; advertise only what is implemented. |
| VTCORE-24 | DSR / CPR / DECXCPR | Cursor position and status reports. | CSI 5 n, 6 n, ? 6 n | must | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Needed by readline, fish and text-size probes. |
| VTCORE-25 | XTGETTCAP with embedded table | DCS + q hex-name ST returns DCS 1 + r hex(name)=hex(value) ST from a compiled-in table (TN, Co, RGB, Smulx, Setulc, Su, Ms, fullkbd...). | DCS + q | must | topic-escape-registry | Terminfo-free discovery; fixes ssh to hosts without our terminfo entry. |
| VTCORE-26 | Reply with the query's terminator | OSC replies use BEL if the query did, ST otherwise. | internal | must | xterm | Scripts and TUIs parse replies by terminator. |
| VTCORE-27 | Colour query and set | OSC 4, 10-19, 104, 110-119 set by name or rgb spec; ? answers truthfully with a settable sequence; resets supported; setting gated per profile. | OSC 4/10-19/104/110-119; KDL allow-color-ops | must | xterm | TUIs query background to pick themes; themed profiles must interoperate. |
| VTCORE-28 | Titles with sanitising and stack | OSC 0/1/2 strip C0/C1 and bidi controls, cap length, push to the stack of VTCORE-22. | OSC 0/1/2 | must | topic-escape-registry, xterm | Titles feed the glass tab strip and palette. |
| VTCORE-29 | OSC 8 hyperlink table with GC | Per-cell link index (20 bits) into a table of id;uri; bitmap GC marks indices used by visible, scrolled and hovered cells and purges the rest; limits URI 2083 bytes, id 250, auto ids prefixed ':'. | OSC 8 ; params ; URI ST; KDL links { enabled; max-uri } | must | vte-konsole, ghostty, alacritty, rio | Bounded memory with abuse limits; follows the egmontkob spec. |
| VTCORE-30 | Modern extension set (absent from xterm) | Underline colour/styles, OSC 8, OSC 7, OSC 133 prompt marks, OSC 9/777 notifications, mode 2026, mode 2027 grapheme clusters; specifications taken from kitty, WezTerm, foot and Ghostty dossiers; always advertised via DECRQM. | DECRQM | must | xterm | Xterm patch #411 documents none of these; each needs its own matrix row elsewhere, this row only commits to advertising them. |
| VTCORE-31 | Left/right margins and column ops | Mode 69 DECLRMM, DECSLRM, DECIC, DECDC; scrolling and insert/delete honour margins; DECSLRM inactive unless 69 set. | DECRQM 69 | should | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Used by modern TUIs and multiplexers. |
| VTCORE-32 | Rectangular area operations | DECCRA, DECFRA, DECERA, DECSERA, DECCARA, DECRARA, DECSACE; DECRQCRA checksum off by default (fingerprinting and screen-read vector), XTCHECKSUM variants. | KDL allow-checksum | could | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Cheap on a cell grid, heavy esctest2 coverage, little real use. Implement after the core. |
| VTCORE-33 | Selective erase and protection | DECSCA protected flag, DECSED/DECSEL honour it. | CSI " q, CSI ? J/K | could | xterm | Per-cell flag already in VTCORE-02; some legacy apps. |
| VTCORE-34 | Double-width/height lines | DECDWL, DECDHL, DECSWL as per-line attribute with scaled glyphs. | ESC # 3/4/5/6 | could | xterm | vttest coverage; modest wgpu cost; showpiece. |
| VTCORE-35 | Conformance level emulation | Advertise one level (VT525-ish plus xterm extensions); DECSCL accepted, no behaviour change. | CSI Ps ; Ps " p | could | xterm | Compatibility only. |
| VTCORE-36 | Legacy key encoding | SS3 vs CSI by DECCKM/DECKPAM; modifiers as CSI 1;mod final; DECBKM; Shift+Tab as CSI Z. Enhanced keyboard protocols are a separate matrix. | DECCKM/DECKPAM/DECBKM; KDL keyboard.backspace | must | topic-keyboard, xterm | Baseline for every terminfo app. |
| VTCORE-37 | Sixel scrolling and register modes | Modes 80 (DECSDM), 1070 (private colour registers), 8452 (cursor right of image). | DECRQM 80/1070/8452 | should | topic-graphics, foot | Correct scrolling semantics for sixel; cheap. |
| VTCORE-38 | Exact pixel-size reporting | XTWINOPS 14t/16t/18t and TIOCGWINSZ pixel fields correct under fractional scaling, padding and splits; sixel heights as multiples of 6 documented. | CSI 14/16/18 t; TIOCGWINSZ | must | topic-media | Wrong or zero sizes break aspect ratio in every image and mpv path. |
| VTCORE-39 | Feature reporting query | iTerm-style capability query. Syntax unverified. DECRQM, XTGETTCAP and DA1 already cover detection. | unverified | could | iterm2 | Only one source and syntax not confirmed. |
| VTCORE-40 | iTerm OSC 1337 compat commands | SetColors, ClearScrollback, StealFocus, RequestAttention, CopyToClipboard; focus and clipboard behind consent prompt. | OSC 1337;... | could | iterm2 | Compat; policy gating mandatory. |
| VTCORE-41 | Nested passthrough policy | Per-pane passthrough for DCS tmux; wrapped sequences when running inside another mux or terminal. | KDL pane passthrough | could | tmux-zellij | Only needed in the nested case. |
| VTCORE-42 | esctest2 conformance in CI | External CI job runs esctest2 against a headless build with a test-only screen-dump hook; known-bug list per terminal. Never vendored or linked (GPL-2.0). | CI | must | xterm | 559 tests give a regression net for all of the above. |
| VTCORE-43 | vttest manual checklist | Release checklist and screenshot-diff corpus from vttest menus. | dev tooling | should | xterm | Human-eyes check for the renderer; licence suits dev tooling (verify before shipping). |
| VTCORE-44 | VT52 mode | Not implemented; CSI ? 2 l ignored. | | no | xterm | Obsolete, separate parser table, no users. |

### Open questions

- Ring buffer (alacritty, rio) versus paged list (ghostty) for scrollback: ring is O(1) scroll but per-page style tables and compressed or shared scrollback for many panes need a decision (VTCORE-04, -06).
- Cell layout: bit budget for graphemes (extras id width) and graphics refs; is 16-bit style id per grid enough or must it be per page?
- Exact numbers and report syntax for theme/visibility modes (2031, 2033, 996, 998, 999) and 2048 were not verified (VTCORE-18).
- iTerm feature-reporting syntax unverified (VTCORE-39).
- Sync-output timeout default and whether to cap total hold time.
- Which DA1 feature codes to claim honestly for the implemented subset.
- vttest licence terms to confirm before bundling anything.


## unicode

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNICODE-01 | UTF-8 decoding with maximal-subpart U+FFFD replacement | One decoder semantics (W3C / Unicode Table 3-7 maximal subpart) shared by the scalar state machine and any SIMD/bulk fast path; fuzz the two for parity. | none (fixed behaviour) | must | ghostty | Deterministic invalid-input output, and fast and slow paths cannot drift. Source: ghostty src/terminal/UTF8Decoder.zig; src/simd/vt.cpp (MaximalSubpart). |
| UNICODE-02 | Unicode property lookup tables | Generate at build time, in pure Rust, a multi-stage table (256-codepoint pages, shared all-default page) yielding width 0/1/2, grapheme-break class and emoji flags in one lookup. Pair with a grapheme transition table (prev class, next class, state -> break, next state) generated by driving a conformance-tested reference segmenter, so rules never drift. Flat 128 KiB width table is an acceptable fast path for the BMP/SMP. | none (internal); unicode version fixed at build | must | ghostty, rio, kitty | Single index on the hot path. We pick staged tables plus generated transition table (ghostty/rio) over runtime wcwidth (foot/utf8proc dependency is C, disallowed). Sources: ghostty src/unicode/lut.zig, props_table.zig; rio-vt codepoint_width.rs, grapheme_lut.rs. |
| UNICODE-03 | Grapheme clustering (DEC mode 2027) | UAX #29 clustering with VS15/VS16 handling. Reported via DECRQM. Default on for clustering in the grid; mode 2027 set by an app forces the unicode width method. No cluster merge directly after a cursor move (avoids cursor desync with apps). Fast path batches only codepoints that are breaks versus the previous cell. | DECSET/DECRQM 2027; KDL `unicode { grapheme-clusters }` | must | ghostty, foot, rio, windows-terminal, wezterm, kitty, contour | Needed for ZWJ, flags, keycaps, modifiers, tags; shipped by most modern terminals. Foot's no-merge-after-move rule and ghostty's forced-unicode-on-2027 are adopted; rio's default-on is adopted because we pair it with UNICODE-04 escape hatch. Sources: foot terminal.c term_process_and_print_non_ascii; ghostty Terminal.zig print; rio config grapheme-clustering. |
| UNICODE-04 | Width policy / text measurement method | Selectable method: `unicode` (cluster width from table), `wcswidth` (legacy sum of per-codepoint widths, variation selectors do not attach to non-emoji bases), `double-width-cap`, `max-codepoint` (foot variants). Default unicode. | KDL `unicode { width-method }`; maps from ghostty grapheme-width-method, foot tweak.grapheme-width-method, windows-terminal compatibility.textMeasurement | must | ghostty, foot, windows-terminal, rio | Legacy shells and tmux compute wcwidth; users need an explicit compatibility choice. Keep the full set (superset of ghostty two-way and WT three-way). |
| UNICODE-05 | Unicode version and East Asian Ambiguous width | Setting to pick the width table version (default latest) and to treat Ambiguous as wide; per-profile. Default latest (not wezterm's 9) since clustering makes old tables wrong. Version selector adds table size; ambiguous switch is cheap, so ambiguous-width is the priority half. |
| UNICODE-06 | Emoji presentation selection (VS15/VS16, Emoji_Presentation) | VT core decides presentation and cell width (VS16 widens to 2, VS15 narrows); the font layer is told to request a colour face (COLR/CBDT/sbix/SVG as supported) or monochrome face; fallback cache key includes the emoji flag. | none (internal); KDL font `emoji-font` | must | kitty, wezterm, foot, ghostty | Correct emoji versus text rendering without guessing from font. Sources: kitty fonts.c (has_emoji_presentation, load_fallback_font); wezterm-font shaper/harfbuzz.rs (Presentation). |
| UNICODE-07 | Cluster cell storage | Cell holds a small tag; clusters (rare path) live out of line: either an offset-addressed chunk allocator inside the page/row (ghostty, 4-codepoint chunks, cap 64 codepoints per cluster) or an interned table keyed by sequence hash (foot, VTE vteunistr, konsole ExtendedCharTable). We adopt page-local chunk allocation: no per-cell heap allocation, copy of a page is a memcpy. Reuse the same allocator style for hyperlink id/uri strings (32-byte chunks). Any inter-process frame format (control socket snapshots) carries a cluster table with per-entry and per-record size caps (zellij). Wide tails use a spacer cell. | none (internal) | should | ghostty, foot, vte-konsole, tmux-zellij | Every mature grid solves this; choice matters for performance and snapshot safety. Page-local chosen over global interning because it needs no GC/refcount and bounds memory. Sources: ghostty page.zig, bitmap_allocator.zig; foot terminal.c; zellij structured_render.rs. |
| UNICODE-08 | OSC 66 text sizing protocol | Implement the full kitty protocol: metadata s,w,n,d,v,h; text occupies an s*w by s cell block; client states width so both sides agree; erase operations remove any intersecting multicell block; support detection by CPR probes; renderer draws glyphs at non-uniform scale and fractional placement. | ESC ] 66 ; metadata ; text ST; KDL `unicode { text-sizing }` toggle | must | kitty (spec); requested by topic-escape-registry, topic-fonts | Resolves width disagreement and gives headings and super/subscripts. Only kitty ships it today, yet the renderer needs multi-cell non-uniform glyphs anyway and the grid model must be decided early. Source: https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/text-sizing-protocol/ |
| UNICODE-09 | OSC 66 width-only fallback and limits | Honour `w` alone when a scale cannot be drawn (s=1 degrade); text payload at most 4096 bytes; blocks larger than the screen discarded. Advertise level via the detection probes. | OSC 66 w= | should | kitty | Defines safe degradation and bounds for hostile input. Same source as UNICODE-08. |
| UNICODE-10 | DECRQM width-resource modes (1020-1023) | Answer DECRQM for xterm utf8/cjkWidth/emojiWidth/privateWidth as read-only reflections of our width policy (UNICODE-04/05). | CSI ? 1020-1023 $ p | could | xterm | One terminal ships it, few apps query it. Cheap to add once policy exists; 2027 DECRQM is already covered by UNICODE-03. Source: ctlseqs.html. |
| UNICODE-11 | Character sets: DEC Special Graphics and SCS shifting | Implement G0-G3 designation (ESC ( ) * +), SO/SI, SS2/SS3, with DEC Special Graphics and ASCII/UK; line drawing maps to box-drawing codepoints. | ESC ( 0 etc. | must | xterm, all VT terminals | Mandatory for curses and legacy TUIs. Source: ctlseqs.html. |

Source: windows-terminal DispatchTypes.hpp. |
| UNICODE-14 | Bidi implicit and explicit modes | Follow the terminal-wg bidi spec: BDSM (CSI 8 h/l) selects implicit (terminal runs UAX #9 per paragraph; default) or explicit (app lays out visually). Paragraphs span soft-wrapped rows; all-LTR rows use a trivial identity map. SCP (CSI 1/2 SP k) sets paragraph direction. Pure-Rust bidi (unicode-bidi crate, MIT/Apache). | CSI 8 h/l; CSI Ps SP k; KDL `unicode { bidi { enabled; default-direction } }` | should | vte-konsole (VTE reference; Konsole per-line only) | Only real bidi design for terminals; adopt VTE's paragraph-spanning approach rather than Konsole's per-line. Sources: https://terminal-wg.pages.freedesktop.org/bidi/ ; vte src/bidi.cc (BidiRunner::paragraph). |
| UNICODE-15 | Bidi per-row mapping structure | Per visual row keep logical-to-visual and visual-to-logical maps, a per-cell RTL flag (mirroring, selection), paragraph direction and foreign-direction flag; paragraph capped (VTE: 500 rows); free maps when row unmapped. Selection and cursor motion go through the maps. | internal | should | vte-konsole | Proven storage that separates algorithm from data; required for correct selection once UNICODE-14 exists. Sources: vte bidi.cc, bidi.hh, ringview.cc. |
| UNICODE-16 | Bidi box-drawing mirroring and paragraph autodetect | DECSET 2500 mirror box-drawing in RTL context; DECSET 2501 autodetect paragraph direction (default off). Numbers are draft: keep them in a remap table. | CSI ? 2500/2501 h/l | could | vte-konsole | Only needed for TUIs in RTL; numbers may change. Sources: vte modes.py; terminal-wg escape-sequences recommendation. |
| UNICODE-17 | Sextants and octants (Legacy Computing) | Draw U+1FB00-1FB3B (2x3) and U+1CD00-1CDE5 (2x4) as built-in sprites from a generated codepoint-to-bitmask table, plus smooth mosaics and ellipse quarters as paths, independent of the font. | KDL `font { builtin-box-drawing }` | should | ghostty, kitty, wezterm | Modern TUIs and charts use them; few fonts carry them; we already render sprites. Source: ghostty src/font/sprite/draw/symbols_for_legacy_computing*.zig. |
| UNICODE-18 | Unicode-aware search matching | Compare cell clusters (including combining sequences), treat empty cells as spaces, Unicode simple case folding (not ASCII-only) with optional normalization-insensitive mode. | search UI toggles; Action `search` | should | foot (cluster-aware), ghostty (ASCII-only, a gap) | Correct search of non-ASCII text; take foot's cluster comparison, improve on ghostty's ASCII folding. Source: foot search.c matches_cell; ghostty search/sliding_window.zig. |
| UNICODE-19 | OSC 66 text-sizing payload cap of 4096 bytes | Parse metadata then bounded text; reject payload over 4096. | escape: OSC 66 | must | kitty, ghostty | Spec limit; ghostty enforces it. Source: kitty docs/text-sizing-protocol.rst; ghostty osc/parsers/kitty_text_sizing.zig#max_payload_length. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| UNICODE-20 | Grapheme clustering mode 2027 above the parser | Parser emits print_str runs; unicode layer segments with UAX #29 and measures width. | DECRQM 2027 | must | ghostty, rio | vte's per-char print forces re-segmentation; ghostty defaults 2027 on. Source: ghostty src/terminal/modes.zig; rio rio-unicode. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |

### Open questions

- Which unicode version to ship as "latest" and how to track new versions (build-time data generation pinned to a crate version vs vendored UCD).
- Whether UNICODE-05 version selector earns its table-overlay cost, or only the ambiguous-width switch ships.
- Final mode numbers for bidi (2500/2501) and OSC 66 detection behaviour are draft or unverified against current upstream.
- Whether non-kitty terminals' absence of OSC 66 means app adoption is too low to justify must; kept must for renderer-design reasons.
- Glyph-scale handling of OSC 66 blocks interacting with ligatures, selection and reflow is not covered by the rows.
- Not present in the rows: Hangul jamo composition, regional-indicator edge cases, normalization on paste, confusable/security display of wide characters.

| INPUT-01 | Kitty keyboard protocol, all five flags (disambiguate 1, event types 2, alternate keys 4, all keys as escapes 8, associated text 16) | Full CSI u encoder: `key:shifted:base ; mods:event ; text u`. Flags live in a push/pop stack of 8 per screen (main and alt swapped on screen switch; pop on empty resets to 0; push on full evicts the oldest). Set (`CSI = f ; m u`, modes 1/2/3), push (`CSI > f u`), pop (`CSI < n u`), query (`CSI ? u`). On by default, no "compat fear" toggle. | `CSI = f;m u`, `CSI > f u`, `CSI < n u`, `CSI ? u`; KDL `keyboard.kitty-protocol` (default on) | must | kitty, wezterm (off by default), ghostty, foot, alacritty, rio, windows-terminal, konsole, tmux/zellij | Table stakes for modern TUIs; every surveyed mature terminal ships it. Per-screen stack is the correct semantics (alacritty, kitty, foot agree). WezTerm's opt-in default is the outlier; we default on because correct stack handling removes the risk that motivated it. ## Open questions

- kbvm vs libxkbcommon as the default (INPUT-07): kbvm maturity and Compose/layout parity are still unverified; the differential corpus and oracle design are specified in gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks but not yet run.
- Should key sequences (INPUT-27) have a default per-key timeout, given ghostty has none and wezterm/kitty do? Proposed: on, 1000 ms, configurable.
- Is modifyOtherKeys (INPUT-11) kept at "should" or raised to "must" if tmux compatibility tests require it?
- Global shortcuts (INPUT-29): which Abyss IPC interface registers them, and portal fallback behaviour on other compositors, is undecided.
- Smart-selection rules (INPUT-38) in iterm2 were not verified from primary sources.
- Warp docs for syntax highlighting (INPUT-55) were navigation-only and not verified.
- Whether legacy encodings 1005/1015 (INPUT-20) are worth shipping at all.


## fonts

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FONTS-01 | Procedural box, block, braille, Powerline and legacy-computing glyphs | Cells in U+2500-259F, braille, Powerline (U+E0B0 range) and Symbols for Legacy Computing (sextants, octants, etc.) are drawn by our code, not taken from fonts. Each sprite is rasterized once on the CPU to an exact-cell coverage bitmap (anti-aliased, thickness derived from cell size/DPI) and uploaded to the atlas like any glyph. One range check rejects non-sprites. | KDL `font { builtin-glyphs { enabled; ranges; box-thickness; solid-shades } }`; GUI toggle per range group | must | kitty, ghostty, foot, alacritty, rio, windows-terminal, notcurses | Six terminals do it. Pixel-exact joins at any cell size and fractional scale, no fallback-font dependence, and blitter output (octants/sextants) looks right. CPU-raster-to-atlas (rio/ghostty) is chosen over WT's packed instruction table and over shader drawing: it is simplest, shares the atlas path, and needs no per-frame cost. Cursor and underline-style sprites use the same path. |
| FONTS-02 | Font stack: per-style families, ordered fallback, per-attribute rules | `font` node has family plus ordered `fallback` list; separate stacks for regular/bold/italic/bold-italic; rules may select a stack by attribute (bold/italic/intensity). User list searched first, then system fallback (FONTS-17). | KDL `font { family; fallback; rule { bold; italic } }`; actions to change font at runtime; GUI font page | must | wezterm, foot, ghostty, kitty | Every mature terminal exposes it; fontconfig's own monospace fallback is poor, so user order must win. |
| FONTS-03 | Codepoint-range font map and symbol map | Ranges (U+XXXX-YYYY) map to a named font, overriding the stack; checked before normal fallback. | KDL `font { symbol-map "U+E000-F8FF" font="..." }` | must | kitty, ghostty, rio | Core control for icon fonts; three terminals ship it. |
| FONTS-04 | Per-font OpenType feature strings | Parse `+ss01 -calt liga=0` style lists per family/style and pass to shaper as feature overrides; applied per stack entry. | KDL `font { features "+ss01" "-calt" }`; GUI chips | must | kitty, wezterm, ghostty, foot, rio, topic-fonts | Core control for coding fonts. |
| FONTS-05 | Variable font instances and variation axes | Per-style variation settings (e.g. wght, slnt) applied both to the shaper instance and to the skrifa outline location; named instances selectable. | KDL `font { variation { wght 450 } }` per style; GUI sliders from the font's axes | must | ghostty, kitty | Required for variable families and weight tuning. |
| FONTS-06 | Integer cell metrics with explicit rounding and adjustments | Cell width = rounded advance, height = rounded line height (round, not ceil); line gap split half above/half below; baseline derived from cell bottom; every metric minimum 1 px. User adjustments (cell width/height, baseline, underline/strike/overline/cursor position and thickness) applied after, in px or percent. | KDL `font { adjust { cell-width; cell-height; baseline; underline-position; underline-thickness; strikethrough-*; overline-*; cursor-* } }` | must | ghostty, kitty (modify_font), foot | DPI-stable layout, no zero-thickness lines; the control surface is expected by users. |
| FONTS-07 | Cell-width overrides for codepoint ranges | Table maps ranges to 1 or 2 cells (or forced narrow) for the wcwidth layer and the renderer, so PUA icons and ambiguous symbols agree between app and terminal. | KDL `font { cell-width "U+E000-F8FF" 1 }` | should | kitty (narrow_symbols), wezterm (cell_widths) | Two terminals; fixes Nerd Font width disputes. Must be consistent with the grid's own width table, so it lives in the shared width layer. |
| FONTS-08 | Nerd Font constraints, cap-height fallback scaling, optional embedded symbols font | Generated per-codepoint table (scale, alignment, constrain-to-cell) for PUA icons, applied when the glyph comes from a symbols/fallback font; fallback fonts optionally scaled to match primary cap height. Optionally ship a symbols-only Nerd Font so icons work unpatched. | KDL `font { icon-height; scale-fallback-to-cap-height; embedded-symbols }` | should | ghostty, rio, kitty, wezterm | Icons are not fixed width; avoids clipping/overlap and patched fonts. Table derived from the ghostty-style generated approach (regenerate, do not copy). Embedded font needs a licence check (open question). |
| FONTS-09 | Shaping engine: harfrust on skrifa | Shape with harfrust (MIT, pure Rust, ShapePlan cached per font/script/language/features); shapes in font units, we scale advances. Parser family is skrifa/read-fonts only (FONTS-24). | none (internal); `font { shaping { enabled; ligatures } }` | must | topic-fonts, wezterm (HarfBuzz), kitty, ghostty | No C allowed; harfrust is maintained and supersedes rustybuzz. |
| FONTS-10 | Run shaping in the grid with cell-aligned ligatures | Shape per line in style/font runs; map each shaped cluster back to grid cells; ligature glyphs and multi-cell clusters occupy their cells exactly. Per-grapheme-only rendering is not accepted for the grid; text-run shaping also used for glass UI text. | KDL `font { shaping { ligatures true } }`, action `toggle-ligatures` | must | wezterm, kitty, ghostty, windows-terminal | foot's grapheme-only grid is a known gap users complain about. |
| FONTS-11 | Shaped-run cache | Single-threaded cache keyed by text+style+font+features; lookups use a borrowed key (no allocation on hit). Layout: bucketed N-way associative table (rio: 256x8, move-to-end LRU inside bucket), values shared (Rc/Arc) shaped glyph arrays; bounded memory. | none; `font { shape-cache-size }` advanced | must | wezterm, rio | Allocation-free hits are the core of fast shaping and fit our single-thread render loop. Combine wezterm's borrowed key with rio's bounded bucket design. |
| FONTS-12 | Simple-text fast path | Classify runs that need no shaping (ASCII/simple scripts, no features that change them, no ligature font) and emit one glyph per cell using the cell advance; skipped when features are set. | none | must | windows-terminal | Large CPU saving on ASCII-heavy output. |
| FONTS-13 | Ligature policy: break under cursor | Split shaping runs at the cursor cell (and optionally selection) so ligatures un-ligate there for editing; policy configurable. | KDL `font { shaping { break-at cursor\|never } }`, `disable-ligatures never\|cursor\|always` | should | kitty, ghostty | Two terminals; small usability win. |
| FONTS-14 | Ligature overlap split for per-cell colour | Glyphs overhanging more than half a cell are sliced into cell-wide chunks so each cell takes its own foreground/background. | none | should | windows-terminal | Correct colours in coloured ligatures; also needed for wide glyph selection/cursor rendering. |
| FONTS-15 | Font discovery: fontdb scan plus fontconfig-parser | Pure-Rust directory scan with CSS-like family/weight/stretch/style query; honour fontconfig config via the pure-Rust parser only, no libfontconfig; extra font directories configurable. | KDL `font { directories; }` | must | topic-fonts, rio | Satisfies the no-C and allowed-library rules. fontdb lacks codepoint queries, hence FONTS-16. |
| FONTS-16 | Per-face charmap coverage index | Cache each face's charmap as sorted codepoint ranges (on disk, invalidated by mtime) so fallback lookup is a binary search without opening fonts. | `apotheosis-ctl font query U+XXXX` | must | topic-fonts (own design), rio (fontconfig charset sort equivalent) | Needed for lazy per-codepoint system fallback and for diagnostics. |
| FONTS-17 | Fallback resolution with grapheme-common font | For a cluster, collect supporting fonts per codepoint and choose one font covering all (ZWJ, keycap, VS sequences); order: user list, symbol map, then system coverage index; merge adjacent runs resolved to the same face. Missing glyph cached once. | via FONTS-02 | must | ghostty, windows-terminal, kitty | Prevents sequences splitting across fonts; merging avoids per-run overhead. |
| FONTS-18 | Colour emoji: COLRv0/v1, CBDT/sbix | Own COLRv1 painter on skrifa's ColorPainter (solid/linear/radial/sweep gradients, clips, composite modes) into RGBA, cached per (glyph,size) in a separate colour atlas; swash/skrifa for COLRv0, sbix and CBDT bitmaps. Emoji vs text presentation decided from VS15/VS16, default presentation and font coverage. | `font { emoji { presentation; font } }` | must | wezterm, rio, topic-fonts | Modern emoji (Noto COLRv1) require it; swash alone lacks COLRv1. |
| FONTS-19 | CPAL light/dark palette selection | Choose CPAL palette by background luminance when the font offers one. | `font { emoji { palette auto\|light\|dark } }` | could | topic-fonts | Cheap polish following theme. |
| FONTS-20 | Hinting and rasterization options | skrifa HintingInstance: none, light (vertical-only auto-hinter, default) and full (interpreter); grayscale AA, optional subpixel only when compositor/output layout is known; fractional glyph offsets. | `font { hinting none\|light\|full; antialias }` | must | topic-fonts, foot, kitty | Light hinting is the usual default; users expect the knob. |
| FONTS-21 | Synthetic bold and italic | When a style is missing, embolden by outline offset and skew for italic; thickness adjustable; toggle. | `font { synthetic-style bold italic; thicken }` | should | ghostty, rio, kitty | Fonts without styles are common. |
| FONTS-22 | Glyph atlas keyed by shaped glyph id | Key = (font id, glyph id, variation/style, size bucket quantized to 1/4 physical px); multi-page shelf-packed atlases (mono R8 and colour RGBA separate), bounded pages with eviction. Never key by char. | none | should | rio | Resize and fractional-scale reuse with bounded memory. Keying on glyph id is mandatory (see FONTS-26). |
| FONTS-23 | Preload ASCII per style | Rasterize printable ASCII for each style at load and on size change. | none | could | alacritty | Avoids first-frame hitches; cheap. |
| FONTS-24 | Single parser family; swash only for UI text and bitmaps | skrifa/read-fonts for outlines, metrics, hinting, colour; swash (also on skrifa) permitted for glass UI text masks and bitmap emoji; grid sprites and COLRv1 use our own path. | none | must | topic-fonts, rio | One safe (forbid unsafe) parser for shaping and raster, dual MIT/Apache. |
| FONTS-25 | Bidi and complex-script shaping with a real shaper | Shape Arabic and other joining scripts per paragraph run with harfrust, not presentation-form substitution; covers glass UI text and, where the grid supports bidi, grid rows. | `font { shaping { enabled } }` | should | vte-konsole (limitation noted) | Avoid VTE's known presentation-forms limitation. Grid bidi scope is outside this category. |
| FONTS-26 | Rejected: char-keyed glyph cache, FreeType/fontconfig FFI, rustybuzz/ttf-parser, cosmic-text for grid, OT-SVG, grapheme-only grid | Not adopted: char-keyed cache (blocks ligatures/clusters), crossfont/FreeType/fontconfig FFI (C), rustybuzz and ttf-parser (stale, second parser family), cosmic-text as grid engine (no exact cell placement), SVG-in-OpenType (large scope, low payoff), grapheme-only grid shaping. | n/a | no | alacritty, foot, topic-fonts | Each conflicts with a fixed decision or loses to a chosen alternative above. fontique (could, FONTS-27) is the one borderline case. |
| FONTS-27 | fontique for UI-text fallback | Possible source of generic-family and script/locale fallback for glass UI text only; must be configured without fontconfig dlopen. | none | could | topic-fonts | Unverified feature set; evaluate against FONTS-15/16 first. |
| FONTS-28 | Font diagnostics CLI and GUI chooser | CLI `ls-fonts` prints the resolved chain with per-glyph source, features, metrics, and which rule matched; GUI chooser previews families, styles, named instances and variation axes live and writes the same KDL. | `apotheosis-ctl font list\|query\|resolve`; Settings > Fonts | should | wezterm, kitty | Debuggability; chooser is the settings-GUI face of FONTS-02..05. |
| FONTS-29 | Glyph Protocol (APC 25a1) | Apps query coverage and register runtime glyphs as glyf/COLRv0/COLRv1 payloads (verbs s/q/r/c); registered glyphs go to a PUA-like sprite space in the atlas; unsupported terminals ignore the APC. | `font { glyph-protocol enabled }` | could | ghostty | Removes patched-font need; experimental, track upstream; payloads are untrusted input so parsing must be sandboxed/limited. |
| FONTS-30 | Font size and cell scale limits | Clamp font scale 0.25-4.0 and line/letter spacing scale 1.0-2.0; actions zoom-in/out/reset and wheel zoom. | `font { size; cell-scale }`, actions `font-zoom-in\|out\|reset` | could | vte-konsole, konsole | Sensible bounds; zoom actions are expected anyway. |

### Open questions

- Embedded symbols-only Nerd Font: licence and size acceptable (OFL fonts vs allowed list)? Needs a decision before FONTS-08 ships it.
- Whether fontique can be built without any fontconfig dlopen (FONTS-27); unverified.
- Subpixel (LCD) antialiasing: needs compositor output-layout info; Abyss integration only? Not settled by rows.
- Grid bidi scope (FONTS-25) belongs to the terminal-core category; confirm the boundary.
- Size/shape of the coverage-index on-disk cache and invalidation (FONTS-16).
- harfrust maturity versus HarfBuzz for rarer scripts (unverified).
- Whether octant/sextant sprite set follows Unicode 16 exactly, and if the legacy-computing range list is user-trimmable.


## rendering

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RENDERING-01 | Instanced quad text pipeline | One instanced quad per glyph (triangle strip from vertex id); instance carries atlas rect, bearings, grid pos, colour, atlas selector (gray vs colour) and flags (no-min-contrast, cursor glyph). Flush on atlas change. Measure cell storage buffer plus instancing vs packed vertex instances. | internal | must | kitty, ghostty, alacritty, rio, zellij (kitty/shaders.c; ghostty cell_text.v.glsl; alacritty text/glsl3.rs InstanceData) | Baseline of every GPU terminal; low CPU per frame, shared across grid and glass UI text. |
| RENDERING-02 | Paged glyph atlas | Glyph sprites rasterised once, packed with skyline (preferred over shelf: tighter) into fixed-size atlas pages; new page appended when full; LRU page/sprite eviction; separate grayscale and colour atlases; cleared on font/size change. Never recreate the whole cache on overflow. | internal; `font { atlas-budget-mb }` | must | kitty, ghostty, alacritty, windows-terminal, zellij, wezterm (anti-pattern) | Grow-only atlases (ghostty, WT, alacritty) never evict; wezterm whole-cache recreation hitches. Paging plus eviction bounds memory and avoids stalls. |
| RENDERING-03 | Persistent GPU cell buffers | Flat bg array (4 bytes/cell, row*cols+col) as storage buffer, per-row foreground instance lists (glyph, underline, strike, overline), list 0 reserved for cursor so it draws first; rows rebuilt and uploaded individually; small uniform block (projection, cursor colours, cell size, min contrast, flags). Per-cell bg sampled in shader for correct blending. | internal | must | ghostty, rio, windows-terminal, kitty | Enables row-granular upload; compact and a direct fit for wgpu. |
| RENDERING-04 | Render-state snapshot per frame | Under a short lock (or via channel from the pool, given our single-threaded core) copy visible rows into reused allocations with fully resolved styles and a map of only referenced extras; build GPU data from the snapshot with no lock held. Plain-text fast path for raw cell copy. | internal | must | ghostty (render.zig RenderState), rio (renderable.rs) | Decouples parse from render, bounds hold time, removes stale-style bugs. |
| RENDERING-05 | Damage model | Four states (Noop, Full, Partial, CursorOnly) plus per-row dirty bits with column bounds from the VT core; cells carry a clean bit; consumed frames record cursor position so idle drains emit nothing; whole-window escape hatch. | `tweak { damage-whole-window }` | must | foot, alacritty, rio, zellij | Idle costs nothing, typing touches one row; cursor bookkeeping avoids rio's idle-wake bug. |
| RENDERING-06 | Frame-callback gated rendering | Record refresh requests; render once per loop iteration after all PTY reads are drained; request wl_surface.frame on each commit, keep one frame in flight, pending damage accumulates and replays from the callback. Pacing derives from the callback, not timers (zellij's refresh pacer is only a fallback if no callback). | internal | must | foot, topic-gpu-latency, zellij | Vsync pacing and batching; supported on every compositor. |
| RENDERING-07 | Present mode and latency policy | Query surface caps: Mailbox if offered, else Fifo; Mesa lists Mailbox and Fifo always and Immediate only when wp_tearing_control_manager_v1 is bound, so never rely on Immediate; do not hard-code (rio's Fifo-only is an X11 workaround); set desired_maximum_frame_latency = 1. Measure per compositor. | `render { present-mode "auto"\|"mailbox"\|"fifo"; frame-latency 1 }` | must | wgpu docs, wezterm and rio (anti-pattern: Fifo, latency 2) | About one refresh less latency than the defaults WezTerm and Rio ship. |
| RENDERING-08 | Report damage to the compositor | Convert row damage to wl_surface.damage_buffer rectangles on each commit. | `tweak { damage-whole-window }` | should | foot, alacritty (rio does not; issue 1570) | Cheap on Wayland, less compositor work, especially on Abyss. Shipped by two mature terminals. |
| RENDERING-09 | Retained grid texture and damage history | wgpu exposes no buffer age: render grid to an offscreen texture updated only on damage, composite with glass UI each presented frame; where partial redraw of swapchain images is used, union last N frames of damage (alacritty keeps 2). | internal | should | alacritty (damage.rs), windows-terminal (dirty-rect model) | Correct partial redraw without buffer age; makes UI animation cheap. Retained texture is simpler than damage history, prefer it. |
| RENDERING-10 | Scroll as ring offset in row buffer | Scrolls recorded as coalesced {region, lines} entries; GPU side rotates row handles / ring offset into the row buffer so scrolled rows are never re-uploaded. | internal | should | foot (term_damage_scroll), windows-terminal (row pointer rotation) | Scroll floods are the worst upload case; the idea ports from memmove to index remap. |
| RENDERING-11 | memfd SHM sliding-window scroll | Not adopted. | none | no | foot (shm.c) | Solves a pixman/shm-specific problem; ring-indexed rows replace it. |
| RENDERING-12 | Buffer-age re-apply / pre-apply (shm fallback) | Only if a CPU/shm fallback ships. | tweak.pre-apply-damage | could | foot | Irrelevant on the wgpu path. |
| RENDERING-13 | Overflowing-glyph pre-pass | Propagate dirty bits across runs of cells whose glyphs bleed into neighbours; allow a glyph to spill into one empty neighbour cell. | `font { overflowing-glyphs }` | should | foot, topic-fonts | Nerd/Powerline icons and wide italics without clipping; required by the dirty-row logic. |
| RENDERING-14 | Procedural box, block, braille, powerline, branch glyphs | Draw U+2500-257F, block elements and shades, braille 2x4 dots, powerline triangles/half-ellipses (slight overshoot to avoid seams) from exact cell metrics, ahead of font lookup; orthogonal lines direct-pixel, arcs and diagonals supersampled; cached in the atlas. | `font { builtin-box-drawing; builtin-braille; builtin-powerline; anti-alias-custom-glyphs }` | must | wezterm, foot, ghostty, alacritty, rio, vte, konsole, kitty | Seamless TUI borders regardless of font; universal across mature terminals. |
| RENDERING-15 | Geometric shapes (arcs, triangles, circles) | Path-drawn sprites sized to the cell. | font block | could | ghostty | Completes sprite set. |
| RENDERING-16 | Missing-glyph box | Own replacement box when no font covers a codepoint. | `font { missing-glyph-box }` | could | kitty | Predictable tofu; aids font debugging. |
| RENDERING-17 | Styled underlines and underline colour | SGR 4:0-4:5, 58/59. Straight in the rect pass; curly, dotted, dashed rasterised per cell into atlas sprites with integer column maths and remainder distribution so patterns tile cell width exactly (kitty technique preferred over SDF: seamless across cells). Fallback single underline. | KDL `underline {}`; escape sequences | must | kitty, alacritty, foot, ghostty, windows-terminal | LSP/editor diagnostics depend on undercurl. |
| RENDERING-18 | Separate rect pass for decorations | Underlines, strikes, cursor shapes, visual bell, selection and search overlays drawn as rects in their own shader. | internal | should | alacritty, zellij | Keeps text shader simple. |
| RENDERING-19 | Text blending and contrast | Offer native / linear / linear-corrected blending; default linear-corrected: blend luminance in gamma space, solve alpha for target linear luminance, premultiplied output, skip below 0.001 difference. Add tunable text gamma/contrast and thin-font boost. | `text { alpha-blending; text-contrast; text-gamma }` | must | ghostty, kitty (text_composition_strategy), foot, windows-terminal | Fixes bold/thin artefacts of linear blending and works on translucent glass; three terminals expose a knob. |
| RENDERING-20 | Antialiasing mode | Grayscale AA default; LCD opt-in and auto-off on translucent surfaces; LCD via dual-source blend where wgpu feature exists, grayscale fallback; colour glyphs flagged. | `text { antialiasing "gray"\|"lcd" }` | must | alacritty, windows-terminal, topic-fonts | Apotheosis is glass by default and compositors blend channels uniformly. |
| RENDERING-21 | Integer-origin grid glyphs | Grid glyphs rasterised once per (glyph, size, style) at zero fractional offset; fractional offsets only for UI text. | internal | must | foot (fcft), swash | Small atlas, sharp grid. |
| RENDERING-22 | Cursor-aware run breaking | Break shaping runs around the cursor cell so ligatures un-fuse under the cursor. | `font { ligatures "always"\|"cursor"\|"never" }` | must | ghostty, kitty | Makes editing visible. |
| RENDERING-23 | Ligature slicing and shaped-run cache | Shaped glyph wider than a cell rendered once and sliced per cell; detect infinite ligatures (repeats over 9 cells) and reuse middle sprite; cache shaped runs. | internal | should | kitty (fonts.c) | Fits ligatures into a per-cell atlas and bounds growth. |
| RENDERING-24 | CPU-side folding of selection, search, inverse, dim | Apply to premultiplied RGBA before upload; shader has no selection bits. | internal | should | rio | Simple shaders, decouples UI features from draw path. |
| RENDERING-25 | Cursor inversion and minimum contrast | Uniforms carry cursor colours/position so text shader swaps glyph colour under block cursor; per-cell WCAG min contrast with per-glyph opt-out flag. | `text { minimum-contrast }`, `cursor { text-color }` | should | rio, ghostty | Cheap accessibility win. |
| RENDERING-26 | Cursor styles and blink | DECSCUSR (0-7), modes 12/13/14, OSC 12 colour; same Action and KDL surface. | `cursor { shape; blink; color }` | must | xterm, all | Universal. |
| RENDERING-27 | Text blink modes | never / focused / unfocused; honour reduce-motion. | `text { blink }` | should | vte, konsole | Accessibility. |
| RENDERING-28 | Backgrounds | Image, gradient, opacity, HSB adjust, per-pane dim; blur via Abyss protocol (other compositors: opacity only, blur hint where available). | `background {}`, `opacity` | should | wezterm, kitty, ghostty | Glass UI exceeds this; keep basics. |
| RENDERING-29 | Focused/unfocused appearance | Appearance object (scheme, cursor, opacity, background) with a second used when pane is unfocused. | `appearance {}`, `unfocused-appearance {}` | should | windows-terminal, wezterm | Focus-dim for splits. |
| RENDERING-30 | Alpha-capable adapter and surface selection | When transparency requested, enumerate adapters keeping those whose surface offers pre- or post-multiplied alpha, rank by device class, never choose software, fall back if device creation fails; clear colour premultiplied exactly when surface composites premultiplied. | `render { adapter }` | must | rio (webgpu.rs, changelog) | Both Vulkan and DX12 paths upstream shipped opacity bugs. |
| RENDERING-31 | Synchronized output (2026) | Buffer grid mutations between CSI ?2026 h/l, present atomically at next frame callback; hard timeout and size cap; legacy DCS =1s/=2s. | `sync-output { timeout-ms }` | must | contour, kitty, foot, ghostty, wezterm (terminal-wg spec) | Removes tearing in tmux/neovim. |
| RENDERING-32 | OSC 4/10-19/104-119 colours | Set, query, reset palette and dynamic colours; resets restore profile colours; per-profile allow toggle. | `colors { allow-app-changes }` | must | xterm, kitty | Background detection for vim/bat. |
| RENDERING-33 | OSC 21 kitty colour control | Batched set/query/reset of colour slots. | same as above | could | kitty (unverified detail) | Nice to have. |
| RENDERING-34 | Inline preedit overlay | Draw preedit at cursor with underline, active segment highlight from cursor_begin/end, wide-char aware; not written to grid or PTY. | `ui { preedit-style }` | must | text-input-v3 | Usable CJK composition. |
| RENDERING-35 | Placeholder run merging | Merge adjacent Unicode-placeholder cells with same id and consecutive row/col into one rect; binary-search diacritic lookup. | internal | should | ghostty | Avoids a draw per cell. |
| RENDERING-36 | Sixel as cell-row slices | Split decoded image per cell row so erase/overwrite/scroll are slice bookkeeping. | internal | should | foot (sixel.c) | Correct ED/EL and scroll without re-decode. |
| RENDERING-37 | Partial-rect texture upload | queue.write_texture only the changed rect of animation frames. | internal | should | design recommendation (unverified benchmark) | Bandwidth saving. |
| RENDERING-38 | GPU YUV sampling and dmabuf import | NV12 planes sampled in the grid pass; hardware frames imported via wgpu-hal texture_from_raw with external-memory-fd and drm-format-modifier extensions. Needs a spike; fallback CPU copy. | `media { hwdec "auto"\|"vaapi"\|"vulkan"\|"none" }` | should | topic-media (unverified end to end) | Zero-copy composition with glass. |
| RENDERING-39 | Custom user shaders | WGSL post-process after grid render (ping-pong textures, time/resolution/background uniforms); animation toggle; instruction/time caps and validation via naga; pane-scoped. Not GLSL/HLSL. | `effects { shader "path"; animate }` | should | ghostty, windows-terminal | Two mature terminals ship it. Sandboxing is an open question. |
| RENDERING-40 | librashader CRT filter chains | Not adopted. | none | no | rio | Large dependency, overwrites alpha, conflicts with glass. |
| RENDERING-41 | Padding-extend edge colouring | Extend edge cell colours into margin on chosen sides, cursor exempt. | `window { padding-extend }` | could | rio | Removes margin seams. |
| RENDERING-42 | Colourspace and bit depth | Tag surface sRGB/Display-P3/Rec.2020 via wp_color_manager when offered, 10-bit surface where supported; matrix from input primaries. | `render { colorspace; bit-depth }` | should | foot, rio | Two terminals ship it; useful on wide-gamut. |
| RENDERING-43 | Graphics-API abstraction layering | Isolate target/frame/pipeline in our own code over wgpu; no multi-API layer. | internal | could | ghostty | Shows what to isolate only. |
| RENDERING-44 | Native Vulkan backend instead of wgpu | Not adopted. | none | no | rio | Bound to wgpu, no C build tools; extra transparency bugs. |
| RENDERING-45 | Dual backend / software renderer | Not adopted; software Vulkan adapter through the single wgpu path. | none | no | windows-terminal | One path is enough. |
| RENDERING-46 | DECSCLM smooth scroll | Not adopted; animated smooth scroll lives in the glass layer. | `scroll { smooth }` | no | xterm | Obsolete mode. |
| RENDERING-47 | fifo-v1 | Use wp_fifo_v1 barriers when advertised. | internal | could | wayland.app fifo-v1 | Smoother animation pacing on some compositors. |
| RENDERING-48 | wgpu-hal present chain | Attach VkPresentInfoKHR pNext for incremental present/timing. | internal | could | wgpu changelog (unreleased) | Only route to real present damage; portability burden. |
| RENDERING-49 | Platform pacing (waitable swap chain, display link) | Not adopted. | none | no | windows-terminal, ghostty | OS-specific; Wayland frame callback is the analogue. |
| RENDERING-50 | Three-primitive GUI renderer | Rects, images, glyphs from atlas for tabs, splits, palette, search; blur as separate pass. | internal | should | warp | Validates minimal primitive set. |
| RENDERING-51 | Text markers / highlight rules | Regex/text markers in up to 3 colour groups, match across wraps; as event-to-actions rules. | `rules { mark }` | could | kitty (marks.rst) | Log highlighting. |
| RENDERING-52 | Structured render frame for clients | Server sends cells, link table, cursor, pane geometry so native client renders without parsing ANSI. | control socket | should | zellij (pane_mirror.rs) | Our GUI is a wgpu client. |
| RENDERING-53 | Cursor trail effect | Typed options under `effects`, not scripts. | `effects { cursor-trail }` | could | kitty (cursor_trail), wezterm issue 7387 | Popular, fits fluid motion. |
| RENDERING-54 | Two-timer delayed render (lower/upper bound) | Each input restarts a short timer; a second longer timer set only after render caps total delay; disabled under DEC 2026. | pty.coalesce-ms/max-delay-ms | should | foot | Bounded latency with burst merging; foot technique. Source: foot terminal.c#fdm_ptmx; config tweak.delayed-render-lower/upper. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| RENDERING-55 | Fractional scale with viewporter, buffer_scale 1 | preferred_scale numerator over 120; set viewport destination to round(px/scale); buffer pixels chosen so round(scale*round(px/scale))==px. | none | must | foot | Crisp grid on 125/150% displays; wezterm integer-only is blurry. Source: foot wayland.c fractional_scale_preferred_scale; render.c surface_scale_explicit_width_height. (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| RENDERING-56 | wl_surface.preferred_buffer_scale integer fallback | Use v6 preferred_buffer_scale when no fractional manager; set_buffer_scale to integer. | none | should | foot | Covers compositors without fractional-scale. Source: foot render.c surface_scale_explicit_width_height. (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| RENDERING-57 | Opaque region discipline | Empty opaque region when translucent; full region when alpha is 1 or fullscreen. | none | must | foot | Wrong region breaks blending or wastes compositor work. Source: foot wayland.c wayl_win_alpha_changed. (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| RENDERING-58 | Rounded-corner blur region approximation | wl_region is union of rectangles, so rounded glass needs stair-step or accepts overhang. | none | could | design | Interface limit of the protocol; decide tolerance. Source: ext-background-effect-v1.xml set_blur_region. (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| RENDERING-59 | wgpu PreMultiplied alpha mode selection from surface caps | Query surface_caps.alpha_modes, choose PreMultiplied/PostMultiplied, premultiply clear colour; fall back opaque. | none | must | rio | Per-pixel transparency requires it; never assume driver support (unverified for Mesa). Source: rio sugarloaf/src/context/webgpu.rs. (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| RENDERING-60 | wp_single_pixel_buffer for solid overlays | Create 1x1 buffer and stretch via viewport for flat overlays without shm allocation. | none | could | foot | Cheap bell flash or dim overlays if separate surfaces are ever used. Source: foot render.c (overlay with single_pixel_manager). (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| RENDERING-61 | CompositeAlphaMode to VkCompositeAlphaFlagBitsKHR mapping | Opaque/PreMultiplied/PostMultiplied/Inherit map 1:1; Auto resolved in wgpu-core. | none | must | wgpu | Defines the transparency contract. Source: wgpu wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/conv.rs#map_composite_alpha_mode. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-62 | Auto alpha falls back only to Opaque then Inherit | Config validation substitutes only for Auto; explicit unsupported mode errors with UnsupportedAlphaMode listing available modes. | none | must | wgpu | Never rely on Auto for transparency; request PreMultiplied explicitly, handle failure. Source: wgpu wgpu-core/src/device/surface_config.rs. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-63 | Mesa Wayland WSI advertises OPAQUE and PRE_MULTIPLIED only | supportedCompositeAlpha has those two bits; PRE_MULTIPLIED selects ARGB DRM formats, otherwise XRGB. | none | must | mesa | On Mesa, transparency = PreMultiplied; PostMultiplied/Inherit are never offered. Source: mesa src/vulkan/wsi/wsi_common_wayland.c#wsi_wl_surface_get_capabilities. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-64 | Premultiplied colour discipline incl. clear colour and blend state | All writes to a PreMultiplied surface carry premultiplied RGB; blend One/OneMinusSrcAlpha; clear colour premultiplied. | none | must | rio | Rio 1967 shows washed-out transparency from unpremultiplied clear. Source: https://github.com/raphamorim/rio/issues/1967. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-65 | Rio issue 1967 attribution | Issue is about Rio's native-Vulkan backend (guess_composite_alpha, unpremultiplied clear); wgpu backend renders correctly; diagnosis unconfirmed and issue open. | none | no | rio | Not evidence of a wgpu transparency bug; record in ADR as premultiply lesson only. Source: https://github.com/raphamorim/rio/issues/1967. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-66 | NVIDIA proprietary composite alpha on Wayland | 2022 forum report: supportedCompositeAlpha 0x1 (opaque only); current drivers unverified. | none | should | design | Runtime query plus in-app warning and Opaque fallback needed; test on the NVIDIA dev box. Source: https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/wayland-vulkan-ignores-vkcompositealphaflagskhr/202227. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-67 | Opaque region handling by app | Mesa WSI never calls wl_surface_set_opaque_region; app should set it for opaque windows/regions so compositor can skip blending. | none | should | mesa | Perf win and correct blur-behind semantic; app owns the wl_surface. Source: mesa src/vulkan/wsi/wsi_common_wayland.c (absence by grep). (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-68 | CurrentSurfaceTexture variant handling (Outdated, Suboptimal, Lost, Occluded, Timeout, Validation) | Outdated/Suboptimal -> reconfigure; Lost -> recreate surface or device; Occluded/Timeout -> skip frame. | none | must | wgpu | Resize/monitor hotplug and compositor events surface here. Source: wgpu wgpu/src/api/surface_texture.rs. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |

Source: wgpu wgpu-types/src/surface.rs. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| RENDERING-70 | Synchronized update mode 2026 as render-hold at grid level | Keep parsing and updating grid, tell renderer to keep presenting last frame until ESU or timeout. | none | must | ghostty, rio | Preserves query replies and avoids raw-byte copy; majority design (ghostty, rio, foot, wezterm). Source: ghostty src/termio/stream_handler.zig; rio rio-vt/src/crosswords/mod.rs#sync_update_deadline. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |

Source: ghostty src/termio/Thread.zig#sync_reset_ms; foot terminal.c. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |

### Open questions

- Dual-source blending availability in wgpu on target Vulkan drivers: confirm fallback quality (RENDERING-20).
- Cell storage buffer plus instancing vs packed vertex instances: needs a benchmark (RENDERING-01, 03).
- Retained texture vs damage history when partial redraw is needed (RENDERING-09).
- Custom shader sandbox limits and Abyss blur protocol shape (RENDERING-28, 39).
- Dmabuf import into wgpu has no verified end-to-end example (RENDERING-38).
- Whether wgpu surfaces on Wayland can request wp_color_manager tags without hal access (RENDERING-42).


## performance

Merged from the per-terminal and per-topic surveys. "Seen in" names the sources of the input rows. Settings named below are proposed KDL keys under `performance { }` / `media { }`; each is also exposed in the settings GUI, as an Action, and over the control socket. Source locations are those cited in the input rows; items marked "unverified" were not verified.

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PERFORMANCE-01 | Event-driven render loop, zero idle wakeups | Render only when PTY data, input, a timer (blink, animation) or resize dirties state. One central timer queue on a calloop timer source drives blink, bell and animations, so the loop sleeps otherwise. | internal; `performance { animation-fps }` | must | foot, kitty, ghostty, alacritty, wezterm | All mature terminals do this. Energy and latency. wezterm's separate low cap for easing effects (default 10 fps) is adopted so blink and fades do not pull full-rate frames. |
| PERFORMANCE-02 | Wayland frame-callback pacing state machine | Per-surface state None/Requested/Received. Request the frame callback at present time, never speculatively. A callback requested with no commit never fires and wedges the loop. Redraw only when none is pending. Pacing follows compositor callbacks, not a timer. `wp_presentation` refresh prediction can refine the schedule. | `performance { frame-pacing }`, `max-fps` (ignored on Wayland, as in wezterm) | must | rio, kitty (`sync_to_monitor`), wezterm, vte-konsole (GNOME 46), rio vblank throttle | Pacing matters more than parse speed (GNOME 46 VTE result). We choose frame callbacks over rio's mHz-derived one-shot timer because the callback is the compositor's own truth and handles VRR and multi-monitor. The wedge comment in rio documents a real deadlock class. |
| PERFORMANCE-03 | Frame skipping under flood, coalesced scroll | When output outpaces refresh, parse as many lines as arrive, then render the latest state once per callback. Intermediate states are dropped and frames are never queued. Scroll and damage accumulate between frames (the GPU analogue of xterm jumpScroll). | `performance { frame-pacing }` | must | foot, xterm (jumpScroll/fastScroll), wgpu Mailbox semantics | Prevents lag after `cat` of a large file. Never render per escape sequence. |
| PERFORMANCE-04 | Delayed-render lower/upper timers (anti-flicker) | Each PTY read re-arms a short lower timer. The first read of a burst arms a hard upper timer. Render when the lower timer fires with no further data, or when the upper timer fires. Defaults follow foot: 0.5 ms lower, 8.33 ms upper. Also honour the kitty-style `repaint_delay` and `input_delay` semantics: the delay is ignored when input is pending or the backlog is large. | `performance { render-delay-lower, render-delay-upper }` (ns, 0 disables), `input-delay`, `repaint-delay`; a `low-latency` profile sets them to 0 | must | foot, kitty, topic-gpu-latency | foot users rely on no flicker from programs that lack mode 2026. We choose foot's two-timer form over kitty's single fixed delay because the upper cap bounds latency while the lower timer still ends the wait early. |
| PERFORMANCE-05 | Keystroke-echo fast path | Output arriving within a short window after a local key event renders immediately and skips the coalescing timers. | `render.echo-window` (T-07 §2) | must | proposal (derived from kitty `input_delay` tradeoff) | Typing latency is the key budget. Coalescing is for bulk output. Not shipped as such by any surveyed terminal, so the window length needs measurement (see Open questions). |
| PERFORMANCE-06 | Synchronized output (DECSET 2026) with safety timeout | Hold rendering from BSU to ESU. Reset by force after 1000 ms (ghostty's constant) so a crashed program cannot freeze the display. | `performance { sync-timeout-ms }` | must | kitty, ghostty | Protocol facts only. Interacts with PERFORMANCE-04: while sync is active the lower/upper timers are suspended. |
| PERFORMANCE-07 | Parse off the render path, with snapshot handoff | VT parsing runs on a worker. The renderer takes only the dirty rows it needs, either as a short-lived snapshot or via channel handoff. A wakeup tells the renderer to draw. | internal | must | kitty, ghostty, alacritty, rio, topic-gpu-latency | Smoothness under floods. Terminals split on the mechanism: alacritty and rio use a shared lock with try-lock and fair hand-off, ghostty uses a short-lock row snapshot. We prefer the snapshot or channel form because the EclipseOS rule is no locks on the hot path. See Open questions on who owns the grid. |
| PERFORMANCE-08 | Bounded, fair PTY drain | Cap bytes per parse slice and per wake. The seen values are: foot 24 KiB per read and 10 reads per wake; alacritty 1 MiB buffer in about 64 KiB slices; rio 64 KiB reads, 4 chunks (256 KiB) per lease; vte about 8 KiB chunks with a 1-byte overlap for split UTF-8 and a 100 ms processing cap per tick. Use 64 KiB reads, about 256 KiB per slice, and a time cap in ms. Unbounded only after hangup. A short read is trusted as drained except after child exit. | `performance { max-read-bytes, max-process-ms }` | must | foot, alacritty, rio, vte-konsole | Four terminals bound the drain, so this is required. Throughput comes from large reads, fairness from the per-wake cap. A time cap is added to the byte cap, taken from vte, because bytes alone do not bound slow sequences. A single noisy pane must not starve input or other panes. |
| PERFORMANCE-09 | Coalesced damage wakeup flag | The parser posts one lightweight "damaged" event and sets an in-flight flag. No more events are sent until the renderer consumes it. The damage itself stays in the terminal state. | internal | must | rio | Prevents event-queue floods under heavy output and keeps the renderer the single consumer. |
| PERFORMANCE-10 | Bulk ground-state text decode with ASCII bypass | In ground state, scan for the next ESC or control byte with a vector scan, bulk-decode UTF-8 up to it into a codepoint buffer (4096-entry chunks). If every byte was ASCII, skip decoding and use the input bytes directly. A partial trailing sequence goes to a scalar decoder. | internal | must | kitty, ghostty | Text is the dominant stream. The SIMD scan is credited for kitty's throughput lead. Avoids a per-byte state machine. |
| PERFORMANCE-11 | Printable-run splitting and width-class batch fill | Split decoded text into printable runs (single mask test for C0/C1) and pass slices to the handler. Fill cells in batches specialised by width class (narrow/wide), grapheme mode and styled state. Codepoints up to 0xFF skip the width lookup. | internal | must | ghostty | Amortises per-codepoint cost on the hot path. Only ghostty is documented here, but it is the same hot path every terminal needs. |
| PERFORMANCE-12 | Bulk OSC/APC/DCS consumption and streaming payload decode | Vector-scan for terminator or abort bytes, then give the whole slice to the string parser. Base64 graphics/media payloads are stream-decoded into a preallocated buffer with no allocation per chunk. | internal | must | ghostty, topic-media | A 1080p direct kitty frame is about 2,025 chunks of 4096 base64 bytes (8.3 MB), about 249 MB/s at 30 fps (arithmetic from the kitty protocol chunk limit). Without a fast path the parser is the bottleneck. |
| PERFORMANCE-13 | Rust vector techniques, no C++ | Use `memchr`-style and `simdutf8`-style crates or stable `std::arch` intrinsics for the scans. Benchmark every hot loop (PERFORMANCE-29) and never assume auto-vectorization. | internal | should | ghostty, kitty | Ghostty measured a 2.8x ASCII slowdown when a replaced memset lost LLVM auto-vectorization. Licences of candidate crates must be checked against the allowed list (unverified). |
| PERFORMANCE-14 | Line-range damage from core to compositor | The terminal emits Full or Partial line-range damage. The renderer keeps damage for two frames to handle buffer age, merges rects with some overdamage, and reports them via `wl_surface.damage_buffer`. A debug mode paints damage. | `debug { paint-damage }` | must | alacritty, windows-terminal | Cuts GPU and compositor work. Same structure serves the instance-buffer rebuild. |
| PERFORMANCE-15 | GPU scroll copy and dirty-region present | Copy the scrolled region on the GPU and redraw only invalidated rows. | `rendering { partial-invalidation }` (default on) | should | windows-terminal | Lower power. Whether wgpu on Wayland can use it is open (see Open questions). |
| PERFORMANCE-16 | Opaque-region hint | When the background is opaque, set an opaque region on the surface so the compositor can skip blending. | follows `opacity` | could | windows-terminal (analogue) | Apotheosis glass surfaces are mostly translucent, so the benefit is small. Applies in fullscreen or opaque-background profiles. |
| PERFORMANCE-17 | Compact instanced quad buffer | One instance per cell or quad, with i16 positions, u16 size and texcoords and a u32 colour, in a CPU-written instance buffer. Instance-buffer size was the biggest performance and power lever in the source terminal. | internal | must | windows-terminal | Direct guidance for the wgpu vertex layout. |
| PERFORMANCE-18 | Sprite atlas keyed by cell size | Render box-drawing, block and powerline glyphs once per (cell size, codepoint) into the R8 atlas, so all glyph kinds use the same instanced draw path. | internal | must | rio, ghostty | Zero steady-state cost, simple draw path. |
| PERFORMANCE-19 | Atlas dirty and resize counters | Rectangle-bin atlas with separate modification and resize counters, so the renderer uploads only on change. R8 for masks, RGBA8 for colour glyphs. | internal | should | ghostty, rio | Cheap upload tracking under wgpu. |
| PERFORMANCE-20 | Generation counters for invalidation | Separate settings, font and misc generations decide which GPU resources to rebuild. | internal | should | windows-terminal | Cheap config hot-reload without a full rebuild. Also drives shape-cache invalidation (PERFORMANCE-21). |
| PERFORMANCE-21 | Row-bounded, position-independent shaping runs and shape cache | Runs never cross rows and split on style or font. The run hash uses relative cluster positions, so identical text at different columns hits the same cache entry. Clear the shape cache when fallback fonts change (wezterm's ClearShapeCache signal; here the font generation counter). | `font { ligatures }` | must | ghostty, wezterm | Main CPU saver for ligature fonts. Unchanged rows are not reshaped each frame. |
| PERFORMANCE-22 | Shared font grid across surfaces | Font set and atlases are shared between windows and panes, keyed on the font config. | internal | should | ghostty | Memory and startup savings with many panes. |
| PERFORMANCE-23 | Delayed reflow during interactive resize | While the window is dragged, truncate the grid cheaply, withhold the new size from the child and hold the pty. After an idle delay reflow the original grid to the final size and resume. Resize notifications to the pty are coalesced (about 25 ms in ghostty). | `performance { resize-delay-ms }`, `resize-by-cells`, `resize-keep-grid` | must | foot, ghostty | Avoids reflow storms and a flood of SIGWINCH. Users rely on it. |
| PERFORMANCE-24 | Dedicated PTY write path and timing constants | Writes, resize coalescing, sync-output safety reset and selection auto-scroll tick are kept off the read hot path. Starting constants: 25 ms resize coalesce, 1000 ms sync reset, 15 ms auto-scroll tick. | internal | should | ghostty | Concrete constants. We use a single-threaded core plus a pool, so this becomes timers in the loop, not a thread. |
| PERFORMANCE-25 | Parallel row rendering workers | Not adopted. | n/a | no | foot | CPU-renderer specific. On the GPU, row work is filling the instance buffer, which is cheap on one thread. |

A control-socket method injects a synthetic key into a PTY loopback and records the time to presentation, reporting p50, p99 and p99.9 under idle and under background load. | control socket `perf.latency-probe`, CLI, settings GUI page | must | foot, topic-gpu-latency, Dan Luu methodology | It is the only way to measure real latency in the app, and it makes budgets enforceable in CI without hardware. Tail percentiles matter more than the mean. |
| PERFORMANCE-27 | Frame stats overlay and log | Overlay with frame time split into wait, render and present, queue depth, missed-refresh buckets and damage area. Optionally log to the journal. | `debug { render-timer = off\|osd\|log\|both }` | should | foot, alacritty | Foot and alacritty both ship a render timer. |

Throughput and latency are tracked separately. |

Add property tests for zero-width attach damage. | test suite | must | rio | Found a whole class of stale-glyph and scroll-offset bugs automatically. Cheapest insurance for a damage-driven renderer. |
| PERFORMANCE-31 | Parser fuzzing and conformance replay | Fuzz or property tests on the parser (panics, unbounded growth) and replay of a conformance corpus (vttest, esctest). | test suite | should | topic-escape-registry | Catches panics. Throughput numbers come from PERFORMANCE-29. |
| PERFORMANCE-32 | Golden-table key encoder tests and fuzzing | Pure-function encoder writing into a fixed stack buffer. Table tests against the kitty keyboard spec and fuzzing of modifiers and flags. | test suite | should | topic-keyboard | Zero-allocation hot path with conformance. |
| PERFORMANCE-33 | Input-rate sustained presentation for VRR | Rolling 100 ms window. | PERFORMANCE-34 | Occlusion and hidden-surface throttle | Stop rendering when frame callbacks stop or the surface is occluded. | PERFORMANCE-35 | Startup cost budget and lazy init | Create the wgpu instance, adapter and device asynchronously while the Wayland window shows a solid background first frame. | PERFORMANCE-36 | Page pool with demand-paged memory | Standard-size pages come from a pool of zeroed, demand-paged buffers. Idle preheated pages cost address space only. Pruned pages are reused for new pages. | `scrollback { lines }` | should | ghostty | Steady-state output at the scrollback limit allocates nothing. We choose page-granular incremental eviction over tmux's batch scheme (PERFORMANCE-41). |
| PERFORMANCE-37 | Page capacity growth hints | A ring of capacities that recent full pages needed (styles, graphemes, strings, hyperlinks) sizes the next page.
