| PERFORMANCE-38 | Reflow node recycling | During column-resize reflow, reuse one destroyed source page for the next destination page. | internal | could | ghostty | Avoids a decommit/recommit pair per page. Mainly benefits large scrollback. |

| PERFORMANCE-84 | Action-per-byte dynamic dispatch (wezterm VTActor) | Parser calls a &mut dyn actor per action. | none | no | wezterm | Prevents inlining on the hot path; use generic sinks. Source: wezterm vtparse/src/lib.rs#VTActor. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| PERFORMANCE-85 | C/C++ SIMD UTF-8/base64 (simdutf, Highway) | Vectorized decode in ground state. | none | no | ghostty, rio | Violates no C/C++ rule; use memchr and std. Source: rio rio-vt/Cargo.toml; ghostty src/simd/. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| PERFORMANCE-86 | Update coalescing and row cap for the AT tree | Send TreeUpdates at a bounded rate (suggested 50 to 100 ms) and expose only visible rows by default, never scrollback. | KDL exposed rows and update interval | must | design | Text diffing is linear in container size and event payloads grow with output rate; Apotheosis needs to keep its main-thread budget. Source: adapters/atspi-common/src/adapter.rs#emit_text_change_if_needed_parent. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| PERFORMANCE-87 | Lazy or off-thread Compose table build | Build the en_US table (about 5,200 lines) on first dead key or Compose press, or on a worker, then hand it to the main loop. | none | should | kbvm | Build time is unpublished; this keeps startup fast and respects the single-threaded core by returning over a channel. Source: kbvm/benches/compose.rs (only a no-match feed bench exists); host Compose file size. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |

## Open questions

- **Grid ownership (PERFORMANCE-07).** Does the parser worker own the grid and publish snapshots, or does the core own it and the worker only emits parsed ops? Needs a spike measuring snapshot cost on a full-screen redraw.
- **Echo fast path window (PERFORMANCE-05).** No surveyed terminal ships this. The window length and its interaction with PERFORMANCE-04 must come from measurement with the latency probe.
- **wgpu damage on Wayland (PERFORMANCE-14, 15).** Whether wgpu's Vulkan present on Wayland honours damage rects or buffer age is not settled by the rows. If it does not, damage only saves instance-buffer work, not compositor work. Unverified.
- **Startup numbers (PERFORMANCE-35).** No published cold-start numbers for wgpu terminals were found. Budgets must be measured.
- **Default values.** Delay, cap and time-budget defaults are copied from other terminals. Re-derive them on the latency and throughput benches before fixing them.
- **Crate licences.** Candidates (`memchr`, `simdutf8`, `lz4_flex`) are assumed acceptable but were not checked against the allowed licence list.
- **Scrollback store (PERFORMANCE-36 to 42).** Pages with compression, or a simpler ring, is a design decision for the scrollback/search category, not settled here.
- **VRR sustain (PERFORMANCE-33).** Single source (rio). Needs a check that Wayland input timestamps give the same signal.


## graphics

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GRAPHICS-01 | Kitty graphics protocol core | APC G control data (key=value) plus base64 payload; object model of images and placements; full action set transmit, transmit+display, put, delete, frame, animate, compose, query. | `ESC _ G ... ESC \`; a=T/t/p/d/f/a/c/q | must | kitty, ghostty, wezterm, rio, zellij | De facto standard; required for kitty animation and mpv --vo=kitty; used by icat, yazi, chafa, timg. Spec: https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/graphics-protocol/ |
| GRAPHICS-02 | Transmission mediums d/f/t/s with partial reads | Direct base64; regular file; temp file deleted after read; POSIX shm mapped then unlinked. S/O bound the bytes read. File, temp and shm are local-only (refuse when the session is remote or the path is not a regular file we may read), and reads are size-capped. | t=d/f/t/s, S, O; KDL `graphics { shm-transmission }` | must | kitty, ghostty, wezterm, mpv (client) | Direct is the only medium that works over ssh; shm removes base64 and PTY bandwidth (about 187 MB/s raw for 1080p30), which makes mpv --vo=kitty smooth. |
| GRAPHICS-03 | Chunking, zlib, tolerant parser | Chunks of at most 4096 bytes, only first chunk carries keys, m=1 until last. Allocation-free incremental parser; accept an unterminated single chunk transfer (mpv omits closing m=0). Per-terminal accumulator keyed by image id with a 10 s stale timeout. Inflate o=z with a pure-Rust inflater, bounded by the decoded cap. | m=, o=z | must | kitty, rio, ghostty, mpv (client) | Needed for conformance and mpv interop. Rio's stale timeout is the best DoS defence for half-sent transfers. |
| GRAPHICS-04 | Pixel formats RGB, RGBA, PNG | f=24/32/100. PNG decoded by the `png` crate (pure Rust). Per-axis cap 10,000 px. | f=24/32/100, s, v | must | kitty, ghostty, wezterm, rio | Baseline of the protocol; crates meet licence and no-C rules (see GRAPHICS-33). |
| GRAPHICS-05 | Palette-indexed formats | f=8/f=16 pixels reference terminal palette slots (incl. default fg/bg), so images recolour on theme change. Stored as indices, resolved at draw time. | f=8, f=16 | could | kitty | Only one mature terminal ships it, but it fits live theme switching in the glass UI. Advertise via a=q so clients can detect it. |
| GRAPHICS-06 | Image ids, numbers, placement ids, reply muting | i = u32 id; I = image number with terminal-chosen id (auto ids in the upper u32 half, as rio does, so they never collide with client ids); p = placement id; q=1/2 mutes OK/error replies. Replies rate-limited. | i, I, p, q | must | kitty, rio, ghostty | Core of the object model; muting and rate limit stop reply floods into the PTY. |
| GRAPHICS-07 | Placement geometry | Source rect x,y,w,h crop; X,Y sub-cell pixel offsets; c,r scale to cell box; C=1 do not move cursor. One textured quad per placement in the wgpu pass. | x,y,w,h,X,Y,c,r,C | must | kitty, ghostty, wezterm, rio | Needed by TUIs and file managers; maps directly to wgpu layers. |
| GRAPHICS-08 | Z-index layering | z orders placements; negative z draws under text; below -1073741824 draws under non-default cell backgrounds; ties broken by lower id. Image layers drawn in the same wgpu pass as the grid and glass UI. | z | must | kitty, ghostty | Needed for glass backgrounds behind text and for correct compositing with images in one pass. |
| GRAPHICS-09 | Relative placements | Child anchored to a parent placement with cell offsets (H,V); chain depth limit 8 (ETOODEEP beyond), cycle check (ECYCLE), missing parent ENOPARENT. | P,Q,H,V | should | kitty, ghostty | Needed for layered composites; small cost. Source: kitty/kitty/graphics.c (parent depth limit). |
| GRAPHICS-10 | Unicode placeholders | Virtual placement (a=p,U=1,c,r); cells hold U+10EEEE with row/column/high-byte diacritics, image id in fg colour, placement id in underline colour; runs of cells merged at render time. Images then scroll, reflow, copy and pass through tmux and our own mux as plain text. | a=p, U=1; U+10EEEE | must | kitty, ghostty, rio | Only image carrier that survives multiplexers, reflow and scrollback; central to a multiplexing terminal. Ghostty graphics_unicode.zig and rio kitty_virtual.rs show the run-merging approach. |
| GRAPHICS-11 | Animation frames and composition | a=f frames with sub-rect, base frame c, edit frame r, X overwrite/blend, Y background, z gap ms (default 40). a=c copies rects between frames (ENOENT/EINVAL/ENOSPC). Disagreement: kitty stores delta chains on disk, ghostty composes eagerly to full-size RGBA at load. We take eager composition, with frames counted against the image quota, because it is simpler and bounded; guard composition recursion depth (kitty coalesced-frame limit). | a=f, a=c | must | kitty, ghostty | Explicit goal of kitty animation. Frames live in a GPU frame store driven by our own clock. |
| GRAPHICS-12 | Animation control | a=a: s=1 stop, s=2 wait at last frame while loading, s=3 loop; v loop count; r+z edit a gap; c selects current frame for client-driven pacing (lets a player drive video itself). Terminal timer, coalesced with the frame scheduler; paused when occluded. | a=a keys s,v,r,z,c | must | kitty, ghostty | Terminal-side timing keeps playback smooth without client wakeups; the c= path covers client-paced video. |
| GRAPHICS-13 | Delete semantics | a=d with selectors a,i,n,c,f,p,q,r,x,y,z; lowercase removes placements, uppercase also frees unreferenced data. Clear screen, reset and alt-screen exit remove the relevant images. | a=d, d= | must | kitty, ghostty, rio | Clients rely on every variant for cleanup; it also drives the quota. |
| GRAPHICS-14 | Unified image store, quota and tiered eviction | One store and one byte budget (default 320 MB, `graphics-quota` in KDL) shared by kitty, sixel and iTerm2 images and animation frames; decoded cap per image 400 MiB at most. Eviction order from rio: inactive screen first, then unreferenced on the active screen, then visible, oldest first. GPU textures freed with the entry. One IPC listing. | KDL `graphics { quota }`; Action `graphics-clear` | must | kitty, ghostty, rio, wezterm | Bounded memory is a DoS defence; one quota and one renderer for every protocol. Rio's tiered order is the most predictable of the sources (kitty and ghostty simply cap). |
| GRAPHICS-15 | Per-screen image managers | Separate store partition for main and alternate screen; alt-screen images vanish on exit and never reach scrollback. | none | must | kitty | Prevents alt-screen images leaking; falls out of tiered eviction. |
| GRAPHICS-16 | Capability detection | a=q probes a load without storing; e=<action> reports supported keys. Clients follow with DA1 and treat DA1 without a query reply as unsupported. Native media protocol advertised through the same query path. | a=q, e=; DA1 | must | kitty, ghostty | All kitty-protocol clients autodetect this way; lets partial support and the native protocol be discovered. |
| GRAPHICS-17 | Sixel decoder | Streaming DCS state machine writing 6-pixel bands into an RGBA buffer; registers via RGB/HLS, repeat, CR/newline, raster attributes. P2 transparency with trailing blank row trim; default 2:1 aspect. Images in a per-grid list, overwritten by text, scrolled and reflowed, composited over text. Pixel and byte caps in KDL. | DCS q; DECSET 80 | should | foot, xterm, wezterm, rio, vte, windows-terminal, zellij | Shipped by six or more mature terminals and used by lsix, chafa, img2sixel, mpv --vo=sixel. Compatibility path, not preferred. Source: foot sixel.c sixel_init/sixel_unhook; render.c render_sixel_images. |
| GRAPHICS-18 | Sixel modes and negotiation | XTSMGRAPHICS read/set of colour registers (1024 max, clamp out-of-range) and geometry; DA1 attribute 4; DECSDM 80; private palette 1070 (default on); cursor-right placement 8452. Report the configured caps through XTSMGRAPHICS. | CSI ? Pi;Pa;Pv S; DECSET 80/1070/8452 | should | foot, xterm, vte | Clients probe these to choose quantisation and size. |
| GRAPHICS-19 | Sixel aspect table | P1 0-9 selects vertical scale from the ten-entry table. | DCS P1;P2;P3 q | could | xterm, foot | Trivial table for old producers. |

Foot row suggests the same. |

### Notes
- foot and alacritty have no kitty graphics; that gap is what Apotheosis fills, so no row.

### Open questions
- Kitty a=f frame storage: eager full-size RGBA (chosen) could exceed quota fast for long GIFs; need a decision on spilling frames to disk or compressing.
- Can wgpu export textures as dmabuf for handing buffers to Abyss? Not found (unverified).
- Whether iTerm2 inline images are wanted at should or could; one source marked protocol details unverified.
- Text sizing (OSC 66) grid layout rules need a design before commit.
- Exact default quota: kitty 320 MB vs rio 400 MiB decoded cap; we use 320 MB with a 400 MiB per-image ceiling.
- No ratified terminal-wg image protocol found; issue search failed.


## media

"Seen in" is "design" where the input rows named no shipping terminal; no terminal-shipping claims are made without a source (unverified).

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MEDIA-01 | Native media protocol with sandboxed decoder helper | Terminal opens the file and passes an fd to a Landlock+seccomp helper that demuxes and decodes; frames return over a unix socket with SCM_RIGHTS as dmabuf or memfd NV12/I420; compressed data never touches the main process. | New APC/OSC; actions `media-open`, `media-play-pause`, `media-seek`, `media-close`; IPC `media.*` | must | design; repo `ec-agentd` sandbox.rs | Decoders are the riskiest parsers shipped; matches the existing agentd sandbox pattern and the fixed helper decision. |
| MEDIA-02 | AV1 software decode (rav1d) | Rust port of dav1d inside the helper; asm fast paths via nasm-rs (external nasm) with cc used only as archiver on x86_64 (aarch64 .S via the C driver is rejected, Rust fallback instead); features asm, bitdepth_8, bitdepth_16; about 6% slower than dav1d. | config `media.av1-software`; cargo feature `asm` | should | design (https://github.com/memorysafety/rav1d, build.rs) | Only pure-Rust AV1 decoder with a permissive licence (BSD-2-Clause), but the asm build needs nasm. Resolved by rule amendment A: x86_64 asm via nasm only in the helper crate behind an av1-asm feature; aarch64 and dev builds use the Rust fallback (speed unmeasured). See gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule. |
| MEDIA-03 | VA-API hardware decode (cros-codecs + cros-libva) | Vendored cros-libva fork with committed bindings (no bindgen or libclang at build time; links va and va-drm) plus a cros-codecs fork without gbm; safe libva wrapper plus codec layer for H.264, H.265, VP8, VP9, AV1; libva 1.20+; hidden behind our decoder trait with software fallback. | config `media.hwdec vaapi` | must | design (https://github.com/chromeos/cros-codecs, cros-libva lib/build.rs) | libva is an allowed dependency with broad codec coverage, but 0.0.x maturity; the libclang and libgbm build concerns are resolved by the vendored fork with committed bindings and the gbm-free cros-codecs (gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule); the trait plus fallback contains the remaining risk. |
| MEDIA-04 | Vulkan Video decode (gpu-video) | H.264 decode directly into wgpu NV12 textures, no RAM round trip; H.265/AV1 planned. | config `media.hwdec vulkan` | could | design (https://github.com/software-mansion/smelter/tree/master/gpu-video) | Allowed dependency and clean integration, but H.264 only today; revisit when AV1 decode lands. |
| MEDIA-05 | Matroska/WebM demux (matroska-demuxer) | Pure-Rust demuxer yielding frames per track with codec private data. | internal | must | design (https://crates.io/crates/matroska-demuxer) | Zlib OR MIT OR Apache-2.0, updated 2026-08; covers WebM AV1+Opus, the primary native-protocol container. |
| MEDIA-06 | MP4/ISOBMFF demux | Candidates: symphonia-format-isomp4 (MPL-2.0, video packet exposure unverified), mp4 0.14 (MIT, stale 2023), mp4parse 0.17 (MPL-2.0, stale 2023). Pick after a spike confirming video packet access; prefer Symphonia to share one demux layer. | internal | should | design (https://github.com/pdeljanov/Symphonia, https://crates.io/crates/mp4, https://crates.io/crates/mp4parse) | MP4 is the most common container, but no candidate is verified for video. |
| MEDIA-07 | Symphonia audio decode (AAC-LC, Vorbis, FLAC, MP3) | Pure safe Rust decoders/demuxers with per-codec feature flags, within about 15% of FFmpeg; optional SIMD via rustfft. | cargo features aac, vorbis, flac, mp3, isomp4, mkv, ogg | must | design (https://github.com/pdeljanov/Symphonia, BENCHMARKS.md) | MPL-2.0 is allowed; covers common audio codecs without C. |
| MEDIA-08 | Opus decode (opus-decoder) | Pure-Rust RFC 8251 conformant decoder, forbid(unsafe_code), MIT OR Apache-2.0. | internal | must | design (https://docs.rs/crate/opus-decoder/0.1.1) | symphonia-codec-opus is not on crates.io, and Opus is the WebM audio standard. |
| MEDIA-09 | PulseAudio-protocol audio output via pipewire-pulse (pipewire-rs rejected) | Pure-Rust MIT pulseaudio crate speaking the PulseAudio native protocol to the pipewire-pulse socket; no libpipewire linked; wrapped behind an AudioSink trait; missing socket means muted playback with a typed GUI error. | config `media.audio-output`; actions `media-volume`, `media-mute` | must | design (https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/pipewire/pipewire-rs, https://docs.pipewire.org/page_streams.html) | The decision text must read "PipeWire" as the runtime service via its pulse module. pipewire-rs needs libclang plus compiled C wrappers (libspa-sys) and pipewire-native cannot play audio yet, so neither fits the no-C rule. See gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule. |
| MEDIA-10 | Audio-master A/V sync | Audio clock from the PulseAudio GetPlaybackLatency reply (sink latency, read and write offsets, underrun count); present video on the frame callback when PTS <= audio clock + display latency; drop late frames; nudge resampler ratio for drift. | config `media.sync-master`; rule event `media-error` | must | design (https://docs.pipewire.org/page_streams.html) | Standard approach; the terminal owns the clock, which no escape-sequence path offers. |

## Open questions

- No-C rule vs rav1d asm (nasm, cc): proposed rule amendment A in gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule (no C-family source compiled; nasm exception in the helper crate; committed bindings for cros-libva; pulseaudio crate instead of pipewire-rs). Owner decision still needed to adopt it.
- MP4 demux: does symphonia-format-isomp4 expose video packets and codec config? Needs a spike.
- Hardware path order: VA-API first (broad codecs) vs Vulkan Video (zero-copy, H.264 only); how does fallback pick per codec?
- Frame return format: dmabuf vs memfd per GPU/driver, and import into wgpu without a copy.
- Kitty animation and `mpv --vo=kitty` paths are fixed decisions but absent from these rows; they belong to the graphics/protocol categories.
- Which shipping terminals (if any) have native video playback? Not established by the inputs; "Seen in" is design-only.


## mux

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MUX-01 | Session model separable from windows | Panes, tabs, splits and floats are plain data owned by a session object; a window is only a view. The server owns ptys and terminal state; detach leaves everything running. | attach, detach, new-session, kill-session Actions; CLI and socket verbs | must | wezterm, zellij, tmux, topic-mux-remote | Prerequisite for layouts, rules, profiles, control socket, detach and restore. Benchmarks (kitty docs, alacritty+tmux about 24.7 vs 54.1 MB/s) show an external multiplexer roughly halves throughput, so ours renders cells directly with no re-encode. |
| MUX-02 | Domain abstraction (local, unix, ssh, exec, tmux-cc, serial) | A domain owns spawn/split/attach/detach/state; panes from any domain implement one Pane trait; the GUI is a client of domains. | KDL `domain` blocks; `domain` field on spawn Actions; `connect <domain>` | must | wezterm, topic-mux-remote | One concept covers local, remote, container and tmux panes; maps to KDL profiles and settings GUI rows. Avoids a bolt-on mux. |
| MUX-03 | Headless mux server over Unix socket with auto-start | Server process owns ptys; GUI clients attach. Client spawns the server if the socket connect fails (opt-out). Socket ownership and permission check on connect. Default single-process mode stays; persistence is a per-domain switch. | `serve_automatically`, `socket_path`, `skip_permissions_check` KDL keys | must | wezterm, tmux, zellij | Detach/attach is the core reason people run tmux. Sources disagree on default (wezterm auto-starts, topic row says opt-in); we take auto-start on demand for domains that ask for persistence, off for the default local domain. Ownership check is a cheap security win. |
| MUX-04 | Resident process with shared fonts, glyph cache, multi-window and single-instance targeting | One process hosts all windows sharing config, fonts and glyph atlas; I/O on one calloop-style loop; per-window render. Daemon mode with no window; CLI picks new vs existing window and named windows. | `--daemon`, `new-window`, `--window <name>`, windowing_behavior KDL key | must | foot, alacritty, windows-terminal | Fast spawn and low per-window memory. Isolate panes better than foot (pane crash must not take the process down). |
| MUX-05 | Single-owner state with message-passing threads | One thread owns layout and pane state; pty, writer, decoder and background jobs are threads that send typed instruction enums over channels. | internal | should | zellij, tmux | Matches EclipseOS handle-based single-owner discipline; no locks on the pane tree. |
| MUX-06 | Mux wire framing | Frame = length (top bit marks compression), request serial, stable numeric message id, then compact binary body; server push messages for pane, tab, title, workspace changes. Obsolete ids retired, never reused. Compression only above a small threshold and only if smaller; off on local sockets; use pure-Rust ruzstd, not the C zstd crate. | internal; `apotheosis ctl` uses it | should | wezterm | Good skeleton; C dependency rules out wezterm's zstd. |
| MUX-07 | Protocol handshake with capability negotiation and N-1 grace | Hello exchange of version plus capability set; accept the previous version, refuse older with a clear UI message. | internal | must | wezterm | wezterm requires exact version equality, which strands long-lived sessions on update. Capabilities beat version numbers for incremental features. |
| MUX-08 | Dirty-line render sync with stable row indices | Clients cache lines by stable row index; server pushes changed line ranges (deltas, not polling); missing scrollback and image cells fetched lazily. | internal | should | wezterm | Robust to reconnect and differing client sizes. Push instead of wezterm's poll. |
| MUX-09 | Multi-client attach with window-size policy | Several clients per session; size policy smallest/largest/latest/manual, plus per-client tab sizes so a small client does not shrink everyone. | `window_size` KDL key, `aggressive_resize` | should | tmux, zellij | Per-client sizing (zellij 0.45) avoids tmux's smallest-client problem. |
| MUX-10 | Read-only watcher client | Attach role that follows a session with no input. | socket stream role; `attach --watch` | could | zellij | Pair viewing and demos; falls out of the client role model. |
| MUX-11 | Session list, names, previews, kill/delete | List live and resurrectable sessions; picker in palette shows live previews; aliases and names. | list-sessions, attach, kill-session, delete-session Actions; palette picker | must | zellij, tmux | Needed for any multi-session workflow; previews make switching fast. |
| MUX-12 | Workspaces | Per-window string tag persisted by the server; GUI shows one workspace; built-in save/restore of layouts per workspace. | switch-workspace, workspace-next/prev, rename-workspace, `default_workspace` | must | wezterm, windows-terminal | Simple model; add built-in save/restore that wezterm leaves to plugins. |
| MUX-13 | Built-in layout presets and free split tree | Per-tab layout algorithm over the pane list: even-h, even-v, main-h/v (mirrored), tall, fat (bias and full_size params), grid, stack, plus free splits. Names accept unambiguous prefixes. | `layout` Actions: select-layout, next-layout, prev-layout; layout params in KDL | must | kitty, tmux, zellij | Covers tiling presets and freeform splits under one vocabulary. Kitty's parameterised form is the model; tmux preset names and prefix matching are the muscle memory. |
| MUX-14 | Binary split tree with full pane operations | Own tree: nodes hold split orientation and fraction; operations walk to the neighbour. Actions: split, close, focus-dir, swap, move, rotate, break to tab, join, attach/detach across tabs and windows, resize, equalize, draggable dividers. | split-h/v, move-pane, swap-pane, resize-pane, break-pane, join-pane, rotate Actions | must | windows-terminal, vte-konsole, tmux, zellij, kitty, ghostty, rio | Union of tmux vocabulary and Windows Terminal tree ops. Rio's taffy proves viable but a purpose-built tree plus MUX-15 gives needed control. |
| MUX-15 | Percent-or-fixed dimensions with constraint resize | Geometry is percent or fixed cells; a resizer solves with minimum sizes and fixed bars. | `size=` in layouts; resize Actions with direction and amount | must | zellij | Layouts reserving fixed chrome while others flex; stable resize. |
| MUX-16 | Zoom focused pane | Temporarily expand a pane over the tab; restore on toggle; modal floats may sit above. | pane.zoom-toggle | must | tmux, zellij, ghostty, windows-terminal, warp-wave | Shipped everywhere; used constantly. |
| MUX-17 | Stacked panes | A cell holds N panes; one expanded, others as one-line titles; combine, break out, resize, make room for new pane; horizontal or vertical title list. | `stacked=true` in layouts, toggle-stack-orientation | must | zellij | Named requirement and zellij's most distinctive feature. |
| MUX-18 | Floating pane layer | Tiled and floating panes kept as separate collections per tab; layer show/hide, per-pane pin, z-order, raise on focus, embed/float toggle. | toggle-floating, toggle-embed-float, `pinned`, floating nodes with x/y/width/height | must | zellij, tmux | Named requirement; scratch terminals and popups. |
| MUX-19 | Floating placement search | Try centred half-size, then top-left, then top-right, stepping an offset until the rect fits inside the viewport and avoids existing floats. | internal; coordinates overridable | should | zellij, tmux | Deterministic staggering; simple algorithm. |
| MUX-20 | Modal floating pane | One per window; blocks other panes; options: close on outside click, forward all keys, close on Esc, stay above zoom. | Action parameters | should | tmux | Model for approval dialogs and pickers; maps to glass dialogs. |
| MUX-21 | Swap layouts guarded by pane-count | Ordered list of layouts each with min/max/exact pane count; first fit wins; cycle or pick by name. | `auto_layout`, next/prev-swap-layout, named selection | should | zellij | Automatic tiling policy without scripting. |
| MUX-22 | Named layouts and session files in KDL | KDL `layout` tree: windows, tabs, splits, cwd, command, profile, focus. Applied by apply-layout, saved by save-layout (snapshot of live state, optionally relocatable, using foreground process). | apply-layout, save-layout, load-layout Actions; `--layout`, `startup_layout`; CLI | must | kitty, zellij, vte-konsole, windows-terminal, wezterm | Project workspaces are a core use case; kitty's snapshot plus konsole per-leaf profile/cwd/command; same vocabulary as live Actions. |
| MUX-23 | Periodic session serialization to KDL | Background job writes the live session every interval (default 60 s) when dirty and non-default: geometry, command, args, cwd, title, focus, floats. Offered for resurrection on next attach or start. File doubles as a hand-editable layout. | `session_serialization`, `serialization_interval` KDL keys; dump-layout, save-session | must | zellij | Crash and reboot recovery; closes tmux's persistence gap. |
| MUX-24 | Atomic session-file writes | Write temp, fsync, rename; keep last good copy; validate on load. | internal | must | zellij | Corrupt restore file defeats persistence (zellij fixed this in 0.46). |
| MUX-25 | Restore policy, undo close, buried sessions | Restore last layout on start (positions, tree, cwd, profile; not contents); time-limited undo-close; buried sessions keep running hidden. | `restore` KDL key, undo-close Action, bury/unbury | should | windows-terminal, iterm2, ghostty | Expected on a desktop. Scrollback restore is an open question. |
| MUX-26 | Running-command discovery and rewrite rule | On serialize, walk the pane's process tree for the foreground command; a KDL rule strips wrappers (prefer declarative match/replace over a shell string). | `command_discovery` rule block | should | zellij | Needed to relaunch what the user was running. |
| MUX-27 | Versioned layout serialization | Layout and floats serialized with a schema version and validation; optional checksum is not needed since KDL gives structure. | schema version in KDL and JSON socket forms | could | tmux | Control socket needs a versioned geometry format; checksum is low value. |
| MUX-28 | Broadcast input | Modes: current, all in tab, all in window, selected panes or match expression; persistent visible indicator; per-pane opt-out. | `broadcast.set` mode=... Action; `--match` | must | iterm2, kitty, tmux, windows-terminal, vte-konsole, warp-wave | Core multi-host admin feature shipped by six; indicator for safety. |
| MUX-29 | Read-only pane | Pane ignores input when set. | toggle-pane-readonly | should | windows-terminal | Protects long-running output panes; cheap. |
| MUX-30 | Remain on exit / hold on close | Dead pane stays with exit status; rerun on Enter; modes on, failed. | `remain_on_exit` KDL key; `hold_on_close` in layouts | must | tmux, zellij | Prevents losing output of failed commands; rerun is a good default for layout command panes. |
| MUX-31 | Run command in new pane | Spawn Action with close-on-exit and zoom flags. | pane.run close=on-exit zoom=true | should | warp-wave | Falls out of the Action vocabulary. |
| MUX-32 | Exec domain command wrapper | Declarative template wraps argv/env of every spawn in the domain (docker exec, distrobox, kubectl); no scripting callback. | KDL `domain` with `wrap { argv "docker" "exec" "{cmd}" }` | should | wezterm | Containers as first-class domains. Wezterm rows disagree should vs could; one ship, high user value, so should. |
