### Open questions

- Is the mux server the same binary and process as the GUI (opt-in headless mode) or a separate daemon that always runs? Rows lean toward same binary, opt-in per domain.
- Should session restore include scrollback contents (ghostty #1847 asks)? Rows only cover structure, cwd and command.
- Wire body encoding is unspecified (varint-serialized binary vs another serde format); needs a crate licence and size check.
- Does ruzstd performance suffice for remote domains, or is no compression acceptable?
- Which tmux-cc features to support in the first release (layout, scrollback capture, key modes)?
- Quake and tab tear-off need Abyss protocol support; spec unwritten.
- Kitty broadcast row source is a directory path, not verified.
- The Windows Terminal workspaces row cites an action name (OpenWorkspace) that is unverified.


## scrollback-search

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-01 | Offset-addressed slab pages with interned styles | Screen and history are a list of fixed-layout pages drawn from a pool. Each page holds rows, 64-bit cells, a ref-counted style table (id 0 = default), a grapheme map and a hyperlink table. All internal references are offsets, so a page can be copied bytewise, compressed, serialized or handed to a search thread. Rows are lazily filled, so memory is proportional to content. Oversized pages go to the heap when styles or graphemes overflow. | KDL `scrollback { lines; limit-bytes }` (see -03) | must | ghostty, foot, xterm, kitty | Memory per history line decides how much history fits. Pages give cheap snapshots, allocation-free steady state and fit handle-based state with no allocation on the hot path. Chosen over foot's power-of-two row-pointer ring (simple, wastes memory) and xterm's row FIFO (no style interning). |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-02 | Tracked pins with serials and garbage state | A pin is (page serial, row, col). Viewport, cursor, selection ends, search hits, prompt marks and annotations register as tracked pins. Prune, reflow and erase fix them up. Pins on pruned pages are flagged garbage. Serial ids, not pointers, allow O(1) validation even when memory is reused. | none (internal) | must | ghostty, wezterm, foot | Stable positions across reflow and eviction underpin selection, search and blocks. Serials are preferred to pointers because they survive allocator reuse. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-03 | Dual logical scrollback limits | Limits in uncompressed logical bytes (includes the active area) and in lines. Whichever hits first prunes whole historical pages. Pages overlapping the active area are never split. Both are changeable at runtime. Compression and disk tiers change physical memory only, not the limit. Defaults are far above xterm's 1024 lines. | `scrollback { lines; limit-bytes }`, Action `scrollback.set-limit`, GUI field | must | ghostty, alacritty, xterm, kitty, foot | Users reason about history length, not RSS. Alacritty's hard 100000-line cap is dropped because cold pages are cheap. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-04 | Idle LZ4 compression of cold pages | Complete pages outside the viewport and before the active boundary are LZ4-block compressed in bounded steps on idle ticks, tracked by an activity serial with a verification pass. Physical memory is released (MADV_DONTNEED) while the virtual range is kept, so restore is infallible. Needs a pure-Rust LZ4 crate (lz4_flex, MIT, unverified licence check needed). | `scrollback { compression=#true }` | should | ghostty | Only ghostty ships it, so "should" rather than "must", though one topic row asked for "must". It claims a 70-90% RAM cut on text history with no throughput cost. It is the enabler for large defaults. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-05 | Opt-in encrypted disk-spill tier | Rows beyond the memory budget go to row, text and attr streams in an unlinked 0600 temp file. Fixed 64 kB blocks are LZ4-compressed, then AES-256-GCM encrypted with an overwrite counter in the IV. Writes are batched and reads cached. Password-role content never enters it. Off by default. Uses a RustCrypto AEAD crate (MIT/Apache). | `scrollback { storage memory\|disk; encrypt }` | should | vte, konsole | Effectively unlimited history with low RSS. Konsole's unencrypted file history is rejected, and encryption keeps secrets at rest protected. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-06 | History modes and save/auto-save | Per-profile modes none, bounded in-memory, disk-backed, with a size control, a save-history action and an auto-save interval. Saved files are 0600. | profile `history-mode`, `auto-save-interval`, Action `scrollback.save` | could | konsole | The user model is useful, but most of it is covered by -03, -05 and -31. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-07 | Text-only pager history tier | A separate UTF-8+SGR ring (size in MB), not interactively scrollable, used for pager/export/old-output search and lazily rewrapped by re-flowing the text stream. | `scrollback { text-tier-mb }` | could | kitty | A cheap very-deep tier, but superseded by -04 and -05 if those ship. Revisit only if they slip. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-08 | Renderer-ready split cell layout | Keep a separate GPU-uploadable cell array beside a CPU-only text and attr array, with on-demand segment growth. | none (internal) | could | kitty | A hint for the wgpu renderer design. It conflicts with -01's single page layout, so decide in the renderer spec. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-09 | Logical-line search with offset map | Wrapped physical rows are joined into one logical line per haystack. Matches map text offsets back to (row, col) pins, so matches span wraps. Wide-cell spacers are skipped. | Action `search.*` | must | wezterm, alacritty, ghostty | Correct search over wrapped lines is the core requirement. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-10 | Lazy-DFA regex engine | Regex via regex-automata hybrid DFAs, four per pattern (forward and reverse for each direction). The forward pass finds the match end and the reverse pass finds the start, with no backtracking. Bytes are stepped from cells with the -09 mapping. Cache and NFA size limits apply, with wrap-around across the buffer. Pure Rust, MIT/Apache. | Action `search` arg `mode=regex`, GUI toggle | must | alacritty, rio, wezterm, vte | Linear time with bounded memory. PCRE2 (vte) is rejected as C. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-11 | Match modes | Five modes: literal, smart case (default), case-sensitive, case-insensitive, regex, plus whole-word and wrap toggles. A cycle-mode Action exists. | Action `search` args `mode`, `whole-word`, `wrap`; Action `search.cycle-mode`; KDL `search { mode; wrap }` | must | wezterm, windows-terminal, foot, alacritty, vte, konsole | Expected feature set. It maps one-to-one to Action arguments and GUI toggles. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-12 | Background sliding-window search | A dedicated worker pages through history newest-first. Page text goes through a circular window that keeps only needle-length overlap, so cross-page matches are correct. It works on copied page data to keep lock hold short, reports results as pin pairs, and refreshes the UI on a short timer. Adds progress count and pause-when-hidden, which ghostty lists as TODO. | Actions `search.start`, `search.next`, `search.prev`, `search.end` | must | ghostty, konsole, wezterm | Searches huge history without blocking IO or the render loop. The worker is a pool task returning via channel, consistent with the single-thread core rule. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-13 | Split immutable-history and active-area results | History results are cached as immutable. The active area is re-searched only when dirty. Viewport highlights re-run only for visible pages. An inactive screen keeps its results, so an alt-screen round trip does not restart. | none (internal) | must | ghostty | Live output must not invalidate the whole search. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-14 | Debounced chunked incremental search | Typing bumps a cookie, and stale cookies are dropped. After a short debounce (default 150 ms, shorter than wezterm's 350 ms) the search runs backward in row chunks so first hits appear fast. | `search { debounce-ms }` | should | wezterm, konsole | Responsive and cancellable. Tuned for latency. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-15 | Selected match as tracked highlight | The current match is stored as an index from the end plus a tracked pin pair, so n/N stays stable during live output and pruning. | Actions `search.next`, `search.prev` | should | ghostty | Stable navigation during streaming. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-16 | Bounded viewport highlight scan | Live highlights cover the viewport plus a capped margin of wrapped lines (alacritty uses 100). | `search { highlight-margin-lines }` | should | alacritty, ghostty | Bounds per-frame cost on huge history. Superseded for committed searches by -12. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-17 | Glass search overlay with scrollbar match marks | Compositor-independent glass overlay drawn by our wgpu UI: query box, mode toggles, match count and progress, highlight-all, and match ticks on the scrollbar. | Action `search.toggle`; KDL `search { highlight-all; scrollbar-marks; opacity }` | must | windows-terminal, konsole, wezterm, foot | Required glass UI. Scrollbar marks show where matches lie in history at a glance. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-18 | Match refinement and extend-selection | While searching, keys extend the match by char, word or line, paste into the box, and Return copies to PRIMARY. | search key table (KDL keybinds) | should | foot | A distinctive refinement workflow, layered on top of -10 and -11 (not replacing them). Foot is the only terminal shipping it, so "should". |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-19 | Copy mode with vi/emacs key tables | A modal overlay with its own key table. The full motion set is Actions: moves, word and zone motions, jump-to-char, line and rectangle selection, marks, search integration. Rebindable in KDL and drivable over the control socket. | Action `copy-mode.enter` and `copy-mode.*`; `mode-keys vi\|emacs`; KDL `keybinds copy-mode { }` | must | wezterm, tmux, zellij, kitty | Keyboard selection is a differentiator. One Action vocabulary makes it scriptable, with no embedded scripting. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-20 | Copy-mode auto-refresh | Opt-in refresh of copy mode as the pane changes, so logs can be followed while selecting. | `copy-mode { auto-refresh }` | could | tmux | Only tmux 3.8 ships it. Needs -02 pins to avoid selection drift. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-21 | Pin-based selection | Selections are pin pairs (line, rectangle) that survive scroll and reflow. Word boundaries come from a word-chars setting. Options for clear-on-typing, copy-on-select and trailing-space trimming. | `selection { word-chars; clear-on-typing; copy-on-select; trim-trailing }`, Action `selection.adjust` | must | ghostty, foot, wezterm | Correct selection semantics under reflow. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-22 | Reflow with tracked selection and images | Logical lines are rewrapped on resize. Selection, cursor and viewport pins are carried across, and image placements are repositioned. | `reflow { enabled }` | must | foot, ghostty, wezterm | A core incumbent strength. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-23 | Alt screen modes with scrollback policy | Support modes 47, 1047, 1048 and 1049 (1049 saves the cursor, switches to a fixed-size buffer without scrollback, clears it). A per-profile and per-app (rule) policy keeps alt-screen output in scrollback, equivalent to xterm titeInhibit. | `profile alt-screen-scrollback never\|always\|on-exit`, rules | must | xterm, ghostty, foot, kitty | Universal escape feature. The policy is the user control. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-24 | Placements anchored to buffer lines | Image and media placements are stored in buffer coordinates, shift on scroll, and are freed when rows leave scrollback or the limit drops. Reflow policy is defined explicitly (anchor to top row of the placement). | none (internal) | must | ghostty, kitty, foot | Correct scroll behaviour and memory release. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-25 | Block index over continuous scrollback | Store prompt, command and output row ranges and exit status per block as pin-based metadata over a single scrollback, with no grid splitting. Navigate by block. | Actions `block.select-prev`, `block.select-next`, `block.select-all-output` | must | warp, iterm2, wezterm, kitty | Block features without Warp's grid-split cost. Shell integration marks populate it (see the shell-integration matrix). |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-26 | Block-atomic eviction | Pruning keeps command blocks whole. Evicted blocks leave a record with a placeholder at the top. | `scrollback { evict-by block }` | should | contour | Avoids half-shown output. Interacts with -03, so page pruning must be block-aware (open question). |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-27 | Scoped search | A scope argument (all, screen, selection, block) limits search to a command's output range. | Action `search` arg `scope`; Find-in-block action | should | iterm2, warp | Reduces noise, and is trivial given block ranges. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-28 | Block output filter | A view-layer row mask over the selected or latest block that hides non-matching lines (match, invert, regex). Source data is untouched. | Action `block.filter` args `pattern`, `invert`, `regex` | should | warp, iterm2 | Grep in place without rerunning. A view-layer mask keeps pins valid. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-29 | Instant replay | Time-indexed screen history (default about 4 MB), scrubbed with keys, with timestamps. | Action `replay.toggle`; `replay { size-mb }` | could | iterm2 | Recovers cleared or overwritten TUI screens, but one terminal and a second history store. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-30 | Annotations | Notes attached to pin ranges via UI, rule or OSC. Navigable and toggleable. Content is never logged. | Action `annotate`; OSC 1337 AddAnnotation | could | iterm2 | Cheap on top of -02. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-31 | Export, capture and pipe scrollback | Actions that take an extent (screen, scrollback, selection, last-command output, block) and a destination (file, clipboard, pager, editor, command stdin, control-socket reply). Includes a pipe-to-command Action and a capture call on the socket. Plain text or text with SGR. | Actions `export`, `capture`, `pipe`, `edit-scrollback`; args `extent`, `to`; CLI `apo capture`; config `pager`, `editor` | must | tmux, zellij, kitty, ghostty, windows-terminal, alacritty | The standard escape hatch for reuse and automation, and one Action replaces many per-terminal commands. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-32 | Clear scopes | Clear screen, scrollback or all, as one Action with a scope argument. | Action `clear` arg `scope` | should | windows-terminal, tmux, kitty | Cheap automation. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-33 | Optional viewport and scrollback serialization | Saved layouts may store pane text up to a line cap, shown on resurrection. Default off. File is 0600 and compressed, and password-role and redaction rules apply. | `layout { serialize-viewport; serialize-lines }` | should | zellij | Continuity after restore, but it stores possibly sensitive text. |
| SCROLLBACKSEARCH-34 | Persistent markers and hover patterns | User-defined regex or text patterns highlighted in the grid (kitty create_marker) or matched on hover with a cursor name (VTE). Implemented on the -10 engine. | Action `marker.add`, `marker.clear`; rules `match { pattern } -> actions` | should | kitty, vte | Reuses the search engine. The rules vocabulary replaces a scripting hook. |

### Open questions

- Is block-aware eviction (-26) acceptable when one huge block exceeds the byte limit? Needs a rule for splitting.
- Does the logical limit (-03) include compressed or disk-tier pages, and what is the default? The rows only give ghostty's 50 MB, which is unverified for our target.
- Pure-Rust LZ4 and AEAD crate choice and licence check (-04, -05). Unverified.
- Reflow policy for image placements (-22, -24) is not settled by the rows.
- Whether search runs over the disk tier by default or only on request (-05, -12).
- Whether search-in-cells (-10) or a text haystack (-09) is the single engine for literal search. Rows disagree (alacritty cells, wezterm text). Leaning to cell-byte stepping with the offset map.
- Exact defaults for debounce (-14), highlight margin (-16) and replay size (-29) need benchmarking.


## shell-integration

Merged from the per-terminal survey rows. Sources are cited per row in the survey files; the primary ones are the kitty shell-integration docs (https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/shell-integration/), the iTerm2 escape-code docs (https://iterm2.com/documentation-escape-codes.html), Ghostty `src/termio/shell_integration.zig`, foot `osc.c` / `terminal.h`, Windows Terminal `adaptDispatch.cpp` and `Marks.hpp`, VTE `termpropsregistry.cc`, and Contour `docs/drafts/semantic-blocks.md`.

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SHELLINTEGRATION-01 | OSC 133 A/B/C/D semantic prompt marks | Shell hooks emit A (prompt start), B (input start), C (output start, optionally with command line) and D (end, with exit status). Marks are stored as row anchors, not recomputed from cells (see -03). Fires command-started and command-finished events. | `OSC 133;A\|B\|C\|D[;status]`; KDL `shell-integration { enabled }`; events `command-started`, `command-finished` | must | kitty, wezterm, ghostty, foot, rio, iterm2, windows-terminal, vte-konsole, warp-wave, topic-escape-registry | Every mature terminal ships it. It is the foundation for navigation, output selection, notifications and rules. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-02 | Lenient OSC 133 transition rules | State machine tolerates sloppy shells: A discards a prompt that never ran, D with no running record is ignored, a new A or nested shell implicitly closes an open C, N/I/P(k=r)/L handled, PS1 redraw does not force a fresh line. | none (internal); conformance tests | must | topic-scrollback-search (contour, foot), ghostty | Real shell scripts emit duplicate, missing and out-of-order marks. Strict parsing corrupts the block list. Contour's table is the most complete. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-03 | Block store with per-row block id | One u32 block id per row (stamped once, carried through reflow). A separate store of up to 1000 command records (state, command line, exit code, start/end times, cwd) that outlives the rows. Derived extents (prompt/input/output) computed on demand from the record. | Action `block.*` family; control-socket `blocks.list`, `blocks.get`; setting `blocks.max` | must | topic-scrollback-search (contour), windows-terminal, ghostty, foot, warp-wave | O(1) row to block lookup, survives reflow and scrollback eviction, and backs pickers, folding and agent perception. Preferred over wezterm's per-query zone scan (-12) and ghostty/foot's per-row flags alone. Row flags remain as the cheap marker. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-04 | Per-cell semantic content (input vs output) | 2-bit per cell (output/input/prompt) set from the current OSC 133 phase, alongside the row flag, so input and output can be separated inside one row. | internal; used by select-output and click-to-move | should | ghostty, wezterm (zones), topic-scrollback-search | Needed for exact output selection and click-to-move on multi-line prompts. Costs 2 bits per cell. Can be deferred behind -03. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-05 | kitty OSC 133 extensions | Accept A options `redraw=0`, `special_key=1`, `k=s`, `click_events=1\|2`, `cl=`; C options `cmdline=` (shell-quoted) and `cmdline_url=`; P with kind for continuation prompts. Command line is held in memory in the block record only. | `OSC 133` option keys (interface facts) | should | kitty, ghostty, topic-scrollback-search | Ghostty adopted the kitty options, so scripts from both work. `redraw=0` also feeds prompt protection (-10). Command line capture is needed for re-run and rules. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-06 | Mark dialect compatibility | OSC 633 (VS Code), OSC 1337 SetMark and ConEmu 9;12 all map onto the same mark set. D with non-zero status marks the block as failed. | `OSC 633;A\|B\|C\|D\|E`, `OSC 1337;SetMark`, `OSC 9;12` | should | windows-terminal, iterm2, vte-konsole | Windows Terminal unifies them; costs only parser branches and lets existing VS Code and iTerm scripts work unchanged. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-07 | Prompt navigation and scrollbar marks | Actions jump to previous/next/first/last prompt with the prompt placed at viewport top. Marks drawn as pips on the glass scrollbar, coloured by category (success, error, running). Exit-status colour in the gutter. | Action `block.jump` (previous/next/first/last, with count); setting `scrollbar.marks`; KDL `shell-integration { prompt-nav }` | must | kitty, wezterm, ghostty, foot, rio, iterm2, windows-terminal, vte-konsole | Primary visible value of shell integration; every mature terminal has prompt jumping. Pips and exit colours come from Windows Terminal and iTerm2. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-08 | Output and command actions | Select, copy, pipe or open in a pager the output of the last or Nth command; select the command line; fold output; sticky header of the current command. | Actions `block.select-output`, `block.select-command`, `block.copy-output`, `block.pipe-output <cmd>`, `block.show-in-pager`, `block.fold` | must | kitty, wezterm, foot, iterm2, windows-terminal, vte-konsole, topic-scrollback-search | Selecting and piping output is everyday use and cheap once -03 exists. Folding and sticky header (Contour) can follow; mark as should within this row if scoped down. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-09 | Command-finished event and notifications | `command-finished` event carries exit status, duration, cwd and command line. Rules can notify, flash the tab, or play a sound when a command longer than a threshold ends in an unfocused tab. | Rule trigger `command-finished` with conditions `exit`, `duration`, `focused`; setting `notifications.min-duration` | must | kitty, ghostty, topic-scrollback-search (contour), topic-escape-registry | Delivers the user-visible payoff of -01 and is the first rule-engine event for shell state. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-10 | Prompt protection during reflow | On resize, rows the shell will redraw (the live prompt) are excluded from rewrap or reflowed as blank rows, so the shell's cursor-up count stays valid. Honours `redraw=0`. | internal; setting `reflow.protect-prompt` | must | kitty, ghostty (via topic-scrollback-search) | Prevents garbled prompts after resize. Both implementations arrived at the same fix independently. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-11 | Click-to-move cursor in prompt | Clicking inside the input zone sends arrow-key (or shell-handled click events when `click_events` negotiated) to move the cursor. Up/Down within multi-line input optional. | KDL `shell-integration { click-to-move }`; Action `prompt.click-move` | should | kitty, ghostty, vte-konsole | Three mature terminals ship it. Needs -04 for correctness on multi-line input. Default on only when the shell declares support. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-12 | Zone derivation by cell scan | Do not adopt. Walking every physical line and splitting on semantic type changes is O(history) per query. | none | no | wezterm | The block store (-03) answers the same queries in O(1). |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-13 | OSC 7 current working directory | Parse `file://host/path`, percent-decode, accept only empty or local host (spoofing guard). Also ConEmu `OSC 9;9`. Feeds new-tab-here, split-here, spawn in same directory, and `-D`/`--working-directory` accepting file URIs. | `OSC 7`, `OSC 9;9`; Actions `tab.new --cwd=inherit`, `split.* --cwd=inherit`; CLI `--working-directory` | must | foot, rio, kitty, iterm2, vte-konsole, topic-escape-registry | Expected behaviour everywhere. Host check from rio and VTE is the safer rule. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-14 | iTerm2 OSC 1337 cwd/host/version | Accept `CurrentDir=`, `RemoteHost=user@host`, `ShellIntegrationVersion`; `SetMark` handled by -06. Remote host shown as tab context. | `OSC 1337;CurrentDir=`, `RemoteHost=`, `ShellIntegrationVersion=` | should | iterm2, wezterm, windows-terminal | iTerm's scripts remain in wide use. Parser-only cost. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-15 | OSC 8 hyperlinks | Hyperlink id/URI stored per cell in an extras table (not in the base cell). Explicit hyperlinks take precedence over regex matches; unique visible links enumerable for hint mode. | `OSC 8`; Action `hints.open --kind=hyperlink`; setting `links.explicit-precedence` | must | alacritty, foot, kitty, wezterm, ghostty, vte-konsole | Baseline feature of every modern terminal. Precedence rule from Alacritty is sound. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-16 | OSC 1337 SetUserVar | Base64-decoded named variables stored per pane. Each change fires `user-var-changed`, and values are match fields (`var:x`), template inputs and queryable. | `OSC 1337;SetUserVar=name=base64`; rule trigger `user-var`; condition `user_var`; CLI `ec-term get-user-vars` | must | wezterm, rio, topic-config-automation (kitty match fields) | Primary no-scripting channel for editors and shells to signal the terminal; drives editor-aware bindings and status content. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-17 | OSC 9;4 progress | Progress state (none, normal, error, indeterminate, paused) stored per pane, drawn as a bar on the glass tab with a timeout (rio uses 15 s) so a crashed program cannot leave it stuck. Also feeds Abyss taskbar progress. | `OSC 9;4;state;value`; setting `progress.timeout` | should | rio, windows-terminal, ghostty (unverified) | Cheap and a good fit for glass tabs. Timeout is the key safety detail. Only rio confirmed in rows. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-18 | Termprops typed property registry (OSC 666) | Registry of typed properties with well-known names, an app namespace, type checks and a blocklist against spoofing; changes batched into events. Could unify -13/-14/-16/-17 internally. | `OSC 666`; rules on termprop events; CLI query | could | vte-konsole | Clean design but only VTE ships it and few programs emit it. Revisit once -16 exists, and consider using the typed-registry shape internally. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-19 | OSC 3008 context signalling | `start=ID;type=...;cwd=...;hostname=...` and `end=ID;exit=...` mark shells, containers, ssh sessions. Used for tab badges and spawning panes in the same container. | `OSC 3008`; setting `tabs.show-context` | could | vte-konsole, topic-escape-registry | Useful for the ssh and container story; spec details unverified, and only VTE/Konsole ship it. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-20 | Shell integration injection without rc edits | Detect shell by basename. zsh: temporary `ZDOTDIR` shim that restores the user's value. fish/elvish/nushell: prepend bundled dir to `XDG_DATA_DIRS` and strip it later. bash: `--posix` plus `ENV` loader that restores rcfile/profile/HISTFILE. PowerShell: encoded command. Feature list passed in one env var and unset by the script. Skipped for explicit `-e` commands. Scripts written once per version to cache. | KDL `shell-integration { mode "detect\|none\|bash\|zsh\|fish" ; features { cursor; title; cwd; prompt-mark; sudo; ssh-env; ssh-terminfo } }`; settings GUI per-feature toggles | must | kitty, ghostty, rio, iterm2 | Zero-touch install with per-feature opt-out maps directly onto the settings GUI. Kitty and Ghostty share the technique; Ghostty's bash handling is more complete than rio's. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-21 | Terminfo on remote hosts | `ssh-env` fallback: downgrade `TERM` to `xterm-256color` and forward `COLORTERM`/`TERM_PROGRAM`. `ssh-terminfo`: push compiled terminfo (and text source) to the remote `~/.terminfo`, compile with `tic` if present, with a local cache of hosts already done. Opt-in per host with a consent prompt. | features `ssh-env`, `ssh-terminfo`; CLI `ec-term ssh-cache list\|clear`; rule action `ssh.install-terminfo` | must | kitty, ghostty | A custom TERM breaks colours and keys on hosts that lack the entry. Combine kitty's push with Ghostty's ssh-env fallback and cache. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-22 | Shell integration over ssh and subshells | Remote bootstrap extracts integration scripts to a temp dir, sets env, execs the login shell and cleans up on exit. Also an opt-in snippet and OSC 133 detection for nested shells. Must be transparent and opt-in. | profile `shell-integration auto\|manual`; Action `ssh.install-integration` | should | kitty, iterm2, warp-wave (details unverified; docs 404) | Needed so marks and cwd survive ssh. Three terminals offer some form, but the mechanism is intrusive; consent-gated. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-23 | sudo terminfo wrapper | Shell function/alias that preserves the terminfo location through `sudo`/`run0`. | feature `sudo` | should | kitty, ghostty | Two terminals ship it; trivial once -20 exists. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-24 | clone-in-terminal and edit-in-terminal | Shell functions ask the terminal over the control socket to open a window cloning env/cwd/venv, or to edit a remote file locally. | CLI `ec-term clone`, `ec-term edit`; Abyss consent prompt | could | kitty | Good UX, kitty only. Build on the control socket, gated by consent on Abyss. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-25 | Trigger-based integration fallback | Rules with prompt regexes stand in for scripts on hosts where nothing can be installed: report user/host, report directory, prompt detected. | rule actions `report-dir`, `report-host`, `prompt-detected` | could | iterm2 | Falls out of the rule engine at no extra mechanism. Regex-based and fragile, so low priority. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-26 | Captured output panel | A rule action collects lines matching a regex into a panel tied to the command; click scrolls to and flashes the line. | rule action `capture-output`; `OSC 1337;ClearCapturedOutput` | could | iterm2 | Useful for compiler errors. Single source. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-27 | Command history from blocks | Per-session (and optionally per-host) history picker built from the block store. In-memory by default; persistence off because human input is never logged by content on EclipseOS. | setting `history.scope=session\|host\|off`; palette command | could | iterm2, warp-wave | Overlaps shell history. Privacy invariant forbids persisting command content by default. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-28 | Shell-driven completions and quick fix | Shell sends a JSON completion list (OSC 633 Completions) or an unresolved-command report (OSC 9001 CmdNotFound); terminal shows a glass suggestion popup. | `OSC 633;Completions`, `OSC 9001` | could | windows-terminal | Windows-only ecosystem for now. Revisit when there is a glass popup primitive. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-29 | Program-declared resurrection command | A program declares its own relaunch command through an OSC and an Action; session restore uses it in preference to process-tree discovery. | Action `pane.set-resurrection-command`; CLI | could | tmux-zellij | Better than guessing for editors and TUIs, but zellij only and needs a new OSC. |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-30 | OSC 7 primary, /proc fallback for cwd | Use terminal-reported OSC 7 URL first; divine from /proc only when absent, with stale-allowed cache. | IPC cwd_source osc7\|proc | must | wezterm | Works across ssh/containers where /proc is wrong; wezterm order. Source: wezterm mux/src/localpane.rs#get_current_working_dir. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| SHELLINTEGRATION-31 | Set TERM_PROGRAM on nested-aware basis | wezterm/ghostty set TERM_PROGRAM; foot deletes foreign ones so nesting is not mis-detected; Apotheosis sets own and deletes foreign. | terminal.term-program | should | foot, wezterm | Shell integration scripts key on TERM_PROGRAM. Source: wezterm config.rs; foot slave.c. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |

## Open questions

- Do we register our own OSC for program-declared state (resurrection command, -29), or ride on user vars (-16) or termprops (-18)? Rows give no answer.
- Is OSC 9;4 (-17) also emitted by Ghostty? The row names only rio and Windows Terminal; unverified.
- Warp's "Warpify" mechanism (-22) could not be verified (docs 404); the ssh push design relies on kitty's bootstrap only.
- OSC 3008 (-19) spec status and exact field set are unverified against systemd.io.
- Is the per-cell semantic bit (-04) worth its memory cost, or can the block store plus row flags give exact input/output selection? Needs a prototype.
- How is consent for ssh terminfo push and clone/edit (-21, -22, -24) presented on non-Abyss compositors, where there is no compositor prompt?
- Should command-line capture (`cmdline=`, -05) be kept only for the live session, given the "no human input logged by content" invariant? Draft stance: in memory, never written to disk or logs.


## navigation

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NAVIGATION-01 | Hint mode: labelled on-screen matchers | Overlay snapshots the viewport, joins wrapped rows into logical lines with a byte-to-cell map, runs each enabled matcher (built-in url/path/file:line/hash/ip/word, OSC 8 ranges, user regex), de-overlaps results, labels them, filters by typed prefix, then runs the matcher's Action list on the pick. Multi-select with a joiner. Named regex groups become action arguments. User matchers are KDL data, no scripting. | KDL `hint "name" { regex; action ...; persist; multiple }`; Actions `hints.open`, `hints.copy`, `hints.paste`, `hints.select`, `hints.run`; mouse/key bindings; CLI `ec-term hint <name>` | must | kitty, wezterm, foot, alacritty, rio, ghostty (requested) | Five mature terminals ship it and it is the headline keyboard feature. Foot's logical-line join is adopted so wrapped URLs match; wezterm's `then-action` and alacritty's action list are both subsumed by the shared Action vocabulary. Matchers in KDL replace kitty's Python and wezterm's Lua. |
| NAVIGATION-02 | Prefix-free label allocation | Minimal-length labels from a configurable alphabet, such that no label is a prefix of another. Longer labels only when matches exceed the alphabet size (breadth-first expansion, as in foot; equivalent to wezterm's compute_labels_for_alphabet). Labels are drawn nearest-to-cursor first. Dedupe identical match text onto one label. | KDL `hints { alphabet "asdfghjkl"; label-style ... }` | must | kitty, wezterm, foot, alacritty | Wezterm and foot both produce minimal prefix-free sets; alacritty's incrementing counter with split ratio is less compact. Pick the BFS/minimal form: single keystroke for the common case, no ambiguity at commit time. |
| NAVIGATION-03 | Match post-processing and revalidation | After a regex match, trim trailing punctuation and unbalanced brackets, re-checking the trimmed span still matches (alacritty HintPostProcessor technique). At activation, re-run the matcher at the stored start cell so a match that scrolled or changed is rejected rather than used. | KDL `hint { trim "default"; revalidate #true }` (defaults on) | should | alacritty, foot (url trim) | Cheap correctness: avoids pasting wrong text or `url).`. Only alacritty documents both; defaults on because there is no reason to disable. |
| NAVIGATION-04 | Default hint set and safe open routing | Ship built-in matchers for URL (common schemes), absolute/relative paths with optional line:col, git hashes, IPs, UUIDs. Default URL hint bound to a key and ctrl-click. Open goes through the Action `open` which uses the portal on Abyss (consent prompt) and falls back to xdg-open on other compositors. | Built-ins named `url`, `path`, `hash`, `ip`; binding `ctrl+shift+o` default; `open` Action | should | alacritty, kitty, foot, rio, konsole | Every terminal that ships hints ships a URL default. Routing through consent matches the EclipseOS no-ambient-authority stance. The path regex must not need lookbehind (rio needs Oniguruma for that); use a Rust regex engine with hand-written boundary checks. |
| NAVIGATION-05 | OSC 8 hyperlinks | Parse `OSC 8;params;URI ST`; empty URI closes. The `id` param joins non-contiguous cells into one link for hover and hint. Hover shows the URL in the status area. Click or hint opens only through an Action, with a scheme allow-list (default http, https, mailto, file with host check, ssh). Links participate in hint mode as a matcher. | KDL `hyperlinks { schemes ...; modifier "ctrl"; hover-style }`; Action `open` | must | Escape-registry spec; kitty, foot, wezterm, iterm2, konsole | The universal link mechanism for ls, gcc, ripgrep. The allow-list is the security boundary, since the URI is attacker-controlled. Spec: https://gist.github.com/egmontkob/eb114294efbcd5adb1944c9f3cb5feda |
| NAVIGATION-06 | Semantic history and hotspot filter chain (file:line) | A filter chain over the displayed text produces hotspots with tooltip and Actions: URL, existing-file (resolve relative path against the pane cwd, stat before offering, accept `:line:col` and `(line,col)`), colour, and user regex. Click resolves to an editor command template with `{path}`, `{line}`, `{col}` placeholders. OSC 8 `file://...#line:col` handled the same way. | KDL `editor { command "..." }`; `hotspots { modifier "ctrl" }`; Action `open-path` | must | iterm2, konsole, kitty, wezterm | Daily productivity feature. Only offering paths that exist avoids false underlines (konsole FileFilter technique). The template is typed placeholders, not a shell string, to avoid injection. |
| NAVIGATION-07 | Smart selection with precision classes and context actions | Double-click or the Action `smart-select` tests rules containing the cursor cell; per rule take the longest match; highest precision class with any match wins, then score. Each rule carries an Action list shown in a context menu, with `\0..\9`, cwd, user, host substitution. | KDL `smart-select { rule "name" { regex; precision "high"; action ... } }`; Action `smart-select` | must | iterm2 (kitty, wezterm via hints/word rules) | iTerm2's ordering is deterministic and fully specified, so it is the one to copy. Context actions reuse the Action vocabulary rather than a second mechanism. Merged with iTerm2 actions-and-substitutions row. |
| NAVIGATION-08 | Copy (vi) mode as pure motion functions on a rebindable key table | Copy mode is a cursor point plus a motion function `(grid, motion) -> point`; viewport follows. Motions: char/line, first/last, High/Middle/Low, word and WORD with End variants, semantic word, bracket match, paragraph, top/bottom, page. Selection kinds toggled inside the mode. Keys live in a named key table of Actions, so every motion is bindable and shown in the settings GUI. | KDL `keytable "copy-mode" { bind ... }`; Actions `copy-mode.enter`, `cursor.move motion=...`, `selection.toggle kind=...`; JSON control socket exposes the same | must | alacritty, wezterm, tmux, rio, contour | Pure functions are unit-testable (alacritty vi_mode.rs) and the table form is what wezterm and tmux ship. Matches the shared-Action and settings-GUI rule. |
| NAVIGATION-09 | Vi text objects including command blocks | `i`/`a` objects for word, WORD, paragraph, brackets, quotes; `im`/`am` for a command block or output (needs shell integration). Applied as a selection extend in copy mode. | Actions `select.object kind=... inner=bool` in copy-mode keytable | should | contour (tmux partial) | Single-terminal for blocks, but word/paragraph/quote objects are vi baseline. Block objects are cheap once prompt marks exist (NAVIGATION-14). |
| NAVIGATION-10 | f/t character jumps, repeat, and marks | `f/F/t/T` jump within line, `;` and `,` repeat and reverse; named marks set and jumped to in copy mode. | Actions `cursor.jump-char`, `cursor.repeat-jump`, `mark.set`, `mark.goto` | should | wezterm, tmux, alacritty (inline search) | Source row said could; two or more mature terminals ship it so raised to should per rule. Costs little given NAVIGATION-08. |
| NAVIGATION-11 | Search primitives: semantic, bracket, inline, line | Shared grid helpers: semantic word boundary with configurable separator characters, matching bracket, inline f/t search, line start/end. Used by double-click, copy mode and smart selection. | KDL `selection { word-chars "..." }` | should | alacritty, foot, konsole, wezterm | Building blocks that three features depend on. Configurable separators exist in alacritty, foot and konsole. |
| NAVIGATION-12 | Selection types with sided anchors | Simple, block, semantic (word) and line selections. Anchors are grid points with a Left/Right cell side so half-cell boundaries are exact, and they move with scrollback rotation. Mouse: double/triple click, modifier for block, right-click expand. Also drivable from copy mode and hints. | KDL `selection { block-modifier "alt"; save-to-primary #true }`; Actions `selection.start kind=`, `selection.expand` | must | alacritty, kitty, foot, wezterm, ghostty | Every terminal needs it; sided anchors fix off-by-one half-cell selection and survive output (alacritty selection.rs). |
| NAVIGATION-13 | Script-aware word selection modes | Optional word-boundary rule sets (ASCII, CJK, Brahmic, coding) and a custom word-character set; triple-click mode choice. Uses Unicode segmentation (UAX 29) as the base. | KDL `selection { word-mode "unicode"; word-chars "..." ; triple-click "line" }` | could | konsole | Only konsole ships mode variants; custom word-chars is already covered by NAVIGATION-11. Also feeds accessibility word granularity. |
| NAVIGATION-14 | Prompt mark navigation | Using OSC 133 (and OSC 1337 marks) record prompt rows; Actions jump to previous/next prompt with a count, scroll prompt to top. Optional alert on next mark for long-running commands. Marks are stored as stable scrollback rows. | Actions `prompt.prev`, `prompt.next count=`, `prompt.alert-on-next`; default keys shift+ctrl+up/down | must | iterm2, ghostty, kitty, foot, tmux, windows-terminal, wezterm | Six or more terminals ship it; core shell-integration benefit. |
| NAVIGATION-15 | Block navigation and jump to output start | Command blocks (prompt, command, output) from OSC 133 become selectable units; Actions move selection block to block and scroll to the start of output; copy block, copy output. | Actions `block.prev`, `block.next`, `block.goto-output-start`, `block.copy-output` | must | warp, kitty (last command output), wezterm (semantic zones), ghostty | Replaces scroll hunting. Built from the same marks as NAVIGATION-14, so low incremental cost. Warp's Wave-style UI is not copied, only the navigation actions. |
| NAVIGATION-16 | Bookmarks | Toggle a bookmark on a block or row; Actions jump to previous/next bookmark; hover preview. Persisted with scrollback persistence rather than session-only. | Actions `bookmark.toggle`, `bookmark.prev`, `bookmark.next` | should | warp, iterm2 (marks), tmux (marks) | Cheap; iTerm2 marks and tmux marks give two mature precedents. Persistence is the improvement over warp. |
| NAVIGATION-17 | Single command palette with modes | One glass overlay with a mode enum (actions, commandline, tab switch, tab search, workspace). A `>` prefix, or backspace on an empty field, switches to commandline mode. Unified search merges actions, history, workflows, saved layouts. Opened with a launch-mode argument. | Action `palette.open mode=`; KDL `palette { default-mode }` | must | windows-terminal, wezterm, warp, kitty | Central to the glass UI. Windows Terminal's mode switching and warp's unified search both converge on one overlay. |
| NAVIGATION-18 | Composable palette sources, including dynamic ones | Palette fed by a flag-composed list of sources: actions, keybindings, launch menu items, tabs, workspaces, domains, profiles, history, saved layouts, ssh hosts (from ssh config), recent dirs, resurrectable sessions. Sources are declared in KDL, not code. | KDL `palette { sources "actions" "tabs" "ssh-hosts" ... }`; Action `palette.open sources=` | must | wezterm, windows-terminal, warp, tmux-zellij | wezterm's ShowLauncherArgs is the best model for flag-composed sources; extending with declarative dynamic sources removes the need for Lua. |
| NAVIGATION-19 | fzf-style fuzzy scoring with match runs | Port the fzf V2 scoring algorithm to Rust: match 16, gap start -3, gap extension -1, boundary and non-word bonus 8, camelCase bonus, consecutive bonus, first-character multiplier 2, Unicode case folding, multi-term patterns. Return matched runs for highlighting; sort by score then name; score subtitle too. | KDL `palette { smart-case #true }` | must | windows-terminal (vendored fzf), wezterm | Proven ranking, deterministic, and test vectors exist from upstream docs. Implement in Rust from the algorithm description, not from the C++ source. |
| NAVIGATION-20 | Synthesised action names and template-expanded entries | Any Action plus arguments yields a generated human name when none is given. A palette entry may declare an `iterate-on` source (profiles, schemes, layouts) and expand one entry per item with typed placeholders such as `{profile.name}`; nested submenus supported. | KDL `command "name" { iterate-on "profiles"; action ... }` | should | windows-terminal | Makes every Action discoverable with zero config and generates per-profile entries. Typed placeholders, not string interpolation of shell. Single source (Windows Terminal spec #2046) but low risk. |
| NAVIGATION-21 | Tab switcher with MRU and commit on key release | Overlay lists tabs in most-recently-used order; cycling key held with anchor modifier; commits on modifier release. | Action `tab.switcher mode="mru"`; KDL `tabs { switcher-order "mru" }` | should | windows-terminal, kitty (goto_tab history), wezterm | Standard desktop expectation (alt-tab). Part of NAVIGATION-17 in the source row, separated because it has its own timing rule. |
| NAVIGATION-22 | Recent directories by frecency | Directories gathered from OSC 7 / cwd reports ranked by frequency and recency; user can pin (star). Palette source and Action to open a tab or split there. Per-profile privacy toggle and clear. | Palette source `recent-dirs`; Action `open-recent-dir`; `history { clear }` | should | iterm2, wezterm (cwd in launcher), zoxide-style tools | Feeds cd and new-tab-in-dir. Single-terminal as a feature but the data comes free with OSC 7, so ship it. Privacy control needed. |
| NAVIGATION-23 | Pickers for sessions, tabs, panes | Tree/preview pickers as palette views: session tree, window/tab/pane chooser with live preview, layout manager, file picker. Mode-switch Action toggles between tab and session scope. | Palette views; Action `switch-mode` | should | tmux (choose-tree, choose-client, switch-mode), zellij (session-manager, layout-manager, strider) | Fast switching is the daily UX of a mux. Implement as palette views over the same sources, not separate plugins. |
| NAVIGATION-24 | Workspaces as named window groups | Windows carry a workspace name; switching shows one workspace and hides others; rename and relative-switch Actions; palette source. | Actions `workspace.switch name=`, `workspace.next`, `workspace.rename`; KDL `workspaces` | should | wezterm, zellij (sessions), tmux (sessions) | Project-switching layer above tabs, shipped in three muxers. |
| NAVIGATION-25 | Resurrectable session list | Saved sessions listed with time since they ended; pick to revive or delete. Delete is a first-class privacy action. | Palette source `saved-sessions`; Actions `session.restore`, `session.delete` | should | zellij | Only zellij ships it, but session persistence is already planned and this is the entry point. Kept at should for coherence with NAVIGATION-24. |
| NAVIGATION-26 | Undo close | Closed tab, window or split restorable within `undo-timeout` (default 5 s). Pane processes are kept alive but hidden until the timeout expires; scrollback retained. | Actions `undo`, `redo`; KDL `undo-timeout` | should | ghostty (macOS only), iterm2, browsers (model) | Prevents data loss. Ghostty's is macOS-only upstream, so keeping the process alive is our extension. Needs a process-lifetime design before it is safe to ship. |
| NAVIGATION-27 | Search seeded from selection; match becomes selection | Action starts search with the current selection as the query; confirming search turns the current match into a selection so copy works immediately. | Actions `search.from-selection`, `search.select-match` | should | ghostty, foot | Smooth copy workflow; two ship it. |
| NAVIGATION-28 | Foreground process detection via tcgetpgrp | tcgetpgrp(master) gives fg pgid; fall back to shell pid; compare to shell pgid to know job is running. | IPC pane.info fg_pgid/fg_pid/exe; event pane.foreground-changed | must | alacritty, kitty | Busy-close confirmation, tab titles, same-cwd spawn. Source: alacritty daemon.rs#foreground_process_path; kitty child.py#foreground_processes. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| NAVIGATION-29 | cwd/exe/cmdline/environ via /proc/<pid> | readlink /proc/pid/cwd, /exe; read /cmdline (NUL-separated), /environ (same-uid). | IPC pane.info cwd/exe/cmdline; actions copy-foreground-cwd | must | kitty, wezterm | No libproc needed on Linux; pure std fs. Source: wezterm procinfo/src/linux.rs; kitty child.py. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| NAVIGATION-30 | Foreground heuristic: highest pid in group, oldest option | When fg group has several processes (script running vim) pick max pid as newest; min pid optional. | spawn-pane cwd=foreground\|root | should | kitty | Correct cwd for scripts launching editors. Source: kitty child.py#get_pid_for_cwd. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| NAVIGATION-31 | Process tree and pgrp map from /proc with TTL cache | Scan /proc/*/stat building pgrp->pids map; cache 1 s scoped via start/stop-caching around one action; use task/*/children when available. | IPC pane.processes | should | kitty, wezterm | Avoid O(procs) scans per query. Source: kitty child.py#process_group_map,CachedProcessData; wezterm procinfo linux.rs#with_root_pid. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| NAVIGATION-32 | Robust /proc/pid/stat parsing from last ')' | comm may contain spaces/parens; split after the final ')'. kitty's fixed space split can mis-parse. | none | must | kitty, wezterm | Correctness for odd process names. Source: wezterm procinfo/src/linux.rs#LinuxStat; kitty child.py#process_group_map. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| NAVIGATION-33 | xdg-toplevel-drag for dragging tabs out into windows | Attach dragged toplevel to a data-device drag so a tab can become a new window under the pointer. | none | could | design | Browser-like tab tearing for CSD tabs; compositor support not verified. Source: xdg-toplevel-drag-v1.xml. (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |

### Open questions

- Hint regex engine: which crates.io engine gives lookbehind-free paths that are fast enough over the viewport, and is a hand-written boundary check enough for URL/path (NAVIGATION-04)?
- Undo close (NAVIGATION-26): how long may a closed pane's process live hidden, and does it interact with the sandbox and resource limits?
- Whether block navigation (NAVIGATION-15) is available only with shell integration or also via heuristic prompt detection; the rows do not settle a fallback.
- Settings GUI representation of the copy-mode key table (NAVIGATION-08): the rows say bindable, not how the GUI shows chords across modes.
- Frecency weighting constants (NAVIGATION-22) were not given; iTerm2 documentation is unspecific. Unverified.
- Whether palette history and recent-dir data are persisted, and the retention default (privacy).
- Konsole script-aware word modes (NAVIGATION-13): whether UAX 29 segmentation alone is sufficient; unverified.


## config

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CONFIG-01 | Single option schema table | One Rust table (key, type, default, constraints, scope, doc text, widget) generates the KDL parser, validation, settings GUI pages, docs, `config show` and the socket's get/set. Doc text lives in the table. | schema table; `apo config show`; settings GUI | must | kitty (options/definition.py), ghostty (Config.zig doc comments, helpgen.zig), windows-terminal (MTSMSettings.h), tmux (options-table.c), wave (counter-example), topic-config-automation | Only way to guarantee "every setting in GUI and KDL" with no drift. Wave's conn:enablewsh vs conn:wshenabled mismatch is the failure case. Data table beats derive-macro-only because the GUI and docs need the same metadata at runtime. |
| CONFIG-02 | Cascading layers with optional-per-layer values and scopes | Every setting optional per layer; getter walks parents. Order: built-in defaults < Abyss/system defaults < user file < includes/profile fragments < `profiles.defaults` < profile < CLI `-o` < runtime override. Option scope (global/window/tab/pane) is declared in the schema. | `profiles.defaults`, per-profile keys; schema scope field | must | windows-terminal (IInheritable.h, FinalizeLayering), tmux (options.c parent chain), zellij (options.rs#merge), topic-config-automation | Sparse files and inherited/overridden indicators need a real layer model. tmux scopes give per-pane settings without a second mechanism. |
| CONFIG-03 | Config introspection CLI | Subcommands over the same parser: `validate` (non-zero exit on error, line-numbered), `show` (effective merged, with layer of origin), `explain <key>`, list actions/keybinds/themes. | `apo config validate/show/explain`, `apo actions list` | should | ghostty (+validate/+show/+explain-config), foot (-C, exit 230), topic-config-automation | CI for dotfiles and layering debugging. Origin-layer output is our addition. List verbs for ghostty are unverified. |
| CONFIG-04 | Published KDL schema for editors | Emit a KDL schema/completion file from the option table at build time. | generated file | could | windows-terminal (settings JSON schema) | Free once CONFIG-01 exists. |
| CONFIG-05 | Keybind grammar | Trigger = modifiers (any order) + one key. Key may be a produced character, a physical key name (explicit `physical:` prefix, physical wins on conflict), or `catch_all` (modified tried before bare). Multi-key sequences `ctrl+a>b`, `combine` for action lists, `mouse_map` (button + mode). Match mode (keysym / keycode / base-layout symbol) selectable so shortcuts work on non-Latin layouts. All bind the shared Action vocabulary. | `bind` nodes; `mouse-bind`; `keyboard.layout-independent-binds` | must | kitty (map, combine, mouse_map), ghostty (Binding.zig Trigger), wezterm (keys docs), topic-keyboard | Combines kitty's sequences/combine/mouse grammar with ghostty's explicit physical-vs-unicode rule. An explicit prefix is clearer than guessing. |
| CONFIG-06 | Per-profile/rule keyboard overrides | Profiles and rules can swap bind sets and switch kitty/modifyOtherKeys modes. | `profile { keyboard { } }`, rule actions | should | topic-keyboard (internal design) | Legacy-tool compatibility without a global compromise. Single-source row. |
| CONFIG-07 | Includes and imports | `include` with relative, glob and optional forms; later overrides earlier; recursion depth cap; each include has its own scope for errors. Theme files and Abyss system defaults arrive through the same mechanism. | `include`, `include-glob`, `include optional=true` | must | alacritty (config/mod.rs#load_imports), kitty (include/globinclude), foot (include=), topic-config-automation | Three mature terminals ship it. Kitty's glob/optional forms added for per-machine dotfiles. |
| CONFIG-08 | CLI `-o` overrides and launch-flag overrides | `-o path=value` and flags (command, cwd, title, app-id, hold) form an Overrides layer applied after config load and re-applied on every reload. | `-o key=value`, `-c`, launch flags | must | foot (-o), alacritty (cli.rs), rio (cli.rs Overrides::apply), kitty (-o) | Hot reload must not discard per-window launch intent (rio). |
| CONFIG-09 | Runtime overrides scoped to window/tab/pane | `Set{path,value,scope}` action stores data in a transient override layer on that scope; resettable (`Reset{path,scope}`, global reset); survives reload; shown in GUI as an override. Setting an identical value emits no change event (loop guard). | Action `set`/`reset`; `apo set`; socket `set` | must | wezterm (set_config_overrides), alacritty (msg config, --reset), kitty (set-* remote), topic-config-automation | Data overrides rather than wezterm's handler-returns-table avoid its documented reload loop. Alacritty's -1 for all windows maps to scope `all`. |
| CONFIG-10 | Runtime-only appearance actions | Named actions (toggle-opacity, set-font-size, set-colors, set-spacing) as sugar over CONFIG-09; never write the file. | actions; palette | should | ghostty (issue #5047), kitty (remote-control set-*) | Demonstrated demand in two terminals. Unifying them with Set keeps one mechanism. |
| CONFIG-11 | Config watcher | Watch parent directories of config and every include, canonicalise symlinks but remember base paths, short debounce, poll fallback, rebuild watch set when the include list changes. | automatic; `reload` action/SIGUSR1 | must | alacritty (config/monitor.rs#ConfigMonitor::new), rio (watcher.rs), wezterm (config/src/lib.rs#watch_path), kitty (SIGUSR1) | Handles atomic-rename editors and symlinked dotfiles. Parent-dir watching is the right technique. |
| CONFIG-12 | Atomic apply with keep-last-good and diagnostics | Parse into a fresh model, collect all errors (file, line, key), apply only if valid; per-field tolerance drops bad entries (bad binds) with a warning. Errors show as glass toast and in the settings GUI. | toast; settings GUI error list; `validate` | must | alacritty (event.rs ConfigReload, ui_config.rs#deserialize_bindings), ghostty (ErrorList.zig), wezterm (config_builder rejects unknown keys) | Never brick the terminal on a typo. Unknown keys are errors with did-you-mean, not silent. Per-field tolerance vs all-or-nothing: error-list collection, apply valid remainder only for non-structural fields; open question below. |
| CONFIG-13 | No live reload | Not adopted: parse-once, new windows only. | | no | foot | We hot-apply file and GUI edits. |
| CONFIG-14 | Comment-preserving KDL round-trip editing | GUI and `config set` edit a lossless KDL document model (comments, order, unknown nodes kept), changing only affected nodes. | settings GUI; `apo config set/get` | must | zellij (config_file_edit.rs), windows-terminal (issue #2933 complaints), topic-config-automation | File stays authoritative. Zellij shows the technique works with KDL. |
| CONFIG-15 | Whole-file rewrite on save | Not adopted. | | no | windows-terminal (WriteSettingsToDisk, inferred) | Loses comments and ordering. |
| CONFIG-16 | Atomic write plus self-write suppression | Write temp+rename; record content hash and mtime; watcher ignores events matching it; reload throttled. | internal | must | windows-terminal (_writeSettingsToDisk, ReloadSettingsThrottled) | Prevents GUI-to-watcher reload loops. |
| CONFIG-17 | Inheritance indicators and reset in GUI | Each control shows inherited-from layer, overridden state and a reset button; changed-only filter. | settings GUI | must | windows-terminal (cascading-settings spec), tmux (customize-mode) | Required for GUI/text parity with CONFIG-02. |
| CONFIG-18 | Settings browser for every option | GUI lists all options generated from CONFIG-01, searchable, changed-only filter; CLI `config get/set` equivalents. | settings GUI; `apo config get/set` | must | tmux (window-customize.c), zellij (configuration plugin) | Direct requirement. |
| CONFIG-19 | Schema version and migrations | `version` node; ordered migrators rename keys and flag rewrite; rename compatibility table so old keys warn and map; `apo config migrate` with dry-run. | `version`, `apo config migrate --dry-run` | should | alacritty (migrate/mod.rs), windows-terminal (FixupUserSettings), ghostty (compatibility table) | Three terminals do renames. Migrations go through CONFIG-14 so comments survive. |
| CONFIG-20 | Importers from other terminals | One-shot import of foot.ini and alacritty.toml (and kitty.conf if time) into KDL. | `apo config import <file>` | should | foot, alacritty (rows suggest) | Adoption path. Source rows say "required"; kept should since single-source. |
| CONFIG-21 | Light/dark theme pair | `theme light=X dark=Y` following portal colour-scheme and Abyss theme changes; CSI ? 996 n report and DECSET 2031 push; SIGUSR1/2 and switch/toggle actions kept; initial theme setting. Themes are fragments that may not set theme/includes. | `theme`, actions `theme-dark/light/toggle`; settings GUI | must | foot (colors-dark/light, ctlseqs), ghostty (theme pair), rio (adaptive-theme), topic-config-automation | Four terminals; portal setting is the Wayland source of truth. |
| CONFIG-22 | Theme gallery with live preview | GUI gallery applying a theme by writing one include or `theme` node; seeds derived from Abyss ec-theme. | settings GUI | could | kitty (themes kitten) | Nice-to-have atop CONFIG-21. |
| CONFIG-23 | Typed `when` conditions | `when` block over closed predicates (os, host, compositor, scheme, battery, output scale); no expressions. | `when` node | should | ghostty (conditional.zig), kitty (KITTY_OS), topic-config-automation | Common dotfile branching without scripting. |
| CONFIG-24 | Profiles with inheritance and override protection | Profile = property bag (shell, theme, fonts, binds, env, init scripts) with parent inheritance; per-session runtime tweaks tracked so reapplying a profile does not clobber them unless asked. | `profile` nodes; action `set-profile`; `apo profile list/apply` | must | vte-konsole (Profile.cpp, ShouldApplyProperty.cpp), windows-terminal, iterm2, topic-config-automation | Core requirement. Konsole's shouldApply idea is realised via CONFIG-09 override layer. |
| CONFIG-25 | Automatic profile switching | `match` on user, host (exact or wildcard), path wildcard, job; additive score (exact host 16, wildcard host 8, job 4, user 2, exact path 1) picks the best; per-pane profile stack (match existing pops above, no match empties); `sticky` keeps. | `profile { match user host path job sticky }`; rule event connect | must | iterm2 (automatic profile switching docs), topic-config-automation | Concrete tested scoring; sane revert semantics. |
| CONFIG-26 | Dynamic profile sources | Watched dir `profiles.d/*.kdl`, per-file error isolation (iTerm2's one bad file blocks all is rejected); optional generators for ssh hosts, distrobox, toolbox tagged with source. | `~/.config/apotheosis/profiles.d/`; `hidden`, `source` | should | iterm2 (dynamic profiles), windows-terminal (generators), topic-config-automation | Two mature terminals ship it. Generators are optional later work. |
| CONFIG-27 | Per-host/connection profiles | Host-glob blocks (first match, optional user@, no inheritance between blocks) selecting profile: shell integration, env, cwd, colours, font. | `profile { match host= }` | should | kitty (kittens/ssh), wave (connections) | Unifies with CONFIG-25. |
| CONFIG-28 | Declarative KDL layouts with templates | Layout files: tabs, nested panes, split direction, percent/fixed size, cwd, command, args, borderless, focus, name; `tab_template`/`pane_template` with `children` placeholder. | layouts dir; `new-tab --layout`, `dump-layout`; action `apply-layout` | must | zellij (kdl_layout_parser.rs, default.swap.kdl) | KDL schema already proven. Add dump-layout for capture. |
| CONFIG-29 | Typed format expressions | Small typed format language for titles, tab labels, status and rule conditions: variables, comparisons, conditionals, string ops. No scripting. GUI editor. | format keys; rule `if` | should | tmux (format.c) | Conditional logic without a script engine; keep small. |
| CONFIG-30 | Sequence capability and policy table | One table: sequence, handler, DECRQM/XTGETTCAP answer, policy class (allow/ask/deny), Action event. | settings GUI, KDL, socket | must | topic-escape-registry (own design) | One place to audit security. |
| CONFIG-31 | Graphics settings | `graphics` node: protocol toggles, storage limit, caps, medium policy true/false/prompt. | KDL and GUI | must | topic-graphics | Follows from CONFIG-01. |
| CONFIG-32 | Ligature mode | `ligatures always/cursor/never`, runtime scoped via Action. | `ligatures`, action `ligatures` scope window/tab/all | must | topic-fonts, kitty (disable_ligatures) | Uses CONFIG-09 scope. |
| CONFIG-33 | Font metric modifiers | Absolute, additive and percent modifiers on cell size, baseline, underline/strike/overline position and thickness, cursor, box thickness, icon height. Unit suffix explicit (`px`, `%`). | `font { adjust-* }` | must | ghostty (adjust-*), kitty (modify_font) | Fixes bad font metrics. Explicit suffix beats kitty's unit-less points default. |
| CONFIG-34 | symbol_map and narrow_symbols | Codepoint-range to font map; range-to-N-cells width forcing. | `symbol-map`, `narrow-symbols` | must | kitty | Control over icon fonts. |
| CONFIG-35 | Text blending and contrast | native/linear/linear-corrected plus gamma and contrast values. | `font { blending contrast }` | should | kitty (text_composition_strategy), ghostty (alpha-blending) | Two shipped. |
| CONFIG-36 | Builtin-glyph toggles | Per-family switches (box, block, braille, powerline, legacy, octants) between procedural drawing and font glyphs. | `font { builtin-glyphs { } }` | should | foot, wezterm | Two shipped. |
| CONFIG-37 | Low-latency profile | One knob setting coalesce 0, frame latency 1, Mailbox present, no vsync wait where possible. | `performance low-latency` | should | kitty (docs/performance.rst) | Users want one control; dependent on renderer research. |
| CONFIG-38 | Dual scrollback limits | `scrollback-limit-bytes` (default 50 MB) and `scrollback-limit-lines` (default unlimited); first reached wins; page-granular eviction. | KDL and GUI | must | ghostty (Config.zig) | Memory-bounded default with line expectations. |
| CONFIG-39 | XTGETXRES | Not adopted. | JSON socket instead | no | xterm | X-specific. |
| CONFIG-40 | Global hotkey | Not adopted; use Abyss binding or portal shortcuts. | | no | wave | Wayland has no global hotkeys. |
| CONFIG-41 | Shell resolution chain with access check | config shell, else $SHELL if X_OK, else passwd pw_shell. | shell.program/args | must | alacritty, wezterm | Robust when SHELL is stale or unset from menu launch. Source: wezterm pty/src/cmdbuilder.rs#get_shell; alacritty ShellUser::from_env. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| CONFIG-42 | terminfo-mode path, direct or none | kitty sets TERMINFO to bundled dir, or embeds whole compiled DB base64 in env (b64: prefix), or leaves untouched. | terminal.terminfo-mode | could | kitty | path/none cheap; direct has poor tool support, defer. Source: kitty options/definition.py terminfo_type; child.py#base64_terminfo_data. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |

Source: design proposal; cros-libva vaQueryConfigProfiles/vaQueryConfigEntrypoints. (gap: gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule) |
| CONFIG-44 | Build features for media components | Cargo features av1-asm, sw-h264, sw-h265, vaapi select helper contents; runtime KDL hwdec, av1-software, sw-codecs, audio-output, sync-master choose among them. | KDL hwdec, av1-software, sw-codecs, audio-output, sync-master | should | design | Separates packaging choices from user choices. Source: design proposal; topic-media.md KDL block. (gap: gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule) |
| CONFIG-45 | Adapter policy: hardware first, then fallback, then explicit error; allow-software config | Try power-preference adapters, then force_fallback_adapter if allow-software, else fail with message. | KDL allow-software (name TBD) | should | wgpu | Makes software rendering an explicit, logged, GUI-editable decision. Source: proposal; wgpu RequestAdapterOptions. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
