Source: ghostty src/terminal/apc.zig#enable, unknown_max_bytes. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| CONFIG-47 | Settings and IPC surface for input and accessibility | KDL keys for compose enable and file path, ctrl fallback, backend choice, accessibility auto/on/off, exposed rows and update interval; actions for compose-reload, announce and key-debug dump; IPC queries for AT tree and last key event. | KDL keys; actions compose-reload, announce, key-debug; IPC queries | should | kbvm | Every setting must be editable by GUI and KDL, and diagnostics must be reachable by the control socket and CLI. Source: derived from kbvm env-var surface (kbvm/src/xkb/context.rs) and AccessKit Adapter API. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |

### Open questions

- CONFIG-12: apply valid remainder with per-field tolerance, or whole-file reject on any error? Proposed: whole-file reject for structural errors, per-field drop for binds and rules.
- Which colour-scheme source takes priority between portal colour-scheme and Abyss ec-theme when both exist?
- CONFIG-20: how many importers at launch, and do they need round-trip fidelity?
- CONFIG-25: is the iTerm2 scoring weight set right for Linux (no remote job names without shell integration)?
- Ghostty list/validate action names were unverified in the input rows.
- Do includes carry a trust boundary (profiles.d from untrusted dirs)?


## automation

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUTOMATION-01 | One typed Action vocabulary and single dispatcher | One serde-style Action enum with typed, parse-validated arguments; a single dispatch entry point serves keybinds, mouse binds, palette, tab-bar clicks, rules, profiles, layouts, socket and CLI. Socket verbs and CLI subcommands are generated from the Action definition. | KDL `action` nodes; palette; `apo action <name> args`; JSON socket `action.run` | must | wezterm, ghostty, windows-terminal, zellij, tmux, kitty, topic-mux-remote | Every mature multiplexer converges here. zellij's parallel hand-maintained clap enum is the trap; generating the CLI avoids drift. kitty's `action` command (runs any mapped action) is the model for socket parity. |
| AUTOMATION-02 | Stable action IDs, layered override | Each binding or command refers to a stable string ID; user IDs namespaced `User.*`; later config layers override by ID; a null/removed entry deletes a default. Keys, palette entries and menus reference IDs, not copies. | KDL `action id="..."`, `bind key=... id=...`; `unbind` | must | windows-terminal, topic-config-automation, ghostty | Windows Terminal's id model gives clean layered overrides and lets the settings GUI edit by identity. Ghostty's positional chain/unbind is order-fragile by comparison. |
| AUTOMATION-03 | Named macros (action sequences) | An ordered action list in KDL, usable as one action anywhere; a named `macro` can be defined once and referenced by ID. Flags of the trigger apply to each step. Prefer an explicit list over ghostty's append-to-previous-binding `chain=` form because it round-trips through a GUI. | `macro` node; `do { a; b; c }` | must | ghostty (chain), windows-terminal (multipleActions), kitty (combine) | Replaces most Lua/Python glue with no scripting. |
| AUTOMATION-04 | Raw input actions | `send-text` (string escapes), `send-bytes` (raw, bypasses encoder), `send-keys` (symbolic, goes through the key encoder and respects current modes), `csi`, `esc`; plus `ignore` (consume) and `unbind`. | Actions `send-text`, `send-bytes`, `send-keys`, `csi`, `esc`, `ignore` | must | ghostty, wezterm, kitty, foot, windows-terminal (sendInput) | Primitive for binding keys to program input. Keeping the encoder-aware and raw variants distinct avoids wrong output under kitty keyboard protocol or application cursor mode. |
| AUTOMATION-05 | Bindings consume press and release | Binding match runs before the encoder; a matched press and its release are swallowed so apps never see half a shortcut. | KDL `bind` | must | wezterm | Correctness requirement for the keyboard path. |
| AUTOMATION-06 | Key tables, chords, leader and mode conditions | Modal binding tables pushed and popped by Actions; multi-key sequences and a leader key; bindings may carry mode conditions (app cursor, vi, search, alt-screen, and negations). | Actions `key-table.push`/`pop`; `bind ... when="..."` | should | wezterm, ghostty, alacritty, tmux | Large keybind space without modifier clashes; mode conditions cover app-vs-terminal conflicts. |
| AUTOMATION-07 | Palette generated from the action registry | Command = Action + title + description; every command is bindable; palette lists all registry entries with current shortcuts, fuzzy-matched, plus user `command` entries. Never hand-built. | `command` nodes; Action `palette.toggle` | must | ghostty, rio, windows-terminal, wezterm | Matches the one-vocabulary decision. Showing live shortcuts in rows is the discoverability win (rio, WT). |
| AUTOMATION-08 | Pipe text regions to commands | Actions `pipe` with source = visible, scrollback, selection, last-command-output (needs OSC 133), writing to a command's stdin; no shell glue needed. | Action `pipe source=... cmd=...` | must | foot, kitty (launch --stdin-source) | foot's only scripting hook and the basis of fuzzel/xurls workflows; folded with kitty's stdin sources so one verb serves both. |
| AUTOMATION-09 | `launch` action | Creates window, tab, os-window, overlay, split, background process or clipboard-writer with cwd policy (current, home, last-reported), env copy, stdin source, hold-after-exit, split location and bias. | Action `launch type=... cwd=... env=... stdin=... hold=... location=...` | must | kitty, wezterm (spawn), windows-terminal (new-tab/split-pane), zellij | The most reused Action: keybinds, rules, hints, layouts and the CLI all call it. |
| AUTOMATION-10 | Layout-specific verbs through one action | A single `layout` action taking a per-layout verb (resize, mirror, rotate, equalize, maximize, bias cycle) so the vocabulary grows without new top-level actions. | Action `layout verb=... args` | should | kitty, zellij | Keeps the registry flat while supporting several layout engines. |
| AUTOMATION-11 | Declarative layouts and startup | A KDL `layout` document (tree of splits/tabs with launch specs) applied by `ApplyLayout` and by a startup rule; also savable from the live tree via socket. Replaces gui-startup scripting and resurrect-style plugins. | `layout { split ... }`; Action `layout.apply`; `rule on=startup`; socket `layout.save/load` | must | wezterm, zellij, konsole (loadLayout), kitty (sessions) | Most common reason people write Lua in wezterm. |
| AUTOMATION-12 | Rules: event, condition, actions | KDL `rule { on <event>; where <match>; do <actions> }` evaluated by the core on a closed event set. Value-returning hooks (tab title, open-uri handled) use typed templates instead of callbacks. | KDL `rule`; GUI rule editor; socket `rule.list/set` | must | wezterm, iterm2, tmux, kitty, topic-config-automation | The only way to cover event-handler automation without scripting. Closed event set keeps rules serialisable and GUI-editable. |
| AUTOMATION-13 | Closed event set | Events: bell, focus, resize, title-changed, cwd-changed, host/user-changed, prompt-start, command-started, command-finished (exit filter), idle, activity-after-idle, long-running, user-var-changed, osc-notify, osc-progress, colour-scheme-changed, config-reloaded, pane-spawned/exited, open-uri, startup. Escape-sequence parsing raises events (OSC 133 for command events). Each carries named captures usable in templates. | `rule on=...`; socket event stream | must | iterm2 (16 kinds), wezterm, tmux (OSC 133 events), topic-escape-registry | iTerm2's list is the most complete template; tmux shows OSC 133 as the source of command events. |
| AUTOMATION-14 | Long-running command notification | Built-in event after threshold (default 60 s) with command and elapsed captures; action `notify` routes to Abyss/freedesktop notifications. | `rule on=long-running threshold=60s do notify` | must | iterm2 | Top-requested automation; falls out of OSC 133 plus the rule engine. |
| AUTOMATION-15 | Output-match triggers | Linear-time Rust regex (no backtracking) run on completed logical lines, last three wrapped lines, off the render thread; optional instant mode for mid-line; per-process filter; capture substitution (`\0`-`\9`) into action params. Actions: highlight, hyperlink, mark, fold, notify, send-text, run, set title/status/var, stop. | `rule { match regex=... instant=... job=... do ... }` | should | iterm2 | Only iTerm2 ships it, but users rely on it heavily. Rust `regex` lacks lookaround; iTerm2 uses ICU, so imported triggers may need rewriting. |
| AUTOMATION-16 | Coverage-floor action set | Seed the registry from ghostty's ~100 actions (input, clipboard, font size, search, scroll, prompt jump, selection adjust, export, tabs/windows/splits, titles, toggles, reload, undo/redo, quit), foot's ~35, rio's ~60, konsole's D-Bus methods (sendText, copyInputToSessions, monitors, setProfile, resizeSplits) and iTerm2's 30 trigger actions. Any action present in those and absent from ours needs a recorded reason. | `apo action --list` | must | ghostty, foot, rio, konsole, iterm2 | A checklist, not a design. Rows from foot/rio that propose a keybind-only closed enum are rejected as a design (AUTOMATION-30) but are valid as coverage floors. |
| AUTOMATION-17 | User variables via OSC 1337 SetUserVar | `OSC 1337;SetUserVar=name=<base64>` sets a per-pane variable; exposed as `var:` match field, in title templates, and as `user-var-changed` event. | Escape sequence; match field `var`; template `{var.name}` | should | kitty, iterm2, wezterm | Shells feed state into rules and tab titles without scripting. Three terminals ship the same OSC, so it is a de facto standard. Kitty OSC detail unverified. |
| AUTOMATION-18 | Scoped variables | Key-values at pane, tab, workspace, global scope via action and socket; readable in templates and match language. | Actions `var.set`/`var.get scope=...`; `apo var` | should | warp-wave (wsh), iterm2 | Share state between rules and scripts without a scripting language. |
| AUTOMATION-19 | Unified match language | One grammar everywhere (socket targeting, rules, binds, profiles, layouts): `field:regex` joined by and/or/not; fields id, title, pid, cwd, cmdline, env, var, state, neighbor, session, recent; negative IDs count from newest. | KDL `where "..."`; socket `match` param | must | kitty, tmux | One thing to learn and implement; powers targeting everywhere. Kitty grammar chosen as the most complete. |
| AUTOMATION-20 | Stable targeting and env discovery | Opaque stable numeric IDs for panes, tabs, windows plus relative tokens (`last`, `next`, `up-of`, `marked`); `APOTHEOSIS_SOCKET`, `APOTHEOSIS_WINDOW_ID`, `APOTHEOSIS_TAB_ID`, `APOTHEOSIS_PANE_ID` exported into each child so scripts default to their own pane with no lookup. | `target=` on every action and socket verb | must | tmux, zellij, alacritty, warp-wave | Handle IDs cover scripts; tokens cover humans. Alacritty and zellij both export an ID env var and it is the best ergonomics. |
| AUTOMATION-21 | JSON control socket and CLI | Persistent Unix socket (path in env) speaking JSON request/reply with request IDs; CLI subcommands (`apo ls`, `launch`, `send-text`, `get-text`, `set-colors`, `set-title`, `goto-layout`, `reload`, `signal`, `action`) are thin wrappers over the same Actions. Caller identity resolved automatically from env. | `apo ...`; socket methods mirroring Actions | must | wezterm, kitty, warp-wave, konsole, alacritty, tmux | Alacritty's one-shot message per connection is too limited; persistent stream is better. Access gated by Abyss peer credentials. |
| AUTOMATION-22 | Typed event stream with ordered replies | Replies carry request ID and sequence; async notifications are separate lines that never interleave inside a reply; clients subscribe to the same events rules use, so status bars and editors follow state without polling. | socket `subscribe`; JSON lines | must | tmux (control mode guards), topic-mux-remote | wezterm has no subscription; tmux control mode shows the correct framing. Row from topic-config-automation was "should"; upgraded because framing must be in v1 of the protocol. |
| AUTOMATION-23 | Effective-config introspection | `get-config` returns resolved values for a pane/window or global, with overrides applied, as JSON; also pane `get-meta` for properties such as cwd, process, title, user vars. | `apo config get`; `apo pane meta`; socket `config.get`, `pane.meta` | should | alacritty, warp-wave | Scripts and the settings GUI need effective values. |
| AUTOMATION-24 | Textual action syntax shared by CLI and config | `apo do 'action args' ; 'action args'` parses the same syntax as the KDL `do` body and startup actions; `;` chains. | `apo do` | should | windows-terminal, topic-config-automation | One text syntax across CLI, palette input and config. |
| AUTOMATION-25 | Parameterised workflows and snippets | Named command templates with typed placeholders (text, enum, static or command-derived choices) and defaults; inserted into the prompt or run. Snippets are saved `send-text` actions in the palette. | KDL `workflow`; Action `workflow.run name=... args` | should | warp-wave, windows-terminal | Reuses the Action vocabulary. Warp row said must; two shipping terminals justify should, and dynamic enums can follow. |
| AUTOMATION-26 | Coprocess | A sandboxed helper bound to a pane receiving its output and optionally writing input. | Action `coprocess.run`; socket subscribe overlaps | could | iterm2 | Overlaps with socket subscription, which already provides a safer path. Sandbox required. |
| AUTOMATION-27 | Named pipes into panes | `apo pipe` sends payloads to a pane or UI element with back-pressure. | `apo pipe`; socket `pipe` | could | tmux-zellij | Generic keep-it-simple variant of zellij's plugin pipes. |
| AUTOMATION-28 | Condition monitors | A rule condition polled or evaluated on change (format becomes true) rather than on a named event, as tmux monitors do. | `rule on=monitor where=...` | could | tmux | Only tmux ships it; closed event set may suffice. |
| AUTOMATION-29 | Graphics actions and events | Actions `graphics.save-image`, `copy-image`, `open-image`; events `image-received`/`image-denied` with protocol, byte count, medium; policy-gated; never log content. | Actions; `rule on=image-received` | could | topic-graphics | Differentiator; writes files so needs consent prompt. |
| AUTOMATION-30 | Media actions and events | Surface-addressed `media.open/play-pause/seek/rate/volume/mute/track/close`; events `media.started/ended/error/position`. | Actions; socket `media.list/open/control/state` | must | topic-media | Needed for the native media protocol to be scriptable under the one-vocabulary rule. |
| AUTOMATION-31 | Font actions and font query CLI | Actions `font-size-increase/decrease/reset/set`, `ligatures-set scope=...`, `font-feature-toggle`, `font-family-set`, `font-reload`, `font-clear-caches`, `blending-set`; `apo font query/list/metrics` reports resolving face and reason (primary, symbol-map, fallback), cell metrics, baseline, decoration geometry. | Actions; `apo font ...`; socket `font.*` | must | topic-fonts, kitty, ghostty | Removes a class of support questions. |
| AUTOMATION-32 | Embedded scripting (Lua, Python, watchers, kittens hooks, custom tab bar code) | Not adopted. Every use case maps to rules, templates, macros, key tables, workflows or the socket. | n/a | no | wezterm, kitty, iterm2 | Fixed decision. |
| AUTOMATION-33 | Keybind-only closed action enum without rules or IPC | Not adopted as a design; use only as coverage floor (AUTOMATION-16). | n/a | no | foot, rio, alacritty | Lacks typed args, rules and socket parity. |
| AUTOMATION-34 | Per-pane Apotheosis env vars | APOTHEOSIS_PANE/WINDOW/SOCKET/VERSION exported so CLI inside pane targets itself. | env table; IPC socket path | should | kitty, wezterm | Mirrors KITTY_WINDOW_ID/WEZTERM_PANE; enables `apotheosis ctl` without args. Source: kitty child.py#get_final_env; wezterm env. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| AUTOMATION-35 | signal-child action and IPC | Send a named signal to foreground group or session leader via action/IPC; kitty rc signal_child. | action signal-child; IPC pane.signal | should | kitty | Control without scripting, shared Action vocabulary. Source: kitty kitty/rc/signal_child.py. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| AUTOMATION-36 | Pane info/processes/events over control socket | JSON pane.info (pid,pgid,fg,cwd,exe,tty,size,exit), pane.processes, events child-exited, foreground-changed, cwd-changed. | IPC methods and events | must | kitty, wezterm | Rules (event->actions) need these hooks; no embedded scripting. Source: design; kitty child.py exposures, wezterm LocalProcessInfo. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| AUTOMATION-37 | Control-socket and helper channels on the same loop | Listener and client streams as Generic sources; blocking work (fonts, decode) on a pool returning via calloop::channel plus ping. | none | must | rio | Satisfies no-locks-on-hot-path and shared Action vocabulary. Source: rio rio-window event_loop (channel, ping). (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| AUTOMATION-38 | Per-window Wayland capability dump action and IPC | Action/IPC listing bound globals, versions, decoration mode, blur capability, scale, clock, present mode. | action + IPC (names TBD) | should | design | Compositor diversity makes support impossible without it. Source: dossier control-surface design (no upstream equivalent verified). (gap: gap-wayland-client-stack-and-surface) |
| AUTOMATION-39 | render.status IPC and render-info/render-restart actions | Expose adapter, driver, device type, alpha/present modes negotiated, dmabuf support, device-lost count; action to rebuild renderer. | IPC render.status; actions render-info, render-restart | should | design | Diagnosability for transparency/GPU issues; fits shared Action vocabulary. Source: proposal. (gap: gap-wgpu-dmabuf-alpha-present-facts) |
| AUTOMATION-40 | Parser limit counters via control socket | Counters for dropped/capped sequences and sync timeouts exposed over JSON socket and as rule events. | IPC counters; rule events | could | design | Makes caps debuggable in the settings GUI. Source: derived; no peer. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| AUTOMATION-41 | Differential state dump for debugging parser | Action to dump parser state and last N actions for bug reports. | action parser-dump (name TBD) | could | ghostty | Helps triage fuzz and conformance failures. Source: ghostty Parser.zig Action formatter. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |

### Open questions

- Event payload schema: which captures are available per event, and do template expressions need conditionals or only substitution?
- Do `where` regexes use Rust `regex` only (no lookaround)? iTerm2 trigger import would need a translation story.
- Rule evaluation order and loop protection (a rule's action raising an event that retriggers it); stop-processing semantics.
- Socket authorisation: which actions need an Abyss consent prompt when invoked by a pane's own child versus an external client (e.g. send-text into another pane, pipe, coprocess).
- OSC 1337 SetUserVar details for kitty are unverified; confirm and decide whether to accept the kitty form too.
- Whether `apo` mirrors tmux-style session/window/pane target strings or only IDs plus match language.
- Whether condition monitors (AUTOMATION-28) are redundant with a `long-running`/`idle` event plus match.


## ui

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UI-01 | Glass background: opacity, alpha-mode, compositor blur | Alpha applies to default-background cells by default, with foot's alpha-mode semantics (default / matching / all) so coloured cells and text stay opaque; premultiplied output from the wgpu renderer; opacity changeable at runtime by Action. Blur is requested from the compositor (ext_background_effect_manager_v1 on every compositor, Abyss included; no first-party glass protocol — T-17 §3); we do not blur in-process. | `colors { alpha; alpha-mode; blur }`, Action `set-opacity` | must | kitty, foot, alacritty | Core of the glass aesthetic. Three terminals ship it; foot's alpha-mode is the most precise rule for which cells are translucent. |
| UI-02 | Background image / window logo | Optional image drawn behind the grid inside the same wgpu pass, with position, opacity and scale. | `background { image; opacity; position }` | could | kitty | Single-source feature; cheap in our renderer but not essential. |
| UI-03 | Cursor trail | Four cursor-quad corners chase the target with a time-composable exponential ease-out (1 - 2^(-10 dt/decay)), which stays correct across irregular frame times under event-driven pacing; leading corners use a faster decay to form a smear; fades when the cursor is hidden; start threshold and decay pair configurable. | `cursor { trail { enabled; decay-fast; decay-slow; threshold } }` | should | kitty, rio | Two terminals ship it and users rely on it for orientation. Rio's composable easing chosen over fixed-duration tweens because it needs no per-frame state reset. Off by default. |
| UI-04 | Shared animation primitives | One spring and ease helper in the renderer used by tabs, overlays, quick-terminal slide, bell flash and trail; honours a global reduce-motion setting. | `ui { animation { enabled; reduce-motion } }` | should | rio, ghostty | Fluid motion is a stated aesthetic; one helper keeps chrome consistent. Rio's spring helper is the model. |
| UI-05 | Command registry | A single table of (name, brief, doc, Action, menu placement) from which palette, menus, settings keybind editor, CLI help and docs are generated. | Action vocabulary; event `augment-palette` becomes KDL `palette { entry }` | must | wezterm | One metadata source avoids drift between five surfaces. |
| UI-06 | Command palette with nested and iterated entries | Palette lists every registry command plus user entries; entries may nest and iterate over a collection (profiles, schemes, layouts, saved macros) like Windows Terminal iterateOn; user entries come from the same list as bindings. | `palette { entry { name; icon; actions } }`, Action `palette-open` | must | wezterm, windows-terminal | Palette is first-class and must surface user layouts and macros automatically. |
| UI-07 | Template language for tab titles, status and badges | Small expression language: variables (cwd, process, user vars, index, zoom, exit status, host), filters (truncate, basename), if/else, and style spans (colour, weight); no functions or scripting. Used by tab titles, status bars, badges and window titles. Konsole-style separate local and remote title formats are expressed as conditionals. | `tab { title "..." }`, `status { left; right }`, Action `rename-tab` | must | wezterm, konsole, kitty | format-tab-title and update-status are the main reasons people use Lua in wezterm; this covers them without a script engine. Wezterm's attribute-list builder rejected in favour of an inline template that the GUI can edit as text. |
| UI-08 | Tab bar | Glass tab strip drawn by the grid renderer with real depth; styles (floating, flat, hidden), max width, per-tab colour and activity colour, inline rename with IME, drag reorder. | `tabs { style; max-width; position }`, `tab { color }`, Action `rename-tab` | must | kitty, wezterm, rio, konsole | Tabs are core. Immediate-mode rendering (rio) is the simplest lifecycle; kitty's style list is the reference for variants. |
| UI-09 | Tab and pane status badges | Native indicators on tabs: unseen output, bell, progress (OSC 9;4), command running, tab status set by rules. | `tabs { badges { unseen; bell; progress } }`, Action `set-tab-status` | should | wezterm, iterm2 | Users depend on unseen-output cues. Native rather than template-driven so they work with defaults. |
| UI-10 | OSC 9;4 progress | Parse ConEmu states 0 remove, 1 normal, 2 error, 3 indeterminate, 4 paused/warning, with percentage; render as eased bar in the tab (about 0.2 s) and optionally forward to compositor/taskbar as a hint; clears on timeout if not refreshed. | `progress { forward-to-compositor }` | should | rio, wezterm, windows-terminal | Emitted by winget, cargo, systemd tools. Safe sequence. |
| UI-11 | Quick terminal / dropdown / hotkey window | Edge- or centre-anchored window with size, screen choice, slide animation, autohide on focus loss and keyboard-interactivity mode. On Abyss uses a first-party protocol; elsewhere wlr-layer-shell, disabled when absent. Modelled as a window role/profile (not a magic name), with the process resident after the last window closes. Toggled by Action, CLI or compositor binding. | `quick-terminal { position; size; screen; animation; autohide; keyboard }`, Action `toggle-quick-terminal`, `apo` CLI | must | ghostty, iterm2, windows-terminal, wezterm | Top-requested feature in several trackers (wezterm #1751, WT #9996). Role-based design chosen over WT's magic `_quake` name. |
| UI-12 | Pane frames, titles and border styles | Translucent rim and title per pane; modes full, titles-only, none; per-pane override and borderless flag; dim-inactive option; focus = brighter fill plus crisp rim, not a glow. | `panes { frames; border-style; dim-inactive }`, per-pane override | must | zellij, tmux, konsole | Needed for splits; per-pane override and titles-only mode come from zellij. |
| UI-13 | Visual pane select | Overlay of short labels on panes; typing a label focuses it (or targets another Action). | `pane-select { characters }`, Action `pane-select` | should | kitty, wezterm | Palette-free pane jump, shipped twice. |
| UI-14 | Typed prompt Actions: Pick, Prompt, Confirm | Actions open glass UI (list picker, text line, yes/no) then run a `then` action list with `$input` / `$choice` variables, replacing Lua callbacks. | Actions `pick`, `prompt`, `confirm` with `then { }` | should | wezterm | Enables interactive macros without scripting. |
| UI-15 | Character / emoji picker | Searchable Unicode and emoji picker overlay that inserts into the pane. | Action `char-select` | should | wezterm, kitty | Two terminals ship it (kitty unicode input); easy in the glass UI. |
| UI-16 | KDL context menu | Right-click menus per target (pane, tab, bar, common) of item / separator entries that run Actions, with toggled labels, after/before placement, remove and clear-defaults so users patch defaults without copying them. | `context-menu { pane { item; separator; remove } }` | should | zellij, wezterm | Same Action vocabulary drives menus; zellij's patch semantics are the best merge model. |
| UI-17 | Popup / command overlay pty | Floating pane that runs a command in its own pty with size, close-on-exit and auto-size; modal option. | Action `popup { command; size; close-on-exit }` | should | tmux, zellij | Fuzzy pickers and git UIs in a float are heavily used; treated as a floating pane, not a separate subsystem. |
| UI-18 | Settings GUI with full parity | Pages generated from the option table with search; edits go to the same model as the KDL; live apply with undo, plus a preview terminal; per-setting "show in KDL" jump and open-file action. Live apply with undo chosen over save/discard since state is plain KDL and undo is cheap. |
| UI-19 | Theme pair following system light/dark | `theme` accepts a light/dark pair switched with the system or compositor theme; chrome and panes both switch; report via DEC mode 2031; optional hue override when detection is wrong. | `theme { light; dark; follow-system; hue }` | should | windows-terminal, tmux, zellij | Three tools ship it; Abyss gives compositor-level sync. |
| UI-20 | Per-host colour scheme | Rule on ssh/host change applies a scheme variant (dark and light) to the pane as a production-host cue; restored on exit. | rule `on host-change -> set-scheme` | could | kitty | Only kitty ships it; falls out of the rules engine for free. |
| UI-21 | Client-side decorations fallback | Draw glass title bar, border and buttons when the compositor offers no server-side decorations; hide when maximized or fullscreen. | `window { decorations; csd { ... } }` | should | foot | Required for compositors without SSD. |
| UI-22 | Padding, centring and resize-by-cells | XxY or four-sided padding; centre modes (always / fullscreen / maximized-and-fullscreen); window snaps to cell multiples with an option to keep the grid on resize. | `window { padding; centre; resize-by-cells }` | should | foot, kitty, alacritty | Users depend on exact padding and centring. |
| UI-23 | Overlay scrollbar and scroll marks | Overlay scrollbar that does not reflow the pane, auto-hides after a timeout, shown while scrolling or hovered; marks by category (prompt, success, warning, error) from OSC 133 exit codes, drawn in the track. | `scrollbar { style; position; auto-hide; marks }` | should | tmux, windows-terminal | Overlay avoids resize churn; marks give scrollback navigation for free. |

| UI-29 | Badge | Interpolated translucent text watermark (host, profile) in a pane corner with font, size, colour and opacity, set by template or rule. | `badge { text; color; size }`, Action `set-badge` | should | iterm2, konsole | Two mature terminals ship it; prevents wrong-host mistakes. |

## Open questions

- Which compositor-side protocol does Abyss expose for quick terminal and blur beyond ext_background_effect_manager_v1? Needs an Abyss spec decision.
- Sticky command header and block status chrome in Warp are unverified; confirm behaviour or design from first principles.
- Is UI-27 copy-block split into must (copy) and should (chrome)? Left merged.
- Remote mux (lag indicator, UI-33) depends on whether Apotheosis ships a mux server; unresolved.
- Cursor trail default (off) and global reduce-motion interaction with UI-04 need a UX decision.
- Template language grammar (UI-07) is shared by titles, status, badges and rules; needs its own spec to avoid becoming a scripting language.
- UI-02 background image: confirm decode path (shared with the media decoder helper or a plain image crate).


## integration

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INTEGRATION-01 | Desktop notifications OSC 9 / 777 / 99 | Parse all three into one internal Notification event. OSC 9 and 777;notify give title and body. OSC 99 is the rich path: chunked key/value payloads with id, title/body/buttons/icon, urgency, expiry, sound, report/close events, capability and alive queries. Per-pane rate limits and an origin badge. Activation reports to the app require consent. | `ESC ] 99 ; i=..:p=body ; text ST` (keys a c d e f g i n o p s t u w); `OSC 777;notify;title;body`; `OSC 9`; KDL `notifications { osc9; osc777; osc99 }`; event `notify` -> actions | must | kitty, foot, vte-konsole, iterm2 family, topic-escape-registry | Every mature terminal ships at least one of these. OSC 99 is the superset and maps directly onto freedesktop notifications, so it is the internal model and 9/777 are degenerate cases. Konsole's raw-parameter event is kept so rules can react to a notification (https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/desktop-notifications/; konsole Vt102Emulation.cpp Notification). |
| INTEGRATION-02 | Focus and visibility filter for notifications | The mux already knows window focus and tab/pane visibility. Suppress or downgrade a notification according to occasion. This is also how OSC 99 `o=` and foot's inhibit-when-focused are answered. | OSC 99 `o=always\|unfocused\|invisible`; KDL `notifications { when-focused }`; per-rule `when` | must | kitty, foot, vte-konsole | Prevents spam when the user is looking at the pane. Apotheosis has the native mux knowledge other terminals approximate. |
| INTEGRATION-03 | Notification delivery backend | Send on Abyss through the notification service directly (D-Bus org.freedesktop.Notifications) from a background task so the UI loop never blocks. Read back the returned id to support replace, close and activation (click focuses the originating pane via an XDG activation token). Keep a templated command (`${title} ${body} ${icon} ${urgency} ${replace-id}` and similar) as the fallback for non-freedesktop setups. | KDL `notifications { backend "dbus"\|"command"; command ... }` | should | foot, rio, vte-konsole | Foot's command template is the proven portable approach. Direct D-Bus is more precise (ids, actions), so it is primary. Rio shows the off-main-thread pattern (foot notify.c; rio-notifier/src/lib.rs). |
| INTEGRATION-04 | Notify on command finish | Stock rule using OSC 133 D timing: when a command exceeds a minimum duration and the pane is unfocused or invisible, fire an action (default notify). Ships as a default rule, not a special feature. | Event `command-finished` with `when` focus and `min-duration` -> actions; kitty `notify_on_cmd_finish`; ghostty `notify-on-command-finish*` names accepted as import aliases | should | kitty, ghostty, vte-konsole (partial, legacy precmd/preexec behind a flag) | Two mature terminals ship it and users rely on it. It is a showcase for the event -> actions engine. Mode set: never / unfocused / always. |
| INTEGRATION-05 | Bell channels | Independent switches for system bell, urgency hint (xdg-activation, red margin fallback absent), desktop notification, visual flash and a command. Mode 1042 lets apps toggle. Expressed as rules on event `bell`. | `bell.system`, `bell.urgent`, `bell.notify`, `bell.visual`, `bell.command`; DECSET 1042; event `bell` -> actions | should | foot | Users configure these separately. Rules make them composable (e.g. flash only when unfocused) (foot doc/foot.ini.5.scd#bell). |
| INTEGRATION-06 | Notify from CLI | `apo notify` raises a notification through the same pipeline as the escape sequences, usable at script end. Also an Action `notify`. | `apo notify --title --body`; Action `notify` | should | warp-wave (`wsh notify`) | Cheap once INTEGRATION-01 exists. Also reachable from rules and the palette. |
| INTEGRATION-07 | Foreground-process introspection as a condition | Expose pane foreground process name, argv, cwd and children (via /proc and the pty foreground process group). Use as a declarative `when` condition so keybinds pass keys through to vim/tmux and titles adapt. No scripting needed. | Condition `process="nvim"` (glob list); socket method `pane.process-info` | must | wezterm | Smart pass-through is the top Lua use-case in WezTerm. Making it a declarative condition keeps the no-scripting rule (docs/config/lua/pane/get_foreground_process_info.md). |
| INTEGRATION-08 | Close confirmation by process statefulness | A declarative list/glob of processes that do or do not prompt on close, using INTEGRATION-07 data. | KDL `confirm-close { skip-for "bash" "zsh"; always-for ... }`; WezTerm `skip_close_confirmation_for_processes_named` accepted as import alias | should | wezterm, iterm2 (confirm-on-quit, unverified), windows-terminal | The declarative list replaces WezTerm's `mux-is-process-stateful` event handler (docs/config/lua/mux-events/mux-is-process-stateful.md). |
| INTEGRATION-09 | Background blur and glass on any compositor | Bind ext-background-effect-v1 with a blur region on the surface; fall back to the KDE blur protocol; degrade with one logged warning when absent. On Abyss, ext-background-effect-v1 only, one box over the window geometry so Abyss draws its own backdrop (T-08 §3); no native or private path. Opacity separate from blur. | KDL `window { opacity; blur }` (ghostty `background-opacity`, `background-blur`, `background-opacity-cells` are names for comparison) | must | ghostty, rio | Direct enabler of the glass window. Both terminals converge on ext-background-effect with a KDE fallback (ghostty src/apprt/gtk/winproto/wayland.zig#syncBlur; rio-window .../ext_background_effect.rs). |
| INTEGRATION-10 | Wayland protocol set | Bind each global opportunistically and fall back gracefully: xdg-shell and decoration, xdg-activation, xdg-toplevel-tag and icon, xdg-system-bell, fractional-scale, viewporter, cursor-shape, presentation-time, single-pixel-buffer, ext-background-effect, color-management, text-input-v3, pointer-constraints, relative-pointer. Add keyboard-shortcuts-inhibit, ext-session-lock awareness and security-context. | Internal; capability report via `apo info wayland` | must | foot, rio, ghostty | Foot's wayland.c#handle_global is the reference list and rio's sctk fork covers the same set. Our own client must match it or lose features. |
| INTEGRATION-11 | IME cursor rectangle tracking | Send the cell rectangle of the terminal cursor to text-input-v3 on every frame it moves so the candidate popup follows. Optionally use the OSC 133 prompt position when the app cursor is hidden. | Internal | must | topic-keyboard (consistent with foot, rio text-input support) | Required for CJK input (https://wayland.app/protocols/text-input-unstable-v3). |

## Open questions

- Does Abyss expose a notification, global-shortcut and dock-panel protocol, and is it ready? INTEGRATION-03, 21 and 22 assume it.
- Does ghostty ship a dropdown on Wayland, and does the xdg GlobalShortcuts portal work in non-Abyss compositors? Row 21 is only partly verified.
- Contour's mode 2031 doc path is unverified; confirm other terminals' support before citing them.
- Confirm that iTerm2 and foot ship close-confirmation lists and OSC 22 (row 8 and 29 cite them without a checked source).
- Kitty panel and quick-access-terminal details are unverified.
- Notification consent: should activation reports (OSC 99) default off per pane or per rule?
- Should the control socket permit send-text and screen reads by default, or only after an explicit capability grant?
- Is OSC 52 read allowed by default? (Row 25 assumes consent.)


## security

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SECURITY-01 | OSC 52 clipboard policy | Separate read and write gates per target (c to Wayland clipboard, p/s to primary), each `allow`/`deny`/`ask`. Default: write ask-or-allow from the focused pane only, read always ask. Payload size cap, never log contents. Ask goes through the glass prompt (Abyss trusted UI consent on Abyss). Primary selection implemented via the primary-selection protocol, not only wl_data_device. | KDL `security { osc52 { write; read; max-bytes } }` per profile; GUI toggle | must | xterm, kitty (clipboard_control), ghostty (clipboard-read/write), foot (osc52), alacritty (Osc52 modes), rio, vte-konsole, topic-escape-registry | Every terminal ships it. Foot/alacritty enum modes are too coarse; kitty and ghostty ask variants plus per-direction gating are the best model. Defaults tighter than all of them (read never silent). |
| SECURITY-02 | Kitty clipboard protocol (OSC 5522) with write bound | Streaming base64 chunks, MIME-typed read/write, id sanitising, strict base64, in-flight transaction replacement. Buffered writes bounded (ghostty uses 64 MiB; ours configurable). Same allow/deny/ask policy as SECURITY-01. | `clipboard-write-limit-bytes`; shares osc52 policy | should | ghostty, kitty | Richer than OSC 52 and the memory bound is the key hardening. Needs the same consent path. |
| SECURITY-03 | Paste safety and bracketed paste | Bracketed paste (2004) with embedded CSI 201~ stripped and C0 controls (BS, DEL, ENQ, EOT, ESC, NUL, stty specials) neutralised, tab/newline kept. When mode is off, or text is multi-line or large, confirm in glass prompt (optionally only at a shell prompt, via semantic marks). Optional tab-conversion prompt, URL quoting, paste-slowly. | `paste { sanitize; confirm-multiline; confirm-bytes; paste-slowly }`; Action `paste-slow` | must | xterm, kitty (paste_actions), ghostty, iterm2, windows-terminal, topic-escape-registry | Universal. Unify xterm's neutralisation with iterm2's confirmation UX; use ghostty's bracketed-safe idea (skip confirm when mode 2004 is active and content sanitised). |
| SECURITY-04 | Capability policy gates per feature class | One policy table in KDL: colour ops, font ops, mouse ops, termcap, title ops, window ops, selection ops, each with `allow`/`deny`/`ask` and named sub-features (xterm disallowed* lists with wildcards). Escape sequences can never change policy; only GUI/KDL/socket can. Denied sequences are silent-drop plus a counter. | `security { <class> { <subfeature> "deny" } }` per profile; settings GUI matrix | must | xterm | Best structured model (named sub-features beat boolean switches). Defaults stricter than xterm. |
| SECURITY-05 | Policy introspection (XTQALLOWED/DISALLOWED/ALLOWABLE) | OSC 60, 61, 62 reply with comma lists built from policy state. | OSC 60/61/62 | should | xterm | Lets programs and tests detect blocks instead of timing out. Only xterm ships it, but it is cheap and pairs with SECURITY-04. |
| SECURITY-06 | Title and icon label reporting | CSI 20/21 t reporting default deny (or hex-only via title modes); every title shown in glass tabs has control characters stripped and length capped. Title stack push/pop (22/23) allowed. | `security { title-report "deny" }` | must | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Classic injection vector; xterm title modes (raw/hex/UTF-8) are the best technique, but deny-by-default is simpler and enough. |
| SECURITY-07 | XTWINOPS safe subset | Allow reports 14, 16, 18, 19 (pixel and cell sizes, needed by image tools) and 22/23. Deny 21. Move/resize/raise/iconify/fullscreen: ask or deny, rate limited, routed through the compositor on Abyss (Wayland cannot honour moves anyway). | `security { window-ops { report-size "allow"; manipulate "deny" } }` | must | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Two rows agree; merged. Reports are required by graphics clients, manipulation is not. |
| SECURITY-08 | Font set/query (OSC 50) | Ask or deny; restricted to installed family names, no paths; query reply never exposes local config. | `security { font-ops "deny" }` | could | xterm | Niche; leaks fontconfig and path abuse risk. Only if a use case emerges. |
| SECURITY-09 | ENQ answerback empty | ENQ returns a configurable string, empty by default. | `security { answerback "" }` | must | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Non-empty answerback is a PTY injection channel. |
| SECURITY-10 | DECRQSS generated-only replies | Support SGR, DECSTBM, DECSCUSR, DECSCA; reply built from internal state, never echoing request bytes. | DCS $ q | should | xterm, topic-escape-registry | Needed by some apps; echo bugs are a known class (iTerm2 CVE-2022-45872, unverified id). |
| SECURITY-11 | Legacy OSC 777 / OSC 666 hardening | Legacy urxvt-style 777 handlers (precmd, preexec, container) off by default; strings length-capped; termprops blocklist and well-known type enforcement. | `security { osc777-legacy "off" }` | must | vte-konsole | Escape-driven features are attack surface; default off. |
| SECURITY-12 | Notification (OSC 99) id and payload sanitising | Identifier charset restricted and sanitised on echo; plain text only, no markup; C0/C1 forbidden unless base64. | OSC 99 `i=`, `e=1` | must | kitty | Stops escape injection through reports. |
| SECURITY-13 | Policy conformance tests | Policy tests run twice, window ops allowed and denied, asserting identical safe behaviour in the denied case. | test suite | should | xterm (esctest2) | Cheap regression net for SECURITY-04/07. |
| SECURITY-14 | Graphics file/shm medium policy | Medium as capability flags: direct always; file (t=f) regular files only; temp (t=t) deleted only if under a known temp dir and path contains `tty-graphics-protocol`; shm (t=s). Refuse devices, sockets, /proc, /sys, /dev. Default local sessions only; prompt mode for others. All read failures return one identical error (no filesystem existence oracle). | `media { file-transmission "off\|local-only\|always\|ask" }`; per-pane rule | must | kitty, ghostty (Limits), topic-graphics, topic-escape-registry, topic-media | Five rows converge. Ghostty's Limits struct is the cleanest shape; kitty's uniform error is the key trick. |
| SECURITY-15 | Image storage quota with LRU eviction | Per-screen byte counter (kitty and ghostty 320 MiB default); oldest/least recently used evicted on overflow; 0 disables the protocol. | `media { max-image-memory }` | must | kitty, ghostty | Primary DoS defence. |
| SECURITY-16 | Dimension and payload caps before allocation | Reject width/height over 10000 and payload over 400 MB (kitty) before allocating; check w*h*4 against budget; sixel capped at 10000 px with canvas growth clamped; iTerm2 inline capped (wezterm 100,000,000 bytes). | `media { max-dimension; max-payload }` | must | kitty, foot, wezterm, topic-graphics | Same defence across protocols; one shared budget. |
| SECURITY-17 | Sandboxed decoder worker | PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP decoded in seccomp/Landlock helper returning RGBA over pipe or memfd with dimension limits set on the decoder. | internal; reuses media helper | should | topic-graphics (design inference, unverified in other terminals) | Decoder bugs cannot reach the terminal process. Not shipped by a mature terminal, so should, not must. |
| SECURITY-18 | iTerm2 download mode (inline=0) | Not implemented. | n/a | no | topic-graphics | Arbitrary file write from output violates no ambient authority. |
| SECURITY-19 | Media surface lifetime and cleanup | Media surface and helper destroyed with the pane; images deleted on reconfigure/exit (mpv a=d behaviour); per-pane memory cap. | `media { max-image-memory }`; Action `media-close` | must | topic-media (mpv vo_kitty) | Crash or kill must not leak GPU memory or helpers. |
| SECURITY-20 | Hyperlink safety UX | Modifier-click or context menu to open, URI shown on hover, confirm unknown schemes, scheme allow-list, local `file:` only when host matches, option to disable. | `links { open-modifier; allowed-schemes; confirm-unknown; show-uri }` | must | vte-konsole (OSC 8 spec, Konsole allow-list) | Prevents deceptive link text and scheme handler abuse. |
| SECURITY-21 | Control socket authentication | Socket in XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, directory 0700, refuse directory not owned by user or with group/other bits (tmux, wezterm, zellij), SO_PEERCRED uid check on every connection. Filesystem permissions alone (alacritty) are rejected. | `control { socket-path }` | must | tmux, wezterm, zellij, topic-mux-remote, alacritty (as counter-example) | Same-uid processes are the threat; add per-client scopes (SECURITY-22). |
| SECURITY-22 | Per-client capability grants | Named capabilities (read pane contents, write input, run commands, read clipboard, manage layout), granted per socket client, cached, prompted via consent UI. Model borrowed from zellij plugin permissions. | `control { grant "<client>" { ... } }`; consent prompt | should | tmux-zellij (zellij) | We have no plugins but socket clients and add-ons need the same split. |
| SECURITY-23 | Key event stream opt-in and redacted | Subscription requires capability, per-pane opt-in, suppressed in no-echo state; never logged. | `apo events keys` | must | topic-keyboard (EclipseOS invariant) | Human input is never logged by content. |
| SECURITY-24 | Consent for command-running actions | Any action that runs a command or injects input and is triggered by output, link, import or rule needs explicit per-rule opt-in plus confirm-once. Prompt shows the command in a bounded monospaced box, ignores Return, activates only on explicit choice. Imported rule packs show a review dialog. | rules `{ requires-consent }`; glass/Abyss trusted prompt | must | iterm2, rio, topic-config-automation | Output-driven run-command is an injection surface; rio's prompt design is the best UX detail. |
| SECURITY-25 | Resurrected commands start suspended | Restored command panes show the command and wait for a keypress; layouts carry `start_suspended`. | layout `start-suspended`; restore confirmation option | must | zellij (two rows) | Avoids unseen re-execution of destructive commands. |
| SECURITY-26 | Session file and socket hygiene | Saved sessions in XDG state, mode 0600; password-role panes excluded; optional wipe-on-detach and idle lock. | `session { persist; wipe-on-detach }` | must | synthesis (zellij, wezterm, project invariants) | Persistence extends secret lifetime. |
| SECURITY-27 | Command rewrite for saved sessions | Exclude/rewrite rules in KDL that strip tokens or wrappers from saved commands (no shell hook). | `session { redact "<regex>" }` | should | zellij (post_command_discovery_hook) | Keeps secrets out of saved layouts without scripting. |
| SECURITY-28 | PTY fd and child lifecycle | Close-on-exec on every PTY fd (rio fixed a bug leaking panes' ptys into later shells); termination escalates after 1 s grace, bounded post-SIGKILL reap, signals reach descendants. | internal | must | rio | Concrete past security bug. |
| SECURITY-29 | Per-surface systemd scope | Transient scope per surface with optional memory and process limits so OOM hits one shell. | `process { cgroup; memory-limit; processes-limit; hard-fail }` | should | ghostty | Cheap isolation; Linux only, falls back silently without systemd user manager. |
| SECURITY-30 | Armed one-time-password channels | Terminal-initiated data transfers (DCS requests) validated against a request id and one-time password held in local shared memory, so arbitrary remote output cannot trigger transfers. | internal template for any terminal-armed channel | must | kitty (ssh kitten) | Template for any channel that moves data on request. |
| SECURITY-31 | Per-domain escape policy for remote panes | Remote panes get stricter default policy than local (clipboard, graphics medium, DCS channels), set per domain/profile, prompt-by-default. | `domain "<name>" { security { ... } }` | must | synthesis (kitty, wezterm) | Remote output is untrusted. |
| SECURITY-32 | Bounded buffers and reply caps | Output watermarks, minimum write size, max age, reply cap (tmux control: 512/8192 B, 300 s, 64 MiB); fuzz the codec. | internal limits | must | tmux | Hostile or stale peers must not exhaust memory. |
| SECURITY-33 | Password manager integration | Backed by Secret Service or portal; typed into PTY only on user confirm; never logged or stored. | Action `open-password-manager` | should | iterm2 | Details unverified; useful but invariant-bound. |
| SECURITY-34 | Passwords/TOTP in config | Not implemented. | n/a | no | kitty (ssh kitten) | Violates the never-stored password rule. |
| SECURITY-35 | TLS domain with PKI | Not in v1; tunnel unix protocol over ssh. If ever added, copy the ssh credential bootstrap and CN-vs-uid check. | n/a | no | wezterm | Rows disagreed (could vs no). Chose no: listening daemon and PKI are large surface, TLS stacks are C-backed, ssh covers remote. |
| SECURITY-36 | Forwarding control socket to remote | Not implemented by default; only per-host, off by default, visible indicator and prompt if ever. | n/a | no | kitty | Gives remote full local control. |
| SECURITY-37 | Remote/web attach tokens | Out of v1. | n/a | no | zellij | Network listener and token lifecycle. |
| SECURITY-38 | Executable config (git plugins, geninclude) | Not implemented; share config as KDL packs. | n/a | no | wezterm, kitty | Code execution at config load, breaks no-scripting rule. |
| SECURITY-39 | Secure keyboard entry | Not needed; document that Wayland isolates input. | n/a | no | iterm2 | No threat to mitigate. |
| SECURITY-40 | Cloud account and telemetry | Not implemented. | n/a | no | warp | Conflicts with local-first and no ambient authority. |
| SECURITY-41 | Single-process isolation weakness | Do not copy: isolate decoders, supervise renderer, keep PTY helpers separate. | n/a | no | foot | Anti-pattern, one crash takes all windows. |
| SECURITY-42 | Persisting evicted scrollback | Memory-only by default; spill to disk only as explicit encrypted opt-in. | `scrollback { spill "off" }` | no | contour | Another store to secure; opt-in variant remains open. |
| SECURITY-43 | close_range for stray fd closing | Close all fds >2 in child via close_range(2) rather than walking /dev/fd (wezterm) or looping 3..255 (kitty). | none | should | kitty, wezterm | Prevents leaking control sockets, decoder helper fds, Wayland fd to children. Source: wezterm pty/src/unix.rs#close_random_fds; kitty child.c. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| SECURITY-44 | Termios polling for echo/canonical state | tcgetattr on master every 200 ms while focused to detect ECHO off (password prompt). | IPC event pane.echo-changed | could | ghostty | Hint for secure-input UI, clipboard warnings. Source: ghostty src/termio/Exec.zig (TERMIOS_POLL_MS). (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| SECURITY-45 | Scrub activation tokens from child env | Remove XDG_ACTIVATION_TOKEN and DESKTOP_STARTUP_ID. | env.remove defaults | must | alacritty | Prevent children reusing launcher focus tokens. Source: alacritty tty/unix.rs#from_fd. (gap: gap-pty-terminfo-child-lifecycle) |
| SECURITY-46 | Render node fd passed into the sandboxed helper | vaGetDisplayDRM needs an open DRM render node; the main process opens /dev/dri/renderD* and passes the fd over the helper socket so the helper sandbox needs no device path access. | none | should | cros-libva | Keeps the decoder sandbox tight. Source: cros-libva lib/build.rs (va-drm for vaGetDisplayDRM). (gap: gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule) |
| SECURITY-47 | Sandboxed helper contains all decoders | rav1d, rust_h264/265 and the VA-API code run only in the helper process under Landlock/seccomp; the main process never links them. | none | must | rav1d | Young decoders and unsafe-heavy rav1d must not sit in the terminal process. Source: topic-media.md architecture; rav1d src unsafe census (mc.rs, ipred.rs). (gap: gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule) |
| SECURITY-48 | Fuzz and conformance gate for software decoders | CI fuzz targets and conformance vectors for rust_h264, rust_h265 and rav1d before they are default-on. | none | should | design | Maturity of the pure-Rust H.264/H.265 decoders is verified only from READMEs. Source: rust_h265 README (fuzzing noted); rust_h264 README (conformance). (gap: gap-media-deps-vs-no-c-rule) |
| SECURITY-49 | Per-protocol byte caps enforced in the parser | Each string sink declares a budget; parser aborts the sequence on overflow and signals abort so half-built transactions reset. | none | must | ghostty | Untrusted payloads (OSC 52, 5522, kitty, sixel) must be bounded; vte std OSC Vec is unbounded. Source: ghostty src/terminal/apc.zig#max_bytes; src/terminal/osc.zig#MAX_ALLOCATING_BUF. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-50 | Fuzzing without libFuzzer C++ in the product build | Structure-aware proptest/arbtest generators, differential tests against vte, corpus replay; optional cargo-fuzz in a separate non-shipping CI job. | none | should | ghostty | cargo-fuzz's libfuzzer-sys compiles C++; strict no-C rule favours pure-Rust fuzzing in the normal test suite. Source: https://github.com/rust-fuzz/libfuzzer ; ghostty test/fuzz-libghostty/README.md. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-51 | OSC 5522 kitty clipboard streaming | Packets with type=read, write or wdata, base64 mime, chunks of at most 4096 raw bytes before base64, strict base64 validation, EINVAL abort on bad data. | escape: OSC 5522 | must | kitty, ghostty | Needs a streaming OSC sink and consent flow; must not buffer whole clipboard. Source: kitty docs/clipboard.rst; ghostty osc/parsers/kitty_clipboard_protocol.zig. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-52 | OSC 52 clipboard cap and consent | Small default cap (1 MiB), write-only by default, read requires prompt; payload streamed to base64 decoder. | escape: OSC 52 | must | ghostty | Clipboard exfiltration risk and memory DoS. Source: ghostty src/terminal/osc.zig. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-53 | SOS and PM strings skipped with zero memory | Enter skip state, terminate only on ST/CAN/SUB/ESC. | none | must | alacritty | Required by the spec chart; costs nothing. Source: alacritty/vte src/lib.rs#anywhere. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-54 | Whole-sequence Vec buffering for APC | wezterm accumulates APC in an unbounded Vec and hands it off at ST. | none | no | wezterm | No cap, allocation per sequence. Source: wezterm vtparse/src/lib.rs#Action::ApcPut. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-55 | Hand off big payloads to sandboxed decoder via memfd | Parser sink writes bounded payload into a memfd/arena; decoder helper process decodes PNG/zlib/media. | none | should | design | Matches fixed decision of sandboxed decoder helper; keeps parser thread small. Source: derived from EclipseOS decisions. (gap: gap-vt-parser-crate-and-sync-timeout) |
| SECURITY-56 | Compose include escapes and resource limits | include supports %L, %H, %S, %%; limits of 1024 includes, depth 128, 500,000 statements and 100,000 rules; recursive includes detected; secure-execution note. | none | should | kbvm | Bounds a hostile or broken ~/.XCompose; keep the limits and surface diagnostics in the settings GUI. Source: kbvm/src/xkb/compose.rs; kbvm/src/xkb/context.rs (max_includes, max_include_depth, max_runtime, max_compose_rules). (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |

### Open questions

- OSC 52 write default: allow from focused pane silently, or ask once per session per pane? Rows disagree (xterm allow, topic-escape ask).
- Does `ask` fall back to deny when no trusted UI exists (non-Abyss compositor)? Glass prompt is ours, so probably prompt in-window, but spoofing resistance there is unproven.
- Should SECURITY-17 (decoder sandbox) be must given hostile image input, despite no mature terminal shipping it?
- kitty uniform-error and temp-dir rules: kitty/graphics.c function names unverified.
- Whether ghostty bracketed-safe skip of confirmation is safe when the shell mishandles 2004.
- Persisted scrollback spill (SECURITY-42) opt-in encryption design.


## accessibility

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ACCESSIBILITY-01 | AT-SPI bridge with terminal-role, visible-grid text model | Use the pure-Rust `accesskit_unix` adapter (MIT OR Apache-2.0, over zbus/atspi) to publish a terminal-role accessible per pane whose text is the visible rows as UTF-8, with a line-break table, caret index and selection. | KDL `accessibility { enabled }`; Action `toggle-accessibility` | must | vte-konsole, topic-config-automation | Only licence-clean, no-C route to AT-SPI; we draw our own UI so nothing is free. Only Windows Terminal and Contour expose terminals meaningfully today, so this is a lead opportunity. Source: https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/accesskit_unix; vte/src/vteaccess-gtk4.cc#vte_accessible_text_contents_snapshot; konsole/src/widgets/TerminalDisplayAccessible.cpp |
| ACCESSIBILITY-02 | Per-pane snapshot with common prefix/suffix diff events | Keep a snapshot of visible text, attributes, line-break offsets and caret; mark stale on output; on demand diff old vs new by longest common prefix and suffix and emit one REMOVE (old middle), one INSERT (new middle), then caret-moved. Drive it from our existing dirty-row tracking to produce per-row runs. | internal; verbosity `accessibility.announce` | must | vte-konsole, topic-config-automation | VTE's diff yields sane typed/erased speech; Konsole's insert-whole-dirty-range-at-0 is poor, so we take VTE's technique. Windows Terminal raises equivalent UIA text-changed/cursor events. Source: vte/src/vteaccess-gtk4.cc#vte_accessible_text_contents_changed; vte/src/vteaccess.cc; windows-terminal TermControlAutomationPeer.cpp |
| ACCESSIBILITY-03 | Scroll-aware update suppression | When the viewport scrolls, suppress the paired contents-changed diff and emit a line-range change from a dedicated scroll handler. | internal | should | vte-konsole | Stops the AT re-reading the whole screen on every scrolled line of output. Source: vte/src/vteaccess-gtk4.cc#_vte_accessible_text_scrolled |
| ACCESSIBILITY-04 | Kill switch and lazy AT activation | Build the tree and snapshots only after an AT client attaches (auto mode), using dirty-row ranges not full-screen scans; every handler early-returns when off. Optional caret-reporting toggle for latency diagnosis. | KDL `accessibility { mode auto\|on\|off; caret-reporting }`; Action; CLI | must | vte-konsole, topic-config-automation | Full-screen snapshot is O(rows*cols) on the hot path; non-AT users must pay zero. VTE has enable-a11y; Contour documents zero cost with no client. Source: vte/src/vte.cc#Terminal::set_enable_a11y; contour/docs/accessibility.md |
| ACCESSIBILITY-05 | Text attribute runs | Return ranges of equal attributes: underline, strikethrough, fg-color, bg-color, extended with weight, font-style, invalid and link. | internal | should | vte-konsole | Conveys colour and emphasis to ATs; both references are weak here (Konsole stubs it), so we exceed them. Source: vte/src/vteaccess-gtk4.cc#vte_accessible_text_get_attributes; konsole TerminalDisplayAccessible::attributes |
| ACCESSIBILITY-06 | Character extents and offset-at-point | Map text offset to rectangle via real cell/glyph widths (union of start and end cell, full line width when multi-row) and the inverse. | internal | must | vte-konsole | Magnifier focus tracking and touch exploration depend on it; Konsole's offsetAtPoint returns 0. Index arithmetic is wrong for wide glyphs. Source: vte/src/vteaccess-gtk4.cc#vte_accessible_text_get_extents, get_offset |
| ACCESSIBILITY-07 | Text-based, not cell-based, offsets | All AT offsets are character indices into extracted text (not cell indices, no blank padding), via a per-char position table. | internal | must | vte-konsole | Correct caret/word boundaries for CJK, emoji, combining and RTL; we pick VTE's text offsets over Konsole's lines*columns. Source: konsole TerminalDisplayAccessible.cpp#characterCount; vte vteaccess-gtk4.cc char_positions |
| ACCESSIBILITY-08 | Granularity queries | Implement char/word/line/sentence/paragraph get-contents-at using the line-break table and the shared selection word rules. | internal; `word-chars` shared with selection | must | vte-konsole | Screen-reader review commands (read word/line) require them; sharing word rules keeps selection and AT consistent. Source: vte/src/vteaccess.cc; vteaccess-gtk4.cc#vte_accessible_text_get_contents_at |
| ACCESSIBILITY-09 | Semantic prompt/command/output blocks | Use OSC 133 marks (with exit code) to expose navigable blocks as accessible text ranges/objects: jump to previous prompt, read last command output. | Actions `a11y-read-last-output`, `a11y-prev-prompt`; KDL `accessibility.semantic` | must | vte-konsole | Neither reference exposes structure; the main chance to exceed prior art. Source: vte/src/vteseq.cc#set_current_shell_integration_mode; konsole Vt102Emulation.cpp (SemanticPrompts) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-10 | Prompt as own focusable editable-text node | While at a shell prompt (OSC 133), expose the prompt as a separate focus target; absent while a command runs. | follows `accessibility.semantic` | should | topic-config-automation | Focus-following readers and magnifiers keep the prompt in view; Contour ships it. Source: contour/docs/accessibility.md; contour/src/contour/display/TerminalAccessible.hpp |
| ACCESSIBILITY-11 | Accessible chrome tree | Publish tabs (tab list), splits (labelled panes with focus state), palette, search and settings as AccessKit nodes from the UI model. | internal; follows UI model | must | vte-konsole | wgpu-drawn glass has no native widgets; without this the chrome is invisible to ATs. GTK/Qt give VTE/Konsole this free. Source: vte vtegtk.cc (gtk_widget_class_set_accessible_role); konsole Qt widgets |
| ACCESSIBILITY-12 | Keyboard traversal of chrome, keyboard-only focus ring | Action cycles focus terminal, tab bar, toolbar (Ctrl+F6 style); focus ring drawn only on keyboard focus; tab switch announced; every settings field labelled with role and order. | Actions `interface.focus-next`, `interface.focus-prev`; rebindable | should | topic-config-automation | Labels/roles/focus order must be supplied explicitly in our custom UI. Source: contour/docs/accessibility.md |
| ACCESSIBILITY-13 | Announcement policy and coalesced live regions | Polite live-region updates for output on a coalescing timer off the render thread, interruptible by keyboard input; assertive for bell/notification; command-finished and progress optional. Never block the async AT call on the render path (WT notes it stalls NVDA). | KDL `accessibility.announce { output; bell; command-finished }`; profile override | should | vte-konsole, topic-config-automation | Prevents speech floods while keeping key events audible. Policy design is ours (unverified upstream); the notification-with-interrupt technique is from WT. Source: windows-terminal TermControlAutomationPeer.cpp#NotifyNewOutput |
| ACCESSIBILITY-14 | Hyperlink exposure to AT | Map OSC 8 spans to AT-SPI hyperlink/hypertext objects with URI and activation action. | Actions `open-link-at-caret`, `list-links` | should | vte-konsole | Data exists upstream but is not mapped to a11y by either reference; keyboard link activation is needed anyway. Source: vte/src/vteseq.cc#set_current_hyperlink |
| ACCESSIBILITY-15 | Pointer-only link hit-testing | Resolve links (including URLs wrapped across rows as one link) only on pointer events, no per-frame cost; map columns through wide-glyph widths and test explicitly. | internal; link rules in KDL | should | rio | Cheap hover/click links; rio issue 1619 shows the wide-glyph hit-region bug to test against. Source: https://rioterm.com/changelog; https://github.com/raphamorim/rio/issues/1619 |
| ACCESSIBILITY-16 | IME via text-input-v3, multi-seat | Implement text-input-v3 in our own Wayland client: preedit rendered in place, cursor-rectangle reports, state per seat (keyboard/pointer/IME). Always on by default. Optional `debug_key_events`-style key decode logging. Foot's DECSET 737769 style app-level IME toggle is an interface fact worth supporting. | KDL `ime { enabled }`, `debug-key-events`; DECSET 737769 | must | foot, wezterm, kitty | CJK users depend on it. Foot ships it with multi-seat; WezTerm needs it on Wayland. Source: foot ime.c, wayland.c; wezterm docs/config/lua/config/use_ime.md, debug_key_events.md |
| ACCESSIBILITY-17 | Minimum-contrast / adjust indistinguishable colours | Per-cell fg nudge toward ratio target (WCAG 1-21) when contrast with bg is too low; skip emoji and images; modes never/indexed/always/automatic; evaluate against effective background on translucent glass. | KDL `minimum-contrast`, `adjust-indistinguishable { mode }` | must | ghostty, windows-terminal | Two mature terminals ship it; essential for readability on translucent glass.
