API itself is blocking. | none | must | design | Apotheosis uses calloop, not tokio; take the default and avoid a second runtime. Source: adapters/unix/Cargo.toml; adapters/unix/README.md; adapters/unix/src/executor.rs. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-24 | accesskit_unix licence tree is clean | About 60 transitive crates checked: zbus family MIT, async-* and event-listener Apache-2.0 OR MIT, rustix tri-licence, memchr Unlicense OR MIT, atspi dual MIT/Apache, accesskit dual MIT/Apache. | none | must | crates.io | All choices are in the allowed set; memchr and similar rely on selecting the MIT arm in cargo-deny config. Source: crates.io API per crate; adapters/unix/Cargo.toml. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-25 | Lazy AT-SPI activation via org.a11y.Status IsEnabled | The adapter thread subscribes to IsEnabled on the session bus (current value first); when true it opens the AT-SPI bus, registers the root, and activates adapters; when false it deactivates them. | KDL accessibility auto, on or off | must | accesskit | Zero tree-building cost until an AT appears. Known wart: IsEnabled is sticky after first Orca run, so it is a cost control, not a detector. Source: adapters/unix/src/context.rs#run_event_loop; zbus-5.19.0 src/proxy/mod.rs; AccessKit issues #314 and #713. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-26 | Activation handler returns None, then update_if_active supplies the tree | Handlers run on the adapter thread. Returning None leaves the adapter Pending until the app calls update_if_active with a full tree; the closure runs only when active. | none | must | design | The grid is owned by the main thread; the AT thread must only wake it. Avoids locks and avoids building trees when inactive. Source: adapters/unix/src/adapter.rs#Adapter::new, update_if_active; context.rs#activate_adapter. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-27 | Terminal node with one TextRun per visible row | Role::Terminal is documented as VT-100-style multi-line document; each row is a TextRun with value, character_lengths (u8 bytes per character), word_starts, character_positions and character_widths. | none | must | accesskit | This is the supported way to express a terminal grid and satisfies ACCESSIBILITY-06/07 shape; supports_text_ranges is true for Terminal with text runs. Source: accesskit/src/lib.rs (Role::Terminal, Role::TextRun, character_* properties); accesskit_consumer/src/text.rs#supports_text_ranges. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-28 | Per-character extents and offset-at-point via text run geometry | character_extents resolves the offset to a text position and returns its first bounding box in window or screen coordinates; offset_at_point inverts the node transform and calls text_position_at_point. | none | must | design | Covers ACCESSIBILITY-06. Needs character_positions and widths per cell, with width 2 for wide characters. Source: adapters/atspi-common/src/node.rs#character_extents, offset_at_point. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-29 | AT-SPI offsets are Unicode scalar indices over extracted text | Global offsets are USV indices into the concatenated run text, with grapheme clusters grouped by character_lengths; no cell indices or blank padding unless the app provides them. | none | must | accesskit | Matches ACCESSIBILITY-07. Rows must trim trailing blanks and mark hard line ends with a newline in the final run of a logical line. Source: adapters/atspi-common/src/node.rs (to_global_usv_index use); accesskit/src/lib.rs (character_lengths docs). (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-30 | character_lengths is a byte-length list capped at 255 per character | Each character's UTF-8 length is stored in one u8, so a single grapheme cluster longer than 255 bytes cannot be represented. | none | should | accesskit | Zalgo-style or long ZWJ clusters in a cell need truncation or splitting to stay valid; handle explicitly. Source: accesskit/src/lib.rs (character_lengths). (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-31 | Text change events from full-document prefix and suffix diff | On each update the adapter builds old and new full text for the container, trims common prefix and suffix, and emits one TextRemoved and one TextInserted for the middle. | none | must | design | Cost is linear in container size per update and one scroll becomes a whole-screen insert, so Orca may speak a screen. Coalesce, cap rows, and control what is exposed. Source: adapters/atspi-common/src/adapter.rs#emit_text_change_if_needed_parent. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-32 | Caret-moved and text-selection-changed events | Selection anchor and focus on the container map to CaretMoved and TextSelectionChanged events when focused. | none | must | design | Needed for caret reading and review cursor; terminal cursor must be set as a degenerate selection on the Terminal node. Source: adapters/atspi-common/src/adapter.rs#emit_text_selection_change. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-33 | Announcement events for bell and notifications | ObjectEvent::Announcement(text, politeness) is emitted over AT-SPI as a live announcement. | action announce | should | design | Right channel for bell, command-finished and rule-driven announcements without altering the grid text; expose as an Action. Source: adapters/atspi-common/src/events.rs. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-34 | Screen coordinates need a compositor-supplied window position on Wayland | set_root_window_bounds is documented as only meaningful on X11 because Wayland clients cannot read their position; window-relative coordinates remain valid. | none | should | design | Magnifier and review cursor accuracy needs the position. Abyss can supply it through its integration; other compositors get window coordinates only. Source: adapters/unix/src/adapter.rs#Adapter::set_root_window_bounds. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-35 | Orca test matrix as a deliverable | AccessKit documents no tested Orca versions; public issues show Orca 48 with atspi_common 0.19.1 and Orca 49.5 and 51.alpha on GNOME 49 Wayland. No public terminal-on-AccessKit report found. | none | must | accesskit | Both accessibility rows are 'must' on an unproven path. Plan Orca 48, 49 and current, on GNOME Wayland and Abyss, with a fixed script, and budget upstream fixes. Source: https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/issues/713; https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/issues/854. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-36 | Separate tree builder from AT-SPI transport | Build AccessKit TreeUpdates in an Apotheosis module that knows nothing about accesskit_unix; the unix adapter is a thin sink. | none | should | design | GNOME's Newton project is a push-based successor with AccessKit as reference client; a clean seam eases the move. Source: https://blogs.gnome.org/a11y/. (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |
| ACCESSIBILITY-37 | Text attribute and sentence-boundary gaps | Reported gaps: font, size, style and colour not exposed over AT-SPI by AccessKit, and sentence boundaries unsupported so Orca Say All falls back to lines. Status for 0.24 unverified. | none | could | design | Do not promise colour or bold accessibility until verified; line-based reading is acceptable for terminals. Source: https://blogs.gnome.org/a11y/; adapters/unix/CHANGELOG.md (text attributes entry). (gap: gap-kbvm-accesskit-parity-checks) |

### Open questions

- AccessKit's unix adapter maturity: does it support the full AT-SPI text interface (offsets, granularity, attributes, extents, hyperlinks) needed for 01, 05-08, 14, or must we extend it or contribute upstream? Verify before committing.
- Does AccessKit expose a text-run model that can accept our snapshot/diff events (02) without rebuilding whole subtrees, and how does it handle per-pane update rate?
- Threading: AccessKit/zbus run on their own executor; confirm snapshot hand-off from the render thread is lock-free enough for the hot-path rule.
- Screen-reader testing matrix (Orca, plus magnifier on Abyss) is undefined; Orca behaviour with terminal role text on Wayland is unverified.
- Does Abyss need an accessibility-relevant protocol for AT focus/caret reporting (magnifier tracking), or does AT-SPI suffice on Wayland?
- ACCESSIBILITY-13 default verbosity values are our design, not prior art; need user testing.
- Rendering minimum-contrast (17) against a blurred translucent background: which effective bg sample to use (theme bg, measured blur luminance)? Unresolved.
- Whether unicode-input (19) should ship given text-input-v3 and compose-key coverage.


## remote

| ID | Feature | Mechanism we adopt | Control surface | Adopt | Seen in | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REMOTE-01 | Selector expression language and window addressing | One boolean selector grammar (field:regex terms joined by and/or/not) over window and tab fields (id, title, pid, cwd, cmdline, env, var, state, neighbor, session, recent); negative ids count from newest; plus simple name/id/last/0 addressing for windows. Same parser used everywhere a target is needed. | `--match`, `--window`, socket `match` field, rule conditions, palette | must | kitty, windows-terminal | Reusable selector for CLI, socket, rules and palette. kitty's grammar is the richer base; wt's name/id/last forms become sugar over it. Sources: https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/remote-control/ ; wt Process Model 2.0 spec. |
| REMOTE-02 | Control socket gating by capability tiers | Unix socket mode 0600, peer credentials (SO_PEERCRED) identify the client. Per-client capability tiers (read, send-text, spawn, config, system) plus command-level allow-lists (globs) and per-pane child grants; prompt on first use of an ungranted tier via a glass dialog. No shared passwords. | `control.allow` in KDL, settings GUI, socket handshake | must | kitty | Adopts kitty's command-glob allow-lists and prompt-on-first-use, but replaces passwords with peer credentials and capabilities (matches EclipseOS no-ambient-authority). Remote-over-ssh use is by forwarding the socket, not by encryption options. |
| REMOTE-03 | Control command set coverage | Every command is a JSON schema with args and a match target. Coverage list: ls (window/tab/pane tree with cwd, pid, cmdline, env), get-text, send-text, send-key, launch, set-colors, screenshot, action (run any Action). | `apo msg <cmd>` CLI, JSON socket methods | must | kitty | kitty's remote-control list is the reference coverage; `action` ties it to the shared Action vocabulary so nothing needs a scripting language. https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/remote-control/ |
| REMOTE-04 | Control event stream with flow control and subscriptions | Line-framed JSON replies and async notifications on the socket. Per-pane output subscription, format/state subscriptions, pause-after-age with low/high watermarks so a slow client cannot stall the pane. Model: tmux control mode (%begin/%end/%error blocks, %output, pause-after, refresh-client -A/-B; watermarks 512/8192 bytes). | socket `subscribe`/`pause`/`resume`, CLI `apo events` | should | tmux-zellij | Enables third-party GUIs and tools (iTerm2 uses tmux -C this way). Source: tmux control.c (control_check_age, control_write_output, CONTROL_BUFFER_LOW/HIGH), cmd-refresh-client.c. |
| REMOTE-05 | Versioned IPC with capability announcements | Every IPC (control socket, client spawn, any mux/frame stream) opens with a protocol version and a capability set (graphics, sixel, compression, structured frames); older oldest-supported version is declared. Never assume peer capabilities. | internal; version in `apo --version` and socket hello | must | tmux-zellij | Mixed client/server versions across a persistent server are guaranteed. Source: tmux tmux-protocol.h (PROTOCOL_VERSION, MSG_VERSION), client.c identify messages; zellij client_to_server.proto. |
| REMOTE-06 | Client spawn protocol with exit-code passthrough | Thin client talks to the server over a unix socket with one packed message (flags, cwd, activation token, config overrides, argv, env); server replies with the child's exit status; SIGUSR1/2 forwarded as IPC frames. Keep -N (no detach), -E env, -o override, -D cwd, -s, --hold semantics so it works as a drop-in terminal in scripts. | `apo` / client CLI flags | must | foot | Users script footclient as a terminal replacement; exit code and flag semantics must survive. Source: foot client-protocol.h, client.c, server.c. |
| REMOTE-07 | Attach to an existing pty | Run the UI against a caller-supplied pty fd instead of spawning a child (VM and serial consoles). Cheap if the pty layer is an abstraction. | `--pty <path>` | could | foot | Niche but real foot capability. Source: foot doc/foot.1.scd. |
| REMOTE-08 | System ssh as transport with private ControlMaster | Spawn the system ssh binary (no C library in our build) with ControlMaster=auto, a pid-keyed ControlPath using %C, ControlPersist=yes, ServerAliveInterval=60, ServerAliveCountMax=5, TCPKeepAlive=no; tear down on exit. Connection sharing makes new tabs fast. | connection KDL (`connection "host"`), palette: connect | must | kitty | Satisfies the no-C rule and inherits ssh_config, agents, ProxyJump, FIDO keys. Source: kitty kittens/ssh/main.go#connection_sharing_args. |
| REMOTE-09 | Short control-socket path workaround | If the runtime dir path exceeds about 35 chars, symlink a short per-uid dir under /tmp so ssh's ControlPath (hash plus temp suffix) stays under ~104 chars. | internal | should | kitty | Avoids obscure ControlPath failures on long XDG_RUNTIME_DIR. Source: kitty kittens/ssh/main.go#connection_sharing_args. |
| REMOTE-10 | Stale ControlMaster probe | Ask the master to forward to a closed port: costs a server round trip, runs nothing remote, 5 s timeout; on failure create a new master. | internal | should | kitty | Detects dead shared connections without hanging a new tab. Source: kitty kittens/ssh/main.go#master_responds. |
| REMOTE-11 | Native askpass via SSH_ASKPASS | Set SSH_ASKPASS (and force-use) to a helper that routes password, passphrase and host-key confirmation prompts to a glass dialog (with fingerprint display) instead of the tty. | connection KDL `askpass=glass\|tty` | must | kitty | Avoids tty interference and gives a proper host-key UI. Source: kitty kittens/ssh/main.py (askpass option), askpass.go. |
| REMOTE-12 | Terminfo and shell-integration shipping on ssh | Optional wrapper copies terminfo and integration scripts to the remote and reuses the master connection; per-host config. | connection KDL `integration=...` | could | kitty | Terminfo is better solved by a widely installable terminfo; revisit later. https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/shell-integration/ (partially read, unverified detail). |
| REMOTE-13 | Remote helper install with consent | On connect, offer to install a small helper under the user's home, add it to PATH for that session only, inject env vars; fall back with an indicator on failure. Always ask first by default. | connection KDL `helper=ask\|auto\|never` | should | warp-wave | Enables remote blocks/marks and files without permanent changes. Source: https://docs.waveterm.dev/connections. kitty's download-on-demand variant is rejected (REMOTE-27). |
| REMOTE-14 | Mux over ssh stdio / proxy_command | Remote persistent sessions: ssh (REMOTE-08) runs a remote proxy command that bridges the mux PDU stream on stdin/stdout; no extra listener, no new crypto. A generic `proxy_command` hook (nc, socat, ssh, wsl, container exec) replaces direct socket connect for any domain. Version negotiation per REMOTE-05. | domain KDL `proxy-command`, `remote-path` | should | wezterm | One hook covers WSL, jump hosts and containers. Depends on a compatible remote binary so version negotiation matters. Source: wezterm docs/multiplexing.md; config/src/ssh.rs (override_proxy_command, remote_wezterm_path). |
| REMOTE-15 | ssh_config host import for connections | Parse ~/.ssh/config (and /etc/ssh/ssh_config) to auto-create plain and multiplexed domains per Host; hide wildcard hosts but apply them; document no Match support and agent detection order; rows overridable in KDL and listed in the palette. | palette: connect, `connections { import-ssh-config }` | should | wezterm, warp-wave | Two terminals ship it. Zero-config remote entry. Source: wezterm config/src/ssh.rs#SshDomain::default_domains; https://docs.waveterm.dev/connections. |
| REMOTE-16 | Predictive local echo on high RTT | Measure input round trip; above a threshold paint predicted echo locally and reconcile on real output. Must be disabled on password-like prompts (use terminal echo-mode/no-echo detection, not a substring match), alt-screen and full-screen apps. | `local-echo-threshold-ms` | could | wezterm | Only wezterm ships it; the password guard must be stronger than wezterm's. Rows disagreed should/could; chosen could until the guard is designed. Source: wezterm wezterm-client/src/pane/renderable.rs#should_predict, apply_prediction; docs/multiplexing.md. |
| REMOTE-17 | Versioned binary terminal snapshot | Record-based format with envelope and typed records (terminal, screen, page, style, hyperlink, history, kitty graphics, checkpoint, continuation); validate on encode, degrade gracefully on decode; machine-readable spec plus verifier. | Actions `session.save`/`restore`, internal | should | ghostty | Backs session restore, detach/reattach and process hand-off. | REMOTE-20 | Session web and mobile client | Built-in web client over the same session (TLS off-localhost, login tokens, websocket heartbeat, mobile keyboard UI). | `web-server` KDL block | could | tmux-zellij | Attractive for the always-on dev box but a large security surface; defer behind REMOTE-02 and the remote story. Source: zellij mobile_web.rs, zellij-client/src/web_client/. |
| REMOTE-21 | Remote-to-local file access | Remote shell reads local files via a socket file proxy URI; process substitution runs local scripts remotely. | consent prompt + capability `files` | could | warp-wave | Useful but a security surface; gate behind consent. https://docs.waveterm.dev/wsh |

Off by default. | connection KDL `copy {}` | could | kitty | Optional dotfile sync. Source: kitty kittens/ssh/main.py (copy_message), main.go#make_tarfile. |
| REMOTE-23 | Delegate host to plain ssh | Per-host field runs plain ssh (or another command) for hosts the bootstrap cannot handle. | connection KDL `delegate` | could | kitty | Escape hatch for odd shells and appliances. Source: kitty kittens/ssh/main.py (delegate). |

Source: mpv DOCS/man/vo.rst; kitty graphics protocol. |

## Open questions

- Predictive local echo (REMOTE-16): should it ship in v1, and what reliable password-prompt guard exists (termios echo state is not visible over ssh)?
- Mux remote binary (REMOTE-14): does Apotheosis ship a headless server binary distinct from the GUI, and how is it installed remotely without the download path rejected in REMOTE-27?
- Terminfo (REMOTE-12): which terminfo name and distribution plan removes the need for ssh-time shipping?
- Frame protocol (REMOTE-18): is a separate server/client split in scope for v1, or does it start as an internal boundary only?
- Mode 2048 path (REMOTE-24): contour vt-extensions location was unverified.
- Capability tier boundaries (REMOTE-02): exact commands per tier, and how forwarded sockets over ssh are authorised.

## Open questions

- Should the nightly/dev channel be a separate `-git` PKGBUILD or a repo-side pre-release channel? Rows give no evidence.
- Terminfo naming: one entry vs. a separate direct-colour entry, and whether to also claim compatibility with `TERM=foot*` consumers (unverified how much software keys on it).
- Should the daemon be the default mode (like foot server) or optional? Rows only cover socket activation mechanics.
- Whether the version query should be an Action in the shared vocabulary or only a CLI/socket method.
